# Power System: The Earthside License and Veyran Weave

## 1. The two interacting systems

Veyra already has magic before Adrian arrives. Its practitioners use **Weave**, a consent-sensitive discipline that changes patterns in the world. The **Earthside License** is a separate interface laid over that world by a fragment of the ancient **First Grammar**. It does not replace Weave. It translates, limits, and records a crossing so that a person from Earth can survive contact with it.

The distinction matters throughout the story:

- **Weave** is local, embodied, relational, and shaped by history.
- **The License** is cross-world, mechanical in its effects, limited by physical anchors, and capable of being edited.
- A player can receive a template from the License and still need years of local practice to use it.
- A Veyran can be a powerful Weaver without ever receiving a template or seeing a System panel.

## 2. What the License can do

The License is a living permission record. It can do the following, and nothing beyond this without a new established rule:

1. **Mark a traveler:** identify a person’s chosen name, body, origin, and return mark so the crossing can recognize them.
2. **Translate a declared act:** turn a direct promise into a Veyran working, such as opening a threshold, identifying a route, or repairing a damaged join.
3. **Show local permission:** display whether a place, person, or object accepts, refuses, or cannot safely receive a working.
4. **Offer a workway:** provide a class-like template that narrows a traveler’s possible magic and states its obligations.
5. **Limit passage:** set a duration, destination, number of people, and condition for a threshold.
6. **Preserve a return mark:** allow a living traveler to seek the return crossing if the mark and the receiving community still consent.

The License does not create a body, resurrect a dead person, grant combat skill, store unlimited items, read thoughts, compel obedience, erase memory on command, or make a person invincible. It cannot safely make a destination that has no local witness.

## 3. What the interface looks like

The License appears as a pale line of text or a small rectangular field when Adrian looks at a mark he understands, speaks a direct promise, or enters a dangerous threshold. It usually gives one prompt, one consequence, and one choice. It never prints a large skill table.

Typical prompts use the language of obligations rather than game mechanics:

> A door needs two willing names: one to leave, one to receive. If you close it from the far side, the person you leave behind keeps the scar.

> This working can change the road, not the history of the road. Choose a new route or accept the old loss.

> You may give one person a workway. The person must be told the cost before accepting.

A panel may be helpful, incomplete, or deliberately misleading. Ivenn’s altered prompts tend to omit a social cost while displaying a clear reward. Adrian and Tamsin learn to read every offer for three things: who is named, who pays, and who can refuse.

The System uses familiar Earthside words because Adrian thinks in those words. Veyrans may call the same thing a **bond**, **crossing**, or **working**, and they do not see a floating panel. The same permission can therefore look like a quest to Adrian and like a dangerous public contract to Tamsin.

## 4. Threshold rules

A **threshold** is a temporary crossing between Earth and Veyra. It is a physical event with social infrastructure, not a personal teleport.

### The three words that must not be blurred

- **Fragment:** a piece of the Grammar’s permission language that has separated from a resonant site. A fragment can take a name, leave a mark on a palm, and pull an arrival toward a damaged site. It has no local anchor, no witness, and no receiving community, so it cannot open a controlled threshold and cannot be aimed, scheduled, or repeated. A fragment is what the first breach is built out of.
- **Recognized License:** a fragment that has been installed in a resonant site, witnessed by at least one willing Veyran, and **fully** accepted by a receiving community. A recognized License is a standing permission with a named holder, a cost, and a review. Only this can hold a route open on purpose. If the receiving community accepts only part of the receiving area, or the local authority has not ruled, the recognition is **incomplete**: the terms can be entered, posted and inspected, and the instrument still cannot be used. Volume 1’s Chapter 10 route is in that state until the trial license in Chapters 48–50.
- **License holder:** a person recognized *by a recognized License* as its named holder. Accepting a fragment does not make someone a holder. Adrian is a **fragment-bearer** from Chapter 1 until a local quorum gives him standing in Volume 1; the narrator must not call him a holder before that.

The distinction is the moral engine of the opening. Adrian does something real and knowing in Chapter 1 — he accepts a fragment and puts his legal name into it. That makes him responsible for his own arrival. It does not make the crossing legal.

### Entry conditions

Every **controlled** threshold requires:

- A resonant place: a lock, gate, boundary shrine, old road, or place where a public law is regularly repeated
- A named Earthside holder
- At least one willing Veyran witness
- A receiving community that understands the purpose
- A material cost such as opened salt, repaired stone, food, fuel, medicine, or a shared labor shift

A **breach** is the exception created by a damaged Anchorstone, not a loophole Adrian can use. What a breach lacks is not a human intention — it is the local infrastructure. There is no resonant site under anyone’s control, no willing Veyran witness, and no receiving agreement, so a fragment in the breach can only grab at whoever is nearest. A breach can pull an arrival because the rule has failed. It cannot be opened on purpose, it always leaves severe residue, and the first task is to turn it into a controlled threshold or close it.

### Passage limits and capacity

Capacity is always counted per opening, never per crowd. The table below is the whole capacity system; any number in the prose must match one of these rows.

| Kind of opening | Who can pass in one opening | How often it can be opened | What raises the number |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Breach** (damaged site) | Nobody chooses. It grabs at most the few people nearest the opening, and cannot be aimed. | Once. A breach either tears wider or is closed. | Nothing. This is why it is a catastrophe. |
| **Single-holder threshold** (Stage 2 onward) | One named living traveler, plus only what that traveler can carry. | Only after a new anchor or a new local agreement; the same route cannot be opened twice in a row by one holder. | A new anchor and new witnesses, which is how Volume 2 improves a route at all. |
| **Quorum route** (the Volume 1 trial) | One named living traveler per opening, carrying a return mark. | Sequentially, one opening at a time, and only after a fresh inspection of the seam by the witnessing group. | Witness count buys durability and legitimacy, never throughput. Ten witnesses still move one person. |
| **Inspected civic threshold** (later volumes) | A small queue, set by local inspection and posted publicly, counted as people per opening. | A posted schedule tied to tide, labor shift, and inspection. | Construction, political agreement, and an agreed schedule — the only route that ever moves more than one person at a time. |
| **Regional network** (Stage 3 onward) | A whole network moves a whole volume’s worth of people **across many separate openings**. A single opening still moves a single opening’s worth. | Whatever the weakest anchor can sustain, which the story shows as a failing network rather than a bigger door. | Independent anchors added by other communities, never by Adrian alone. |

The first crossing carries Adrian alone. The first failed expansion carries six more because the breach is grabbing, not transporting. No opening in the series ever carries a crowd at once; when the story needs dozens of people moving, it shows dozens of inspected openings, a queue, a ledger, and a labor schedule.

A passage is limited by the weakest of its anchors, not by Adrian’s desire.

The following are hard limits:

- A dead traveler cannot return.
- A missing or broken return mark can be repaired only through a new witnessed act, not automatically.
- A threshold can be closed from either side, and a person on the wrong side may be stranded.
- Travel through a closed or corrupted route causes body injury and local residue; it is not a clean reset.
- Earthside time and Veyran time are not identical. Most early crossings differ by hours, but later events can move the return date by days or weeks.
- The License can carry living people, ordinary cargo, and carefully limited records. It cannot safely carry an unlimited army, a contagious substance, or a forced prisoner.

### Reverberation

Every opening causes **reverberation** in the local world. The cost is concrete and local:

- water shifts and a nearby lock loses pressure
- crops germinate at the wrong time
- bells, songs, or memories repeat out of order
- a road is briefly visible where no road exists
- animals flee or become aggressive
- a person near the site forgets a short but important interval

A passage can be physically successful and socially disastrous. A village may vote to close a bridge because the bridge keeps working but the surrounding fields no longer produce stable harvests.

## 5. Return marks and failure

A return mark is a physical and social anchor, not a button labeled “return.” It may be worn as a mark, carried as an iron token, or established through a place-bound ritual. The mark contains the traveler’s chosen identity, not their complete history. Each mark belongs to one named living person; a route never lends its mark to a group. A person without a mark cannot cross through a controlled threshold unless the receiving side first establishes a temporary mark through a public working.

A quorum route carries one named living traveler per opening, and the number of witnesses standing behind it never changes that. Passages are sequential, never simultaneous, and each opening requires the witnesses to confirm the traveler’s name, destination, medical condition, and right to refuse. The route can be used again only after a new inspection and local agreement. This limit prevents a single mark from becoming a shared ticket.

If the receiving side refuses future passage, the mark becomes difficult to use. If the route is broken while open, the named traveler is the person most likely to be stranded; the other travelers are not automatically dragged through. A short written message can be carried through an anchored token, but it consumes the same one-person capacity and is not a safe substitute for a return.

### Failure modes

- **Wrong witness:** If a working is performed without a genuinely willing witness, the residue rebounds. A forced bridge can fold travelers into the nearest remembered route, produce a body at the wrong age, or leave a person unable to remember a destination.
- **False promise:** If a person signs a permission they do not understand, the mark develops a black line. Repeated abuse can create a **black scar** on a route or a person.
- **Overload:** If too many permissions share one anchor, the route tears. The local place may lose a room, a boundary, a family relationship, or a named season.
- **Unclosed working:** A person who accepts a duty and disappears transfers part of the cost to the people who remain. The world experiences this as broken roads, strange inheritance, or a public oath that points at the wrong heir.
- **Death:** A dead traveler’s permission does not transfer automatically. If the return mark is still active, it may attract attention from people who want to use it; if it is broken, the route becomes a black scar until repaired by witnesses.
- **False System prompt:** A corrupted prompt can lead a player into a working that appears beneficial and is socially destructive. The person still chooses; responsibility does not vanish because the wording was false.

The story should show the aftermath of failure, not merely announce a penalty. A failed crossing may require a funeral, a repaired lock, a changed harvest, a public apology, or a community vote.

## 6. Workways and class templates

The License calls its workways **templates** because Adrian’s mental model needs that word. Veyrans call them **workways**. A template is a permission profile, not a complete skill tree.

| Earthside display | Veyran meaning | Benefit | Limit and cost |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Wayfinder** | roadright / path reader | Sense joins, read a route’s recent choices, find a safe passage through difficult terrain | Must personally walk or bear witness to a route; cannot teleport; repeated route work taxes memory and balance |
| **Hearthkeeper** | shelter-weaver | Share basic recovery, stabilize water and shelter, recognize household needs | Cannot impose treatment; must accept the patient’s consent; leaves a residue of the patient’s strongest fear or memory |
| **Artificer** | material speaker | Repair tools, work metal, build anchors, make compatible parts | Materials are not conjured; each work records a debt to material, maker, and user; a failed object can injure its maker |
| **Listener** | oathreader | Hear changes in names, contracts, and spoken intent | Cannot know unexpressed thoughts; prolonged use makes ordinary lies difficult to distinguish from truth; social isolation is common |
| **Courier** | threshold runner | Move quickly between marked crossings and carry urgent messages | Cannot conceal a living passenger or undeclared magical cargo; exhaustion and exposure accumulate |
| **Warden** | groundkeeper | Reinforce a claimed place, organize defense, resist involuntary displacement | Must defend the place and its residents; abandoning a claimed ground causes a physical and social backlash |
| **Scribe** | witness-copyist | Translate local law, preserve a disputed record, make consent legible | Cannot sign on another person’s behalf; forged or incomplete copies create dangerous absences |
| **Unbound** | no named workway | Move between specialties and avoid one template’s fixed obligation | Has no special protection and must learn each practice from local teachers; the most dangerous route for a desperate traveler |

Templates are learned through practice, failure, and relationships. A player cannot collect every template and use them simultaneously. Choosing a workway is choosing a social role that other people can reasonably expect the holder to perform.

## 7. Adrian’s power stages

The stages below are narrative milestones, not levels displayed as numbers. Each stage adds options and responsibility.

### Stage 0: Unlicensed body

Adrian can see an occasional pale prompt and perceives harsh light in a join when he is injured or frightened. He has no stable template and no reliable return. He can survive by observation, tools, negotiation, and the first people who help him.

**Cost:** ordinary injury, exhaustion, language loss, and immediate social suspicion. A wrong choice can kill him without a dramatic warning.

### Stage 1: Witness

Adrian can perceive the nearest join-line, understand one direct Veyran promise at a time, and mark a safe route for himself. He does not create a working; he can only make an existing pattern visible enough for others to act on.

**Earned through:** helping the Lock of Salt during the first flood, accepting a public debt to a place he cannot clear by himself, and failing in front of witnesses to close the outer tear alone. All three must happen. Until the debt and the failure, he has a **provisional join-reading** — he can follow one join-line for as long as somebody else is holding the working, and it leaves a headache and a nosebleed. A provisional reading is not a rank, carries no duty, and cannot be refused by a local witness because it was never granted to him by one.

**Cost:** headaches, sensory bleed, and a responsibility to the place he has witnessed. A black scar can attach to him after a failed working.

### Stage 2: Passage holder

With two willing local witnesses and a named destination, Adrian can open a single-holder threshold: one named living traveler per opening, carrying only what that traveler can hold. He can carry one return mark and one basic workway template. This stage is **not reached at the end of Volume 01**; the first trial route is operated collectively, and Adrian remains a Witness until later practice, local trust, and a failed return earn him the Passage permission.

**Earned through:** a later controlled rescue, Tamsin’s and Mina’s trust, and the sacrifice of an easy return route.

**Cost:** each opening causes reverberation and physical strain. A failed passage can injure another person. Adrian cannot open the same route twice without a new anchor or a new local agreement.

### Stage 3: Coven steward

Adrian can connect several independent witness marks into a small network, translate consent for a shared work, and help a willing player choose a template. He can repair a damaged threshold with a team, but cannot command the team.

**Earned through:** the first settlements, the White Ledger conflict, and a public failure that forces him to share authority with Tamsin, Mina, Dante, and local leaders.

**Cost:** Adrian becomes responsible for the network’s weakest link. A remote failure can send a shock, a false quest, or a black scar back to him. He must disclose the network’s limits.

### Stage 4: Rule speaker

Adrian can translate between Earthside and Veyran legal language, read a Crown Law’s history, and propose a public correction to a disputed record. He can open a regional network of thresholds only when multiple local witnesses agree.

**Earned through:** the Aurel hearings, the Glass Steppe expedition, the Unwritten Coast campaign, and the collapse of a region he had tried to govern centrally.

**Cost:** conflicting records and memories enter him. Every major correction can cost a personal memory, a relationship, or a physical scar. The more authoritative he becomes, the more people use his name as a weapon.

### Stage 5: Bridgewright

Adrian can work with independent bridges, protect a distributed network from a hostile permission, and call a shared emergency crossing. He cannot create a bridge from nothing, compel a community, or defeat an army by opening a gate.

**Earned through:** the First Room expedition, the Bridge War, and his decision to obey local evacuation plans rather than impose his own.

**Cost:** he loses his personal return mark and suffers identity bleed. He becomes physically vulnerable when the network fails. His power is relational: the bridge is only as strong as the people willing to keep it open.

This is the **one and only time** Adrian gives up a personal return mark. From this volume onward he has no mark of his own: every time he crosses, in either direction, other witnesses must agree to carry him. Later sacrifices are not repetitions of this one; see Stages 6 and 7.

### Stage 6: First Anchor

Adrian can expose the First Grammar’s single-authority patch and help distribute its permissions among independent seals. He can stop a forced version lock for a time, but cannot replace the device with a new central control system.

**Earned through:** refusing to reset the First Room, losing the Ivenn contest, and accepting that the old system must be dismantled rather than mastered.

**Cost:** permanent loss of solo access, a significant personal memory, a lasting bodily injury, and the knowledge that the bridge may fail without him. He cannot return to Earth alone — and since Stage 5 he could not return alone in any case, because he no longer holds a personal return mark. What he surrenders here is the **recognized seat of first holder**: the standing recognition that lets one name answer for the Grammar’s permissions.

### Stage 7: Concord keeper

There is no higher personal stage. Adrian can act only through a quorum of independent witnesses. His final authority is the ability to facilitate a shared decision, not to overrule it.

**Earned through:** public confession, surrendered admin access, and the final defense built from dozens of local and Earthside choices.

**Cost:** he gives up the title “first licensed player” as a power and accepts ordinary legal status on both sides. The bridge remains open because other people accept duties, not because he remains essential.

## 8. Progression discipline

Growth must be shown through specific scenes:

- Adrian trains his body and pays a practitioner for correction.
- He practices a workway with a local teacher and is refused a shortcut.
- He fails to save someone because he did not ask the right witness.
- He loses a route, a memory, a legal right, or a comfortable relationship.
- He changes the design of a network after listening to a person he previously treated as a variable.
- He gives a power or permission to someone else and loses convenience.

No chapter should solve a major problem with a newly unlocked ability that has not appeared in practice. If Adrian solves a problem early, the solution must depend on observation, relationships, materials, or an established cost.

## 9. System-message rules for prose

- Use no more than one short System panel in an ordinary chapter.
- The panel must state an immediate rule, warning, or choice in plain language.
- A panel never explains the entire world or announces a reward for good behavior.
- Characters may ignore, misunderstand, or argue with a panel.
- The narrator may render a prompt as a pale field, a feeling, or a line of text, but it remains a bounded interface.
- The System may be wrong, particularly when Ivenn’s patch is active. Adrian must verify important permissions with a human witness and physical evidence.

## 10. Rules established in Volume 01 Chapters 11–20

These are additions to the sections above, not replacements. They are on the page and binding from Chapter 11 onward.

1. **A workway displaces a duty, and the displaced duty belongs to somebody who is not in the room.** Marrow states the cost in Chapter 16: a wayfinder must personally walk or bear witness to a route; repeated route work taxes memory and balance and takes the road's name into the place where you keep your name; and the limitation is an **erosion**, not a punishment — nobody comes and tells you about it. Critically, the obligation and the temptation are the same object: a workway's holder cannot refuse to carry a road, which is why a wayfinder is the obvious person to be asked to move a boundary stone, and why the Chapter 7 offer was made to Adrian and not to the anchorwright. **Noor Haddad's question — *who does the job?* — is the standing local test of any proposed workway**, and it means an offer must always be costed against the labour it displaces before it is costed against its holder.

2. **A permission has no state.** The Chapter 7 and Chapter 16 offers both end *it is not yet accepted*, ten days and two refusals apart. There is no record of a refusal anywhere in it. Anything built on the assumption that a permission is a thing that can be accepted, refused, and then recalled is built on a thing that does not exist. Adrian writes this down in Chapter 16 and does not know what it means.

3. **The interface's costs are placed, not stated.** The dangerous construction in this volume is not an outright lie. It is a true-sounding sentence in the grammatical position where a cost would go, arranged so that a man stops looking for one: *the cost has been paid, there is nothing further to pay* (Chapter 10), *the cost of the stone is not part of the workway* (Chapter 16), *a holder who does not come may be replaced at the next sitting* (Chapter 11, which is also stronger than what the ledger says). Read every offer for three things — who is named, who pays, and who can refuse — and check each answer against a **written local record**, never against the field.

4. **A holder may be shown a thing and thereby be made its only record.** The Chapter 15 offer — *a holder may be shown which one the water is using* — is true, useful, and refused, because it would leave the answer in one man with no page, no second pair of eyes, and no argument available afterward. A singleton is a failure mode, not a shortcut. The rule generalises: **any permission whose whole content is knowledge, rather than a working, is a permission to stop being a witness.**

5. **The interface's offers stop.** Adrian stops looking for the field on the **Friday night of day 18 of the flood, in Chapter 19**, and the scene that stops him is a man saying the word *quest* out loud for a thing in a wall. He tells the room not to use the word and writes the date down. The interface has said nothing in Chapters 13, 14, 17, 18, 19 or 20 — including at the moment ninety people ask in public what the Narrow Mark is for. Its silences are information and a writer should use them: the panel is loudest when it wants something, it is absent when a public act is being performed, and, so far, it has never once come out of the main breach. Two cautions for later batches. First, the moment a character stops expecting the interface **must be dramatised, not recalled in a notebook** — a retrospective entry is not the same beat and the state files will be believed. Second, a promise may only be made once: the word *quest* has now been spent, having moved from Adrian's private vocabulary in Chapter 5, to Jonah Pike's mouth in Chapter 19, to a refusal on Adrian's own page in Chapter 20.

6. **A local record is only as good as the people who can read it, and the people who can read it are the people who can act on it.** This is why the Chapter 12 leak was not a leak, and why the only durable line on that market board is a door rather than a reassurance. Where a record has to travel, ask who carries it. Peth Lammay's question in Chapter 13 is the standing test: does the community the document is *about* know the document exists, and in what hand.

7. **A written promise about a public place is stronger than a spoken one, but only if it is a promise.** A cost that can be paid once is a fee. A cost that is a labour, paid daily, by whoever is nearest, with nobody's name at the top, is a promise — and the keeper of a room can tell the difference on sight. This is why Nyla Ferrow's price for opening the ground is a rota and not a sum, and why Vail will strike out any name put at the top of it.

## 11. Rules established in Volume 01 Chapters 21–30

These are additions to the sections above, not replacements, and they are on the page and binding from Chapter 21 onward.

1. **A successful working's residue can reach a site the working could not touch, and the site need not be damaged to be opened.** Chapter 25. The working was performed at the Lock of Salt, a resonant site, with consent, at a cost, by locals, at a known tide, and it did exactly what it was set to do: the pale edge of the main breach stepped nine inches toward the near gate, in the direction of the stone that was not holding anything. Its residue — water, pressure, attention — travelled down under a bank and arrived at an older damaged threshold beneath a chapel floor on the same river, and opened it. **The distance between a working and its residue is not bounded by anything the performers can see.** Any later working whose cost is entered as a season, a salt measure, or a day's labour has to be costed for where the residue lands, and a cost column that names only the field is incomplete.

2. **A breach is defined by missing infrastructure, and the flagstone rule is the local test.** On the twenty-fifth day, nineteen persons were carried out of the hole in the yard and **no person was carried out of the hole from the Lock of Salt.** A hole that opens in a building is a hole in a building. A hole that opens in the load-bearing gate of a tidal lock, in a course of stone that the city repeats a maintenance oath in twice a day, is the main breach. The outer tear of Chapter 5 and the tear of Chapter 25 are the same kind of event and not the same place, and the Chapter 25 tear is a *scar in a place* — the first in the series — while Owen's Chapter 38 mark is a *scar in a person* and the first scar that ends a life.

3. **A return mark is established by a separate, expensive, witnessed act, and the act is a plot event with a cost and a date.** Chapter 27, the twenty-eighth day, at the top of the tide, at the sixth hour. The cost column: the standing labour in the yard doubled for the day, a season of the bottom field entered against the instrument, and the day of four men's time that the fresh inspection of the day before came out of, entered again because it had now been taken twice. The working was performed by Hesper Saye, Tamsin Quill, Hettis Roke and a Saltwake mason in the low room by the salt wharf, in about forty minutes. The mark belongs to the person who goes and to nobody else, and **a person without a mark cannot cross through a controlled threshold, which is why the first crossing out of this world costs a fresh inspection, a broken set of terms, and a boy's decision.** The instrument was used in breach of its own terms, is entered as a breach, and is not a precedent.

4. **The interface cannot be refused out of existence, and it now offers to end the obligation rather than to begin it.** **Three prompts in this batch, in Chapters 24, 27 and 29**, all true, all with the cost in the grammatical position where a cost goes. **Chapter 22 carries no prompt** — its four clauses are the Reed Reach's term sheet, a document read on a raft. The closing construction of the batch is new and it is the most dangerous thing the interface has ever said to Adrian: *a debt may be closed*, and a closed debt is a small one, and a small one may be paid by any person and accepted on behalf of the debtor, and *there is no further cost.* That is not a lie about the wall. It is a lie about the field, and it is an offer of **relief**. A system that offers relief is more dangerous than a system that offers power, because relief is the thing a tired person actually wants. Adrian refused it in public and asked to be **continued as a creditor**, which is the correct answer and the humiliating one.

5. **The channel is two-way, and being two-way is not the same as being controllable.** On the twenty-fifth day the black line in the **chapel stone** — not the floor, not the arch, not the gate — carried twelve words of English out loud in a yard in front of about three hundred and ten people, in a flat reading voice, and Adrian understood them and eight hundred million Veyrans did not. It is the first time the stone has said anything that was not the old form of the maintenance oath, and it is the first time a thing from the other side has been audible in public in this world. **The main breach still does not speak. The gate is measured and never heard from.** The voice's source is still a man standing in the lock join below; the chapel stone is still the channel; and the channel can be closed by nobody, at any cost, by anybody. Later volumes may not make it steerable.

6. **A working with no witness leaves its residue in the person, and a bystander is the commonest case.** Mina Okoye, Chapter 25, in a doorway in the lock-chapel yard an hour after a floor came apart, and it is the volume's whole theory of consent in a form that has nothing to do with magic: **a hold is a working without a witness, and the residue of one goes into the person who did it.** This is the mechanism by which Owen Park's shoulder is destroyed and it is the same mechanism by which the city's own flood work quietly spends its dockers four at a time. Any later scene in which a character acts physically for somebody else in a resonant place is a working, and it should be costed as one whether or not anybody says so.

7. **Stage 1 does not grow in this volume and the terms that govern it have not changed.** Adrian remains a Witness. He still cannot perform a working, open a threshold, or hold a place. In Chapter 25 he was in the lower gallery of the Lock of Salt as the man with a rule and a stub of chalk, was asked in front of eleven men whether he was in the working, and said no. In Chapter 27 his whole part in the first crossing was to read a line and mark it in three places, and Hesper Saye made him say that out loud afterwards **so that it would be in a book before it was a story.** Do not let a later chapter quietly promote him into the working because the story needs a climax. The volume's climax is performed by Tamsin Quill, Ottoline Vask, the dock crew and Hettis Roke, and Adrian is the man who says the number.

## 12. Rules established in Volume 01 Chapters 31–40

These are additions to the sections above, not replacements, and they are on the page and binding from Chapter 31 onward.

1. **A rescue is a working, and a working needs a pattern, and a hole is not a pattern.** Ottoline Vask, in a yard, in front of the witnesses, refusing to read six terms she had agreed to read eight days earlier: *a rescue is a working. That is not a figure of speech, it is the definition and it is the reason the six items exist.* Any later chapter that sends a person anywhere on the strength of a cost, a consent and a mark is re-performing Chapter 27 and must be costed and objected to as one, **and a person with a mark in their hand and a scar in a floor will use what is there.**
2. **A description is not a place.** The sixth item of the Chapter 27 terms named a site by *description* — *a dressed joint, described on the twenty-fourth day in the words of the mason and the anchorwright, entered in this book and not named* — because Vask refused to name the thing on the twenty-fourth and Adrian found her refusal a principle rather than an evasion. The description is **true of two places in Saltwake**, one of them the instrument and one of them the scar, and the second crossing went to the scar, and **the same mason's own rule from the twenty-fourth — a quarter of a thing is not a thing — is the mechanism of the volume's only death.** A later writer must name a site by what is at it and not by what has been written about it.
3. **A breach carries a person and drops them back where the rule broke, and the rule that broke can be a rule about a floor.** Chapter 37. The Chapter 27 opening was a **route**: a pattern, a place, a hand, a witness, a cost, and a person named by themselves who can say no on the day. The Chapter 38 crossing was a **breach**: a scar, two marks on one floor where one person was named, and about four minutes and a half in which nothing happened at the one place in the world where an opening has ever been made lawfully. **A breach does not respect the terms, because terms require a working and there was no working.** A line in a place can be mended with work and money; **a mark in a person cannot, because nobody steps over a hand** — Vask, in a yard, and this is the volume's standing rule about marks and it is now load-bearing for the rest of the series.
4. **The first black scar in a person's own return mark, on the thirty-eighth day, in Owen Park.** It is not a black line and it is not a black scar in a place. **It is the first scar carried in a living person that ends a life and the first one another traveller might have tried to inherit, and it did not transfer: Eli Mercer's mark is not one, and about a third of it went.** A dead traveler's permission does not transfer automatically. **The one return mark in the world before Chapter 35 was Eli Mercer's, established on day 27; a second was established for Owen Park on day 37 and destroyed on day 38.** Adrian's thin black line in the palm is still a line, is thirty-nine days old, and has not moved one sixteenth of an inch, and the two must never be merged.
5. **A rule in a keeper's hand on a keeper's wall is a record, and a reason in a good coat's hand is an argument.** Ivenn Marrow asked for *the* true reason for refusing a workway to be written in Adrian's own hand on the keeper's board; Adrian wrote his own; Owen Park, on the thirty-eighth, in a room, four hours before he died, identified the difference and asked for the true reason for *his* refusal to be written in a book, and Adrian understood then that the request had been about protection and not about power. **Any later writer who uses the keeper's board must remember that it is not a roll, that it is washed every Sunday, that a Crown clerk can stand in the yard and read it, and that a rule on it outlives the man who wrote it.**
6. **A cost that has been entered five times is not a cost; it is a notation.** Sera Vail, in her own hand, read out twice, on the thirty-seventh day: a season of the bottom field entered five times in one month against the instrument, the mark, the day's terms and the working on the twenty-fifth. **A cost column must be re-entered at its new size or it stops being a cost and becomes a figure a court likes the look of.** This is the volume's standing correction to Adrian's own instrument and it is his fault, because the column is his.
7. **A record is not a finding and cannot be used as one.** Priya Shah, in a hall of about three hundred and fifty, on the thirty-ninth, using the standing clause of Adrian's own form: *it obliges somebody to answer it and it obliges nobody to believe it, and it is not a finding and it cannot be used as one.* Therefore nobody may write *he died because*, and the entry of the death is four pages instead of one sentence, and it contains three causes instead of one, and every person in the room wanted a shorter entry. **The plural principle's second visible act in the series is a person refusing to let a record collapse into a cause.**
8. **A record is not a fence.** Nell Bray, docker, in a yard of about two hundred and ten people, outarguing both the holder of the pull and a Hearthguard of nineteen years: a record tells you afterwards; a fence is there before. **This is the sentence the next ten chapters of the series have to answer, and it is the reason the trial licence of Chapters 48–50 has to be a thing that stands in a place and not a thing that is read in a book.**
9. **A description of what a person said they saw goes in the book under a heading about the person, or it goes nowhere.** The eleventh page, the fifth column, Gwil Merrivale's sighting of a thumb-shaped light at the bottom of a reed channel below the second gate at Long Water — the first statement of a thing a person said they saw ever entered in the Concord's book, entered on the wrong page, under a heading about eleven people from another world, because there is nowhere else in the book for a stranger's words, and countersigned without being read. **The form on the loft wall failed and the book did not, and Vail has said in print that she put it there to get it in.**
10. **The interface's fourteenth construction is the most dangerous and it is not a cost sentence — it removes one.** Three prompts in this batch, in Chapters 33, 36 and 40, and none in the other seven. *A private debtor may be replaced and the debtor's name is not struck out*; *the terms of a crossing may be written by the person who will be carried through it, and a person carried through a working is not answerable for the working*; *an entry that is wrong may be entered against any entry, and the person named must answer it, or withdraw it, or stand by it in public and say that they can say no more*. The second takes a thing away and calls it free, which is a new move: **the earlier eleven put a cost in the position where a cost goes; this one puts a freedom there.** The third is the interface arriving in a yard, in a language built out of Adrian's own, nine days after this city invented a procedure for being wrong in public, and it is the first evidence in the series that the thing can read a local record. Adrian refused all three in public and named the third a version lock with better manners, which is the first time he has used the phrase for anything but the Crown's letters.
11. **The Single Witness problem arrives in this world in a reasonable accent, from a man in a good coat, in a yard, and it is not the Registry's demand.** Chapter 40: the join in the Lock of Salt gate can be closed, and closing it wants the same hand on every occasion for as long as the city stands, and that is a fact about how a working works. **Sera Vail has entered underneath it that it is not the notice of the Registrar of Crossings and Tides, is not the same instrument, and that the two documents which say the same thing do not agree with each other.** The Registry is a bureaucracy and its instrument is a form with a seal and a quarterly sitting; the Choir's answer is a design about how a city witnesses. Adrian does not know yet which one he is looking at, and **a later writer must not let the reader confuse them either.**

## 13. Rules established in Volume 01 Chapters 41–47

These are additions to the sections above, not replacements, and they are on the page and binding from Chapter 41 onward.

1. **A single-holder standing is the old form of the oath with the words taken out, and it is the shape every instrument in this volume's argument has.** Chapter 0042. A join wants one pair of hands; the *old form* of the maintenance oath bound the gate **and the standing of the man in it**; that form was stopped in Saltwake sixty years ago after a man in it died because the standing was a man and the man could not be got. **The Crown's third instrument, the Registry's notice, the hundred and thirty words of the fortieth, and Marrow's private offer of the nineteenth day of the new month are all the same clause, and only two of the four come from a court.** The term is **standing**, and a thing that has a standing is a thing whose answer cannot be answered. It is a hundred and forty years older than the coat it arrives in. **Any later chapter that offers a cheaper, more local, more voluntary version of a single-author instrument is offering the same thing, and the objection is not about who pays.**
2. **Adrian never performed a working in this batch and performed one in this volume.** Chapter 0043, in the lower gallery, four feet back, with a rule and a stub of chalk, while a mason took a prop out of a near gate and a boy of nineteen held the chalk. Chapter 0047, in the same gallery, at the eleventh hour of the night, with the same rule, reading a number that had not moved. **The hand in the closing working is the crew and is not a man, and Adrian is not on the roll of persons who may open it, at his own request, in his own hand, with the date on it, and nobody whose standing comes from him may open, inspect, sign or suspend it.** Do not promote him into the working in Chapters 48–50; the volume's climax is performed by named locals and he is the man who says the number and reads the terms.
3. **The distributed working is the ordinary maintenance of the lock and not a new rite**, and it is **a widening, not a rota**, and the toll and the pattern are the same forty words. Six conditions, entered and sealed on the forty-seventh day: no single person may open or suspend it and there is no opening in it at all; four named inspectors and a gauge before every tide in whatever weather there is, and if the gauge reads what it read on the twenty-seventh day of this flood the tide is not worked; a flood-season review with a date on the page before the day; a right to be asked before a day's work is added, in a room, in words, with the answer entered; a roll of forty names with **no name at the top of it**, owed by the city and not by a man, a man who has not been paid does not come and there is no entry and no fault and nobody goes to anybody's door; and the bar on the holder of the pull. **The city is the debtor, and the price has no final figure: the day the bottom field is not paid in March, the shift stops and the join stops being worked and the join will be where it is.**
4. **The owing is the mechanism and not the fairness.** Renna Ord. A crew turns up because the city owes them and they have not been paid, not because the work is right; a thing nobody has asked for cannot be owed, therefore cannot be paid, therefore is a tax; and a refusal a person cannot afford is not a refusal. **These two sentences are the reason the crew refused the first version of the working and accepted the second, and any later chapter that asks a crew to do something for free has re-opened the forty-first question of this batch and must be answered on the page.**
5. **Answerable is not the opposite of possible.** The standing test for every instrument offered in this world, and the reason the Chapter 46 field is the worst of the sixteen: *a person struck out of a roster may be entered again by a person who remains on it, and the person who enters him is answerable for him.* If a thing cannot be done it cannot be done and a page saying so is a wall. If a thing can be done by a person who will answer for it, then it can be done, and there is a quarterly sitting and a stipend and a lawyer in Aurel. **The interface's first construction that names a process instead of a person, and a person can be refused in a yard and a process cannot be refused in a yard, it can only be filled in.**
6. **A promise is not a term and a page is.** Chapter 0047, Priya Shah, in front of about two hundred people: *do not promise; promises have no date and no price and nobody can check them in four months; sign the paper.* A promise binds one person to another and there is no page, and the only man this city watched make a promise made it by saying he had asked for nothing in a room for a month, and he is under a board. **Any later instrument in this series is entered with terms, a date, a cost, a right of review and a right of refusal on the page, and is never entered as a promise, and a character who offers one is offering a Single Witness instrument with better manners.**
7. **The statement of error's third clause is a real procedure and is not a version lock, and using it is correct.** A pale field on the fortieth night of the flood offered the whole of the Concord's first procedure in Adrian's own language and Adrian refused it in a yard of about a hundred and forty people and called it a version lock with better manners. On the forty-seventh he **stood by a statement of error entered against himself by name and said publicly that he could say no more**, and said aloud that both facts are true and that he does not know which is more important. **The clause is a local instrument with a notary, an hour, a person named, and a term. The field is a field with no office and no hour.** A later writer must keep the two apart and must not let the field be mistaken for the procedure.
8. **The gate was measured on a working tide and stepped, and the step was entered as a decision in a mason's own hand before the number was read.** The twenty-third reading is **eight and seven-eighths**, a quarter of an inch in one step, and the step is a **decision** and not a press: Vask took the old near-gate prop out and put a new one in at the seventh hour of the morning of the forty-third day because a staging leg had to go where the old one was, took eleven minutes, and entered it herself. **A press is a curve and a work is a decision and a crack does not step**, and about nine people in that gallery decided the number was a boy's within four seconds, and a fact was entered instead of a fault because **a fault can be given to somebody and a fact can only be carried.** The column is still **not continuous** and has two ruled lines before the twenty-third reading and two after it. The next tide is the forty-ninth day, a Monday, and the working is on the board for that day.
9. **Nobody was asked the crew.** The standing repair of the whole volume, and it is Renna Ord's, not the protagonist's. The last forty days of this city's book were written by nineteen people about a crew of forty, and about nine people had known for a month that a crew was the answer and had not been asked and had not said so, and all three of them said the same eleven words: *we had not been asked and we were not going to say.* **Any later chapter that finds out what a city is going to do must find it out by asking, in the words they use, in a room they are in, and must dramatise the asking.**
10. **The interface has now made sixteen offers across five batches and has never once registered a refusal of one**, and it has no memory of any of them. Batch 0005 carried **two**, in Chapters 42 and 46, and **none in 41, 43, 44, 45 or 47**, and **a room of two hundred and forty people committing a man to a thing for the rest of his life produced no field at all.** The Chapter 37 finding stands and is now twice: **a man dying produced no message and a city committing produced no message.** Its silences are usable and are becoming more usable as the volume approaches its climax. **A clerk from the Registry has been keeping a book of these fields since the eighth day of the flood — eight by the forty-seventh — all in a stranger's language, none in anybody else's, and he has never been asked to stop and is not going to be, and this city has not convicted him of anything and is not going to.**

## 14. Rules established in Volume 01 Chapters 48–50

These are additions to the sections above, not replacements, and they are on the page in the volume's closing three chapters. They are binding for Volume 02.

1. **A form of watching a scar is a form and not a promise, and it has a price on it.** Chapter 0048. Five items on a slate on a nail in the lock-chapel arch: salt, four chalk depths, four people twice a day, a readable slate, and the price of the salt. **It cannot close anything, cannot mend a dressed joint under a floor, and cannot say where a residue will go. No later chapter may make it do either of the two things it cannot do, and no later chapter may let a form become a promise.** A form with no price on it is a favour and a favour is a thing that stops.

2. **A question is not a post, and a post about a person is replaced by a form about a place.** Chapter 0049. The keeper's rule about a man came off a board in daylight on the sixteenth day of the new month; the post of holder of the pull ended at noon on the forty-ninth because its term had a date on it; and the duty inside it — that somebody is told and asked before the ground begins — went onto a nail as a **question**, refusable, with the refusal entered. **Any later writer who needs a man to be the answer for a floor must show the rule coming off a wall in daylight first.** A post is a chair with a man in it; a question is a page.

3. **A crack does not step, and a decision can be looked up while a number can only be taken.** Chapter 0049. The main breach stepped on the eighteenth day of the new month and again on the twenty-fourth, and both steps are entered in an unnamed mason's own hand **before the number was read**, and both readings are eight and seven-eighths, and the join is closed and dressed. **A press is a curve and a work is a decision and a crack does not step; a closed join does not get narrower, it gets dressed.** The column is not continuous, has two ruled lines after the twenty-third reading with one filled and **one left**, and **a later writer may not give it a figure that is not in `state/continuity.md` and may not move a step or take the twenty-fifth reading.**

4. **A working can be made cheaper without being made private, and the count per opening is not the quorum.** Chapter 0050, and the volume's central argument. **Four to authorise, two to open.** The quorum that authorised the route on the fourth day of the flood — Tamsin Quill, Nell Bray, Ovin Sarr and Hettis Roke — does not change and is not changed by any instrument. **If an instrument in this series reduces a burden, check whether the reduction moves a decision into a person. If it does, it is the old form of the oath with the words taken out and a smaller number on it.** The two witnesses may not both be of the crew, because the crew is the hand and the hand may not hold the door.

5. **The trial licence is the first standing route in the series, and it is built out of ordinary work.** One named living person per opening; two witnesses not of the crew, who may each refuse; a fresh inspection signed by four people before every opening in whatever weather there is; a return mark separately established, costed and witnessed, belonging to the person who goes, **with the mark not being evidence of the route and the route not being evidence of the mark**; **no automatic marks and none for a person who asks**; a review with a date on the page before the day; suspension by any one of nine named persons, by the receiving community by its own count, or by the four; **no land rights, no class, no standing, no workway for anybody named in it**, entered as a term and not a favour; and a page that says what happens on the day the crew does not turn up. **There is no working route in this world and there is still no crowd route: the licence is the only one, it is on trial, and it can be lost at a review in a room in daylight.**

6. **A record of a death is a record and not a finding, and a Crown column is a column and not a reason.** Chapter 0050. **Nobody may write *he died because*.** Owen Park's four pages stand, are not shortened, are not summarised in any instrument, return, quarterly description or letter, and every sentence in them is entered as what a person said with the attribution in the sentence. When the Concord's assessor of strangers tried to compress them into a Crown column four lines high, **the correction came from the person who wrote the clause and not from the man the clause is about**, and the four lines that replaced it were true, fitted the column, and said nothing about cause. **A volume or a series may not shorten a record because a form is small.**

7. **A stipend is a wage, a wage is owed to a person, and a person is described on a roll.** Chapter 0050. **The price of being visible in this city is being described, and the description goes to Aurel four times a year in a wooden case.** Any payment proposed for the seventeen must be costed against this before it is costed against the city's ability to pay. **A wage is not a gift and a stipend is not a settlement, and the cheapest way to bind a person in this world is a line in a quarterly return.**

8. **Nobody answers for the next test, and that is a term.** Chapter 0050. A small seat with a name on it and a review date was offered the holder of the pull a third time in five weeks and **the instrument says nobody**, and the answerer says the sentence himself: *a licence with my name on the top of it is a document that only this city has ever been frightened of.* **A licence with nobody in it is the first thing this city has written that is not shaped like a man**, and it is the standing local answer to every single-author instrument in the series, from the Registry's notice to Marrow's private offer.

9. **The crew is the ordinary work of the lock and the cost column is the wall.** Chapters 0048–0050. **The forty men still go under the near gate twice a day at the ordinary volume, out of the line and not together, and that is what the bottom field pays for. The working on the tide is once a month now that the join is shut.** The cost column was entered seven times, has no figure on the end of it, and a woman in a village four miles away has to decide in March how much of her life to put in the ground. **Nothing in this volume saved the field and no later volume may retroactively save it by saying the join was closed earlier or the work was cheaper than it was.** Renna Ord's prediction of four men hurt before March is entered as a prediction with a date and the right date beside it, neither above the other, and both stand.

10. **The interface has now made seventeen offers across six batches, has never once registered a refusal, and was silent four times in a row at the volume's most consequential moments.** One field in this batch, in Chapter 0050, and it is the best of the seventeen because **it puts no lie in it at all**: the roll may be closed, the person named may be returned on the next tide once, and the person named does not owe the standing charge. **Three true things and a price, and the price is a field and nine households who have refused twice.** The refusal is immediate and the reason is social: closing the roll means a woman in Camden is never told anything, ever, by anybody. **A man dying, a city committing a man to a thing for the rest of his life, a city closing a join in its own gate and a city signing a licence have each produced no field at all**, and that is now four for four and is the standing evidence that the wall speaks only when it wants something. **Any later field that is attractive must be checked for what it puts in the position where a cost goes, and by the volume's close a reader should be able to find that position without being told.** *(Amended by §15.10: the silence count became five for five, and a city discovering that its door does not go home joined the list. Further amended by §16.18, §17.18 and §18.18: the count is now **eight for eight**, and a city reading a document it cannot refuse has joined the list.)*

11. **No person from another world acquired a land right, a class privilege, a title, a workway or a seat anywhere in Volume 01, and Adrian Vale is Unbound on the last line of the volume.** His entry in the roll of strangers says **Unbound** in his own words with two dates on it; his page of the trial licence says he is party to the instrument and is given nothing by it; the seat of holder of the pull is vacant; and the licence's line for the next test is **nobody.** **The first licensed player ends Volume 01 as a man who reads a number off a wall four feet back from it and says out loud, when he is made to, that he is not in the working.**

## 15. Rules established in Volume 02, Chapters 51–60

These are additions to the sections above, not replacements, and they are on the page in the first ten days of Volume 02. **They are binding from Chapter 61 onward.**

1. **A receiving community cannot be made from this side.** Chapter 0051. A controlled opening needs a receiving community that understands the purpose; the far side of the Narrow Mark is a room with no window and no door anybody has found, about eleven people have stood in it, and **no instrument in this world can create a receiving party from this side.** The licence cannot go home and cannot bring anybody in, and the reason is page two of the licence, which nineteen signers did not read. **Any later chapter that opens a route toward a person must either produce a named receiving party on the far side, with a date, or say in a room that it cannot be done.** A refusal of an opening is a *reason*, not a refusal of the person, and it is entered with a name, a date and a person who asked.

2. **A fact about a shape is a third thing a question can be**, after a fact and a fault. It has no person to give it to. It is entered as a fact, it is not a fault, and it is not nothing.

3. **A description is a removal with a column for where the person is.** Chapter 0055. The roll, the quarterly return, the schedule of crossings and the ground-book of a gate all have the same shape, and the last column — where the person is — is the widest one and is always blank, because the office is nine hundred miles away and has never once sent anybody to fill it in. **No name in Saltwake has come out of one of those cases in nine hundred years.** Any later chapter that sends a roll, a schedule, a return or a description **must enter the column and enter the blankness.** A record obliges somebody to answer it and obliges nobody to believe it, and a form may not shorten a record because a form is small.

4. **A workway is a job and not a power, and a job means somebody is entitled to expect a thing of the holder.** Chapter 0053. In Saltwake every other work has a place where somebody expects the holder on an ordinary morning — a bench and a bar, the Reach, four beds and a shelf, a chain, a floor and a pail — **and there is no office, guild, bench or Crown instrument that can say what a holder of a workway is expected to do.** A workway in this city is a claim on a job that does not exist. **The cost of a workway is not the work. The cost is that a standing is a reason to be angry, and a seat is only ever got rid of by the people who are angry at it.** The procedure written in Chapter 0057 has nine clauses and nine weaknesses, and **it cannot compel and cannot remove, and both weaknesses are on its face, and the work itself is not written down anywhere and the document says so in twenty-eight words.**

5. **A rule about a thing is not a rule about the world, and the protagonist's own page proves it in public.** Chapter 0057. The page of the twenty-sixth day of this flood bars a workway offered to any of the seventeen **by this instrument or by anybody using it.** It is the longest page in the licence and it is perfect, and **a pale field in a room is not the instrument, nobody used anything, and there was no hand on any lever.** The bar was not broken and it did not reach. Standing test for the rest of the series: **if an instrument bars a document and is expected to bar a practice, it is the same page, and it will fail in the same way.**

6. **The two workway fields of Volume 02 put the whole of the cost on a named person and call that fair, and neither contains a lie.** The lie is the position. Each states the benefit plainly, says that nothing is asked of this city and nothing is asked of the licence, and closes with *the cost is entered* in the position where a cost goes. **A reader should now be able to find that position without being told, and every later attractive field must be checked against it.**

7. **A guild is not a person, and a guild can be refused, and the fear of the word *list* in this city is a fear of persons and has never once been about guilds.** Chapter 0059, Jonah Pike's fifth and sixth sentences. A city in which nothing that goes wrong has a person in it is a city in which nothing that goes wrong can be fixed. **A list of houses with two strangers in each is a roll and a roll goes to Aurel; two names on a page with a date is a page.** A household may say no, need not give a reason, is not asked a third time after a second, and is not entered anywhere. **This is the front door of the White Ledger and it was opened in a yard in about two hundred and forty people's hearing.**

8. **The honest useless form.** Chapter 0056. A form completed honestly by people who have decided to be unhelpful is complete and carries nothing, because **a summary is what an office acts on and a summary is where a decision hides.** It is not deception: every column is filled, the true hour, the true cost, the true number of men, no rounding, and the document cannot be reduced. **Its cost is that compliance is still compliance, and it writes down what eleven people want, and that goes to a building nine hundred miles away four times a year in a wooden case.** A Crown fee is a statement about what an office thinks you are, and **an office that is not paid does not collect the fee either.**

9. **A cost that stops is a cost that has stopped and is never to be entered as paid by anybody, on any day, for any reason.** Used for the first time on the thirty-third day of the new month, in public, and it worked exactly as written. **And a thing that is not done is not done** — not deferred, not short, not carried forward.

10. **The interface has now made nineteen offers across seven batches and has never once registered a refusal of one**, and the count in a clerk's book at Aurel is ten, and the count of refusals in a second book kept at home is nineteen and has never been shown to anybody. **Two messages in ten chapters, both workways, one refused in under a second by the wrong man and one accepted in four seconds.** **And the silences were five for five in this batch: a man dying, a city committing a man to a thing for the rest of his life, a city closing a join in its own gate, a city signing a licence, and a city discovering that its door does not go home have each produced nothing at all.** *(Amended by §16.18, §17.18 and §18.18: the count is now **eight for eight**.)* The wall spoke twice in ten days and both times it wanted a standing. **A standing is now in this world and cannot be taken back by refusing more of them.**

11. **A man who cannot be right about a number hears a number, and a join that is dressed does not knock, and both are entered and neither is deleted.** Chapter 0060. Three knocks on the twenty-ninth day of the new month and four on the thirty-fifth, reported by a man in the lower gallery of the Lock of Salt, **with nobody opening anything and nothing in the city being done at either hour.** A number going up is not a pattern and a number going up is also not nothing. **No later chapter may use the knocks as an instrument, a signal, a proof or a rescue, and no form on a nail may be made to say who is in the room.**

12. **Adrian is Stage 1 at the end of Volume 02 Batch 0001 and has gained nothing.** Sixty days in this world, no working performed, no mark of his own, no workway, no title, no land right, no class privilege, no seat, party to the trial licence and given nothing by it. **He has put his own name in one Crown document, in a column headed *person to be asked*, and a woman of twenty-four told him in twenty-five words what he had done.** In about six pages he has written down what a person may not do and in one sentence what a person may do, and the sentence is twenty-four words long and it belongs to Tamsin Quill and she said it once, on a stair.

## 16. Rules established in Volume 02, Chapters 61–70

These are additions to the sections above, not replacements, and they are on the page in the ten days before the flood-season review. **They are binding from Chapter 71 onward.**

1. **A review's questions are not a list of subjects. A review's questions are a decision about what the answer is allowed to be, and the person who writes them has done something to the door that no amount of daylight undoes.** Chapter 0068. A date on a slate with no questions on it is a queue; a page of questions with a date on it is a review; and the difference between the two is a decision that only the people already in the room can make. **The only people who can write the questions are the people who are already in the room, and about nine people in Saltwake are not already in the room, and two of them are on no roll at all.**

2. **The difference between a question a city asks when it wants to keep a door and a question a city asks when it wants to be able to close one is not in the tense and is not in the mood. It is in who is able to answer.** Sera Vail, Chapter 0068, nine words and twenty-six years of Crown procedure. A question the holder of no standing can answer is a question; a question only somebody the room can be angry at can answer is a mechanism. **A page may therefore be checked for this, and the check is not stylistic.**

3. **A second page headed *what we are not asking and why* is part of the instrument and not an annexe.** Chapter 0068. Nineteen reasons in nineteen lines in nineteen hands, and if the instrument is reviewed without the second page, the review is reviewed without it too. **A page about what a city is not asking, sent by itself, is a thing an office can answer in one line; sent beside the questions it is a thing an office has to answer in a paragraph.**

4. **A Wayhouse is local law and local law in this world means somebody in the room had to say no to something.** Chapter 0064. Six things: a bed and a bowl for anybody travelling; a levy on the road's trade at a rate the traders agreed among themselves; the keeping household named with a date, and a household is a door with people in it and does not go to Aurel in a wooden case; **open to a list of nobody, with no roll of who has slept there and no roll of who has eaten;** closable by its own household for its own reasons on a date on the page, and not asked why twice; and not a Crown instrument and not a page anybody may carry to a building nine hundred miles away. **And the sixth, which is the only reason it will still be there in four months: nobody may be turned away for asking what it is, at the door, on the step, in any weather, and being answered is not agreeing to anything.** The levy is the only one of the six that costs somebody who is not in the room, and it is collected whether anybody uses the house or not. **A household may say no, need not give a reason, is not asked a third time after a second, and is not entered anywhere — and a later writer must show that this same clause, applied to a bed, produces the disappearance of a person who did not turn up, because the clause that protects the house is the clause that removes the person.**

5. **A board anybody can read is a thing anybody can copy, and moving a thing off a wall onto a page with a date is the only legality mechanism this city has found.** Chapter 0064. **Nobody in that yard said the word *legal* and about nine people had it in their heads.** A thing becomes legal in a city when a person can be angry at it, and not one person in that yard could be angry at a page with a date on it.

6. **There is no money in this city in any coin a wage can be counted in, and a wage must be counted in something a person can carry.** Chapter 0065. Four transactions, three mistakes and one habit. **And the cost of being paid on a second page is that a wage off a second page is a wage a person can be removed from, which is Renna Ord's rule with a new mechanism behind it.** Any Volume 02 payment, wage, credit or advance must be costed against **a stipend is a wage, a wage is owed to a person, a person is described on a roll** and against **a wage off a second page is a wage a person can be removed from**, in that order.

7. **A bar on a document is a bar and not a wall, and a page is not a rule and does not stand up on its own.** Chapter 0065, and this is the pre-echo of the Volume 02 midpoint. The twentieth field was not stopped by the bar: **the bar bars the instrument and not the wall, a pale field in a room is not the instrument, nothing was used, there was no hand on any lever, and the bar did not reach.** What stopped it was **nine people in a room reading a page out loud, which took nine seconds, and none of the nine had any authority.** The acceptance in the same room by the only person who could say no to it is entered in her own words with a date, and **nobody has yet asked her whether she meant it.** Any later chapter that needs a document to stop something must show a room, in daylight, with people in it, or it must show the failure.

8. **A Crown office that will take payment in kind has learned what a district has, and a district that can pay a fee in the only thing it has is a district whose good faith has a price on it and a buyer.** Chapter 0067. The office adopted the rate as a standing rate for the district, would not review it in the quarter, and adopted it the same evening without a committee. **The generosity is the finding and a kind offer is worse than a rude one, because a rude one can be refused on its face.** A line is added in a notary's hand: that the office has been told what this district has, and this district has been told what the office will take, and neither is on any page the office has sent and both are now on a page in this city. **Both are facts and facts cannot be un-learned by a person, and the salt was not sent and the finding stands anyway.**

9. **A district that is described as paying in kind is a district that has told the office what it can be made to do, and a thing an office knows it can make a district do is not a favour and is not a courtesy and is a mechanism.** The refusal of the twenty-first field, Chapter 0067, in three parts, of which the third is this one. **The twenty-first field is the best of the twenty-one because there is nothing in it to point at: it is not a workway, it puts no person in the grammatical position where a cost goes, and it is an offer to describe a place.** Standing test for every later field in the series: **a field that describes a thing, rather than offering one, is a different animal and not a cheaper version.**

10. **Being useful is not safe and is not unsafe. It is a description.** Nadia Brandt, twenty-nine, Chapter 0066, to two men who were both arguing about the wrong thing. A description is a removal with a column for where the person is, and that is not hers and is in this city in about two hundred and forty people's hearing. **Any character in this series who keeps a man safe by making him useful has made a description and not a protection, and the character has to be able to be told so by a person with no standing.**

11. **The right to be asked is a thing a holder can refuse, and refusing it is not ingratitude.** Chapter 0066. A man of thirty-five refused, in four seconds and correctly, the one offer Adrian Vale has ever made anybody: *you are the only person here who gets asked. You, not me.* **And the second half of the refusal is the sentence the volume is about: if you ask me, it stops being a thing I do and it becomes a thing I am a part of.** Both halves are correct, one of them is load-bearing, and neither man said which.

12. **A person may be asked whether he is able to do a thing, and the answer may be no, and the answer is entered and is not a fault and is not a wage and does not go in the roll.** Chapter 0061, nineteenth of nineteen lines. **It is the only one of the nineteen that is a question, it has the same shape as the fifth item on the form on the nail, and it is the correct shape for the whole of this series: a person may be asked, and the answer is a document, and the document is the only protection, and a person who is never asked is not a person who has refused.**

13. **Nineteen lines of what a person in this city may be asked to turn up for.** Chapter 0061. **A widening and not a rota, and a rota is a list with an order in it and this has no order and no beginning.** The load-bearing clauses: a person who does not turn up has not refused and is not paid and owes nothing; a person who turns up and is not paid is owed it and the owing is entered; **nobody goes to the door of a person who has not been paid;** no name is written at the top of anything; a refusal does not stop the line being offered again on a different day; nobody is asked twice about a thing inside nine days; nobody is asked to turn up for a thing that is not paid until the paying is entered. **A person with no work may be asked to turn up for anything, may refuse, and the refusal goes nowhere.**

14. **The clause that protects a household from being asked again is the same clause that makes a person who did not turn up unfindable, and a person cannot have one without the other.** Chapter 0061, the first Wayhouse bed of two. **It is not a fault and it is a shape, and it is the general form of every protection this city has built in seventy days: the describing was the whole of the protection and there is no second thing.**

15. **A refusal which costs other people money is a decision about other people, and a Crown instrument can use that clause correctly, in the form required, to help a village, and the village is right and cannot afford to be right.** Chapter 0069. **A correct instrument used correctly can do damage, and no instrument in this world can make a correct thing expensive.** The village refused a credit, lost the quarter, and a Crown officer entered that he was the one who made it affordable and that nobody asked him to and that he will be asked about it.

16. **A form written by a city that has decided to be unhelpful and a form written by a city that has nothing look the same on the page, and the page is what an office reads.** Chapter 0069, Priya Shah's eighth sentence. **The honest useless form's cost, entered in Batch 0001 as *compliance is still compliance*, has now produced a consequence nobody planned: a Crown officer read the shape of it and came to a village in four days. Its first consequence in Aurel is still about eleven weeks out and must not be delivered early.**

17. **Adrian Vale is Stage 1 at the end of Volume 02 Batch 0002 and has gained nothing.** Seventy days in this world, no working performed, no mark of his own, no workway, no title, no land right, no class privilege, no seat, party to a trial licence that is twenty days old and gives him nothing, **and offered, for the first time in seventy days, the right to be asked, and refused it correctly.** He performed no working in Volume 01 either. **The black line in his palm is sixty-nine days old and has not moved one sixteenth of an inch; the measurement column has twenty-four readings, both of the last two eight and seven-eighths, and one ruled line left, and the twenty-fifth reading has not been taken and was not taken in this batch.**

18. **The interface has now made twenty-one offers across eight batches and has never once registered a refusal of one**, and the count in a clerk's book at Aurel is twelve and the count of refusals in a second book kept at home is twenty-one and has never been shown to anybody. **Two messages in ten chapters, in Chapters 65 and 67. One of the two was accepted by somebody and one was refused, and the refusal is not the interesting half.** Both of this batch's fields put **nothing** in the grammatical position where a cost goes — the twentieth not even a person, and the twenty-first not even a cost — **and the twenty-first is the first field in the series that is an offer to describe a place rather than to give a person a standing.** *(Amends §14.10 and §15.10: the silence count became six for six, and a city finding out what a person with no work is for joined the list. Further amended by §17.18 and §18.18: the count is now **eight for eight**.)* **The wall spoke twice in ten days and both times it wanted a description — of a person, and of a place. The standing is now in this world and cannot be taken back by refusing more of them, and a description cannot be refused by the person described if the description removes the asking.**

## 17. Rules established in Volume 02, Chapters 71–80

These are additions to the sections above, not replacements, and they are on the page in the volume's midpoint batch. **They are binding from Chapter 81 onward.**

1. **A review's questions are a decision about what the answer is allowed to be, and the person who writes them has done something to a door that no amount of daylight undoes.** Chapter 0075, in front of about two hundred and forty people. The binding partition of the eleven is **four that keep a door, six that can close one, one that is neither and is about a person, and one trap — the third, drafted by the notary, who may not grade her own paper.** A later writer may not restate the count as four-and-six over eleven and may not make the number of traps four.
