## 36. Rules established by the review repair pass on Volume 06, Chapters 0261–0270 — AND THE TWO THAT ARE GENERAL

**These are additions to the sections above and not replacements. §33 IS THE CALENDAR AND IS NOT CHANGED. §32 IS THE HOUSE FORMATTING RULE AND IS NOT CHANGED. §35 IS THE SECOND TEN DAYS OF THIS VOLUME AND IS NOT CHANGED except in five figures that §36.3 names, and every one of those five was wrong. §§23 to 31 are not changed. The full pass is `state/batch-summaries/volume-06-batch-0002.md` §14 and it is the record, and this section is the part a later writer has to carry.**

1. **A RULE SATISFIED MECHANICALLY CAN PRODUCE THE THING THE RULE EXISTS TO PREVENT, AND IT DID, IN THIS VOLUME, IN THE FIRST TEN DAYS OF THE BATCH.** The ten leads of Chapters 0261 to 0270 were required to carry four derived interval figures with the day each is measured from named in the same sentence. They carried all forty values and all forty were right. **They also came out as one paragraph written ten times with the numerals changed, which is the recapping ledger that `outline/volume-06.md` §17.2 forbids in any shape, and a reader stops on it, and no check in the batch record could see it, because every check the record ran was a check on the figures.** The general rule this establishes for every batch after this one: **a mechanical check that verifies a required element is not evidence that the required element has been written rather than satisfied. A chapter lead is read by a person and not by a script, and a lead that carries four figures has to be four different sentences.** All ten have been rewritten, no two share a sentence, the forty values are all still on the page, and the anchors are named three ways: *the sixty-ninth day of the Long Month* for the store's board, *the ninety-sixth day of the Long Month* for the case, and *the two hundred and twenty-ninth day of this flood* for the wind at that wall. **That last one is a new anchor in this volume and a later writer may use it, and it is a reference to a day and not a date in the fourth form.**

2. **A COUNT IS CHECKED AGAINST ITS ANCHOR AND NOT AGAINST ITS ARITHMETIC, AND THIS IS THE SECOND TIME IN THIS VOLUME THAT THE DIFFERENCE MATTERED.** The case's age was ten in Chapter 0261 and twelve in Chapter 0263. Both are right. Both were measured from the first day of the Short Month, which is day 256, and the case came up the river on day 251. **A check that counts forty figures and finds forty numerals has not checked anything an anchor is for.** The same fault was in the two chapters that dated Hettis Roke's refusal, and the same fault was then written into `state/batch-summaries/volume-06-batch-0002.md` §8.4 as a reference that was *not zero and not a fault*, which is the worst of the three available outcomes, because a state file that certifies a slip launders it. **The general rule: an interior date reference in a chapter is re-derived from the chapter that put the day on a page, and never from a state file and never from a sibling chapter's memory of it, and a state file that publishes a non-zero string as approved must name the page it was checked against.**

3. **THE FIVE FIGURES §35 CARRIED THAT WERE WRONG, so that no later writer inherits them.** The case's anchor in Chapters 0261 and 0263; the date of Roke's refusal in Chapters 0262 and 0268 and in §8.4 of the batch record; the fifteen-per-cent exchange figure in §35.16 and in three state files; the eighty-three-word maximum marked turn in §35.16 and in §8.2 of the batch record, which was a breach of `outline/volume-06.md` §17.4 published and defended rather than cut; and the 281 long sentences, which is now 269.

4. **THE BOLD IS THE ONLY SPEAKER MARKER THIS MANUSCRIPT HAS, AND A CHAPTER THAT LEAVES A TURN UNMARKED IS NOT SHORTER, IT IS UNREADABLE, AND THE METRIC THAT MEASURED IT COULD NOT SEE IT.** In the Adrian and Tamsin exchange at the end of Chapter 0270, eight turns, Tamsin Quill is marked at the first and the last and unmarked at the two in the middle. In Chapter 0267 a marked turn changes speaker three paragraphs after the last one with no section break and no attribution. In Chapter 0266 a mark opens and closes inside a clause. **Three narrative paragraphs carried bold, against §17.3, at Chapter 0262's coda, Chapter 0263's coda and Chapter 0269's last argument, and the batch record §7.1 printed that every `**` in the ten files was inside a quoted speech, which was false, and the way it was false is the general lesson: the check was run against speech and not against narration and it could not tell the two apart.** The repair is attribution, not markup: the unmarked turns are marked, a mark that had been on the wrong voice is taken off it, a change of marked voice gets a named beat in front of it, and the three narrative paragraphs are plain with not one word of their prose changed. **And the exchange figure moved from 25 of 166, fifteen per cent, to 49 of 125, thirty-nine per cent, and the general rule for every batch after this one is that a metric of exchange shape must count unmarked speeches and not only marked ones, because a batch that scores well on it by leaving half its turns unmarked has not been measured.**

5. **A SPEECH IS MEASURED WHETHER OR NOT IT IS MARKED, AND §17.4's EIGHTY WORDS IS A CEILING ON THE TURN AND NOT ON THE SPEECH.** The longest marked turn in Chapters 0261 to 0270 is seventy-eight words. A hundred-and-twenty-one-word speech in Chapter 0266 carried no mark and was longer than the eighty-three-word marked turn the batch record published and defended, and no statistic that counts marks can see it.

6. **THE ONLY DISTANCES IN THIS WORLD ARE NINE MILES, ELEVEN MILES AND FOUR MILES, AND A ROAD, AND *UP THE ROAD PAST THIS CITY* AND *OUT PAST THE LAST NAMED HOUSE*, AND NINE HUNDRED PACES PAST A MARKET TOWN, AND NO CHAPTER OF THIS VOLUME MAY PUT A NUMBER ON THE MARCHES.** The review pass removed six: nine hundred miles on a room that the same chapter measures at nine hundred paces, twice; a book carried nine hundred miles up a nine-hundred-pace road, twice; a market town at nine miles where two chapters put it at nine hundred paces; and **a coast road, which is not a road in this world at all.** **Nineteen uses of nine hundred miles stand across the ten chapters and every one of them is the Crown's page, its office, its clerk or its reading, and that is the only thing that distance is for in this book, and a later writer checking a new use of nine hundred miles should ask only that question.**

7. **A CHARACTER MAY CONTRADICT HERSELF AND A CHAPTER MAY NOT, AND THE DIFFERENCE IS WHETHER THE CONTRADICTION IS THE CHARACTER'S OWN WORK.** The woman of about thirty at the stall says on the tenth day of the Short Month that she made the pail line herself and on the eleventh that it was not hers. Repaired **as a person and not as a slip:** she takes it back in her own mouth, names the day and the hour she said it, and gives the reason, which is that she said it to a stranger and it was not true. **A reader will forgive a woman seeing something differently the next morning and will not forgive a chapter disagreeing with itself, and the cheapest repair is usually to let the second statement be about the first.**

8. **AND THREE HOUSE RULES THAT ARE NOT ABOUT PAGES.** *The narrator does not claim a fact about the manuscript.* *this book* is the Register in this world and is not the novel; two uses of it as the novel were removed from Chapters 0266 and 0267. *Two sentences in one chapter may not be the same sentence,* and a twenty-one-word sentence repeated two lines apart in Chapter 0268 was removed. *A sentence of forty words or more may not stand verbatim in more than one chapter of a batch,* and there are 269 of them across these ten files and none is duplicated, and the fall from 281 is the lead frame.

9. **AND WHAT THE PASS DID NOT DO, WHICH IS THE LIST THAT MATTERS MOST.** No plot beat, no day, no weekday, no month day, no anchor, no live thread, no cast member, no ending, no card and no outline was edited. The ninth line was not moved, dated, read aloud or resolved. The board was not written on. Nobody put a boot on the nine feet and nobody opened the bag. The keeper of the Redroot gate is at zero in every form including her working, and the proof of that is a person-by-person read and not a search. The delegation has not left. The about four hundred and forty are not in these ten chapters and their chapter is Chapter 0279. The Regent has not been put into the schedule. The consent fracture is still unpaid and is still Chapter 0272. **There is no panel in Chapters 0261 to 0270 and there should not be, and the wall said nothing on all ten days, and the one panel of this volume stays where §17.7 put it, on day 256.** `state/phase-ledger.json` is controller-owned and was not touched.

## 37. Rules established in Volume 06, Chapters 0271–0280 — WHAT THE THIRD TEN DAYS OF THE VOLUME PUT ON A PAGE

**These are additions to the sections above and not replacements. They are on the page in the third ten days of this volume. §33 IS THE CALENDAR AND IS NOT CHANGED, and its fifth form and its weekday anchors stand exactly as the outline phase wrote them. §32 IS THE HOUSE FORMATTING RULE AND IS NOT CHANGED. §34 and §35 are the first and second ten days of this volume and are NOT CHANGED and not re-stated at less than their length here. §36 is the review repair pass on Chapters 0261 to 0270 and its nine rules bind every chapter written after it and are not changed. §27.14's conflict about the twenty-fourth day of the Long Month is STILL LIVE and is not settled here and no chapter of Chapters 0271 to 0280 attaches a weekday to that day or mentions it at all. §§23 to 31 are not changed. The batch's record is `state/batch-summaries/volume-06-batch-0003.md` and the records of the first two ten days are `state/batch-summaries/volume-06-batch-0001.md` and `volume-06-batch-0002.md`.**

1. **A HETHEGUARD'S REFUSAL BINDS A BODY AND IS DATED TO AN INSTRUMENT AND NOT TO A DAY, AND IT IS THE VOLUME'S MIDPOINT.** Said out loud on the twentieth day of the Short Month, **a Wednesday, in a room over a market in a market town nine hundred paces past the last named house, in daylight, with a date in chalk on the door and about nine people in it, and it is four sentences and then one more, and the room measured the sentences and not the seconds.** Four sentences: the instrument has been used twice in this city and on two different days and a question that has gone to everybody in writing four days early cannot be changed afterwards; the first time this city used it, it stopped the man who wrote it, in front of two hundred and forty people, and the man who wrote it was standing in that room; the second time it worked and what it worked at was a Hearthguard of forty-four and it produced a no out of a man who had not been asked anything; **I refuse on the Hearthguard's behalf, the refusal binds the company, and it is dated to that instrument and not to today, and I would like the date on the door outside to stay the date on the door outside.** And then one more sentence for the route itself: **a rack that has not been worked does not come off, and a rack stands in a channel, and a channel takes its water from a gate, and eleven weeks of a channel without a household's water is eleven weeks of a salt season that is not recovered in this flood or the next one.** He said it at the same speed as the other four and did not stop at the end of it. *Open. **A later writer may not merge day 275 and day 50, may not make him the author of the instrument on either day, may not have him improved on any of the five sentences, may not attach a weekday to the twenty-fourth day of the Long Month, and may not put the about-N-seconds refrain, the person-who-does-not-want-to-be-asked refrain or the in-about-nine-seconds refrain into any chapter whose finding is a refusal.***

2. **THE REVERSAL IS ON A PAGE AND IT IS NOT A LESSON.** The plan was built out of the one fact that was supposed to make it safe — that a household is the only thing that can hand its own days back and that nobody can do it for a household — and he was going to use the four days of notice so that the days would be back before anybody had been asked, **which is the same act done faster, by a man who has never done it faster before.** He worked it out alone in a yard at about the fourth hour of the afternoon and the chapter says he worked it out and does not say he was right, **because nobody in the room told him and nobody in the yard offered.** *Open. **A later writer may not have anybody else tell him, may not have him say he was right, may not have him say he was wrong, and may not make the refusal a lesson.***

3. **A BODY IS NOT A PERSON AND THE REACH'S OWN RULE HAS A HOLE EXACTLY THE SHAPE OF A BODY.** The instrument's third condition is that the question goes to everybody who will be in the room, and on the nineteenth day of the Short Month the man who wrote it found out that three of the four places he had put it in are a body, a household and a stall, **and a company that keeps a place has no words of its own, and the eleven words it has are eleven people's, and giving a person a question that belongs to a body is giving the question to nobody.** The rule that has gone from household to household for about four hundred years and has no name **has never had anything to say about a body, and the hole in it is exactly the shape of a body.** *Open. **A later writer may not fill the hole, may not put a company on a posting, may not give a body a voice, and may not name the rule.***

4. **THE CONSENT FRACTURE IS PAID AND IT IS NOT MENDED, AND THERE IS A FIFTH CONDITION.** On the seventeenth day of the Short Month a woman who reads Crown Law said out loud what entering the second way would cost — **a name on a register is a name in a book, a book is kept, a name in a book can be looked up, and there is no office between that table and the place the days are supposed to go, so the person who enters it becomes the name the road is held in, and the third of the four places on the Crown's page is a holder.** The asking was withdrawn at the sixth hour of that morning before the writer set out; no apology was made, none was refused, and no forgiveness was asked for. **She then said there is a fifth condition, that there always was, that the four that are written down are about the question and not one of them is about the man asking it, and that she is not going to hand it to him.** *Open. **A later writer may not have her forgive it, may not have either of them say it was a misunderstanding, may not have the fifth condition given, and may not print the question itself.***

5. **THE CROWN'S SCHEDULE HAS A HEADING, AND A HEADING IS THE STRONGEST THING ON A PAGE.** Said out loud once, in a room in daylight with a date on the door, on the eighteenth day of the Short Month, **a word of two syllables with a hard break in the middle of it, which is the heading on the fifth sheet, the sheet with the four places ruled on it and nothing in them, and the four places are a date, a gate, a holder and the days.** It was entered on a second slate in a room over a market in this city with nothing under it and no name beside it. **A clerk files under a heading, and the day that sheet gets a heading in a building nine hundred miles away, the four places under it stop being a request made by somebody and start being a form somebody expects back.** *Open. **A later writer may not define the word, may not translate it, may not put anything under it, and may not use the word in this world anywhere else.***

6. **A FAVOUR IS A THING THAT STOPS, AND IT HAS NOW STOPPED, AND ABOUT EIGHT PEOPLE PROVED IT BY NOT TURNING UP.** Nine people were paid out of a pocket at four parts in five of the last paying day, the cost was named out loud in a market, and the next morning about eight of the nine did not come to a drain they had turned up for every day since the ninth day of that month. **The reason is that a fifth which is written on nothing can be a fifth which is written on nothing the next time, and nobody did it on purpose and everybody did it, and four of the nine said they would be at the drain and four said a thing that is not a no and four said nothing at all and shut a door.** Nobody went to get anybody. *Open. **A later writer may not repair it by inventing a word, may not convert a figure about a drain into a wage, and may not have anybody say the word that does not exist.***

7. **ABOUT FOUR HUNDRED AND FORTY ARE ON A PAGE AND THE ONLY PAGE IS THE ENEMY'S.** Said out loud on the boards of a salt wharf in this city, in daylight, on the twenty-fourth day of the Short Month, by **the woman of about thirty-four who keeps a stall by a wharf, whose name is on a page in the world and on no page you can read, and whose chapter does not print it.** The counter is **a man of about thirty-eight who keeps a tally on eleven miles of flats and has put the number in a return four times a year since the winter before the water; nobody has ever answered it, nobody has ever come, he does not know what it is for, and he has never been asked.** They are not on the compact, not on the posting, not on the Marches' roll of gates, and not on any instrument in this city, and **about nine of the flats are on a rota with no readable name on it.** *Open. **A later writer may not rescue them, may not turn the counter into a helper, may not say what a wage in salt is worth, and may not put anybody on those flats into a room. The horror of it is on a page in a mouth: a schedule that cannot be filled in by anybody will be filled in from the returns, because a return is a document with a heading and four places and no name in it, and it is the only correct paper anybody in four hundred miles has, and a schedule filled in that way goes out correct and is about the wrong people.***

8. **THE MARCHES KEEP A ROLL OF GATES AND THE CROWN HAS SENT A ROLL OF PEOPLE, AND NOBODY HAS EVER PUT THE TWO SIDE BY SIDE.** Found on the twenty-fifth day of the Short Month, in a room in daylight with a date on the door, by a person reading it out in the local language. **The Marches' roll is old and nobody knows how old: a gate, a keeper, a set of days, line for line, and nothing else, and nothing else on the back of it, and there has never been a column on it for a person for longer than the oldest thing any of the four men who keep it has.** The Crown's roll has a heading and four places. **Both were in one room on the same morning and one page lay face up and one lay face down and nobody put them together, and a keeper and a holder are two words for two different arrangements and the distance between the two arrangements is one man's life and about four hundred miles of road.** The sentence that has been in this book since day 154 — *a description is a removal with a column for where the person is* — was said once in that room, in a mouth, and was not improved on. *Open. **A later writer may not put the two rolls side by side, may not say what the difference is for, may not name what a single version is for, and may not put the nineteen stones and the eight that are different into this room. `outline/volume-06.md` §12 also dates to this volume the asking of a Hearthguard of forty-four about his own eight, and nobody asked him in these ten chapters, and that asking is still owed.***

9. **AND THE THINGS THESE TEN CHAPTERS DID NOT DO, WHICH A LATER WRITER HAS TO KNOW ARE STILL UNTOUCHED.** The ninth line of the posting was not moved, not dated, **not read aloud** and not resolved; the two uses of *the ninth line* in these chapters are Adrian's own comparison to the ninth line on a wall in a yard, on a page from Chapter 0260, and a man of about twenty-six reading the roll on a wall of a room in a market town, which is a different page with nine names on it. The board was not written on and the wall above it was not washed. The bag was not picked up, nobody put a boot on the nine feet, nobody counted the boards under it, and the pan was not replaced, the beam not carried home, the coat not taken off and the pan not turned out. The two holes nine feet apart were not measured, cleared or counted, and the man of about fifty-two who digs is in none of these ten chapters. The two brooms are on their boards. **The keeper of the Redroot gate is not named, not counted, not on a bank and not in a yard in any of the ten files, her days were not counted, her year was not named, and she was not used — and the proof of that is not a string, it is a person-by-person read at `state/batch-summaries/volume-06-batch-0003.md` §6.2.1: no woman in these ten chapters has a stick, a broom, a board on boards, an invert, a day of silence, or a gate.** The man of about thirty-two who stood on a bank on day 245 is in none of these ten chapters and is in no sentence with Adrian Vale and his days are not counted here. The delegation has not left and the word is at zero. The Regent is not named and has not been asked. Jonah Pike is on no page in these ten chapters and is presented as nothing. The girl of seventeen is on a page in one of them, on no roll, unasked, and unresolved. **Nine Elms, the Narrow Mark, chapel, Marrow, Alys, Kern, *invert*, *broom*, *silence*, *knock*, *player*, *Earth*, *System*, *Earthside* and *isekai* are at zero across the ten files — AND *knock* WAS NOT, TWICE, AND THIS LIST WAS PUBLISHED AS A CLEAN ZERO WHILE IT WAS NOT. In the writing pass it stood in Chapter 0277 for a man at four doors and became *spoken at*; in the review repair pass at `state/batch-summaries/volume-06-batch-0003.md` §14.1 it stood a second time in Chapter 0278, in a barrow's wheel against a ridge, and is now *caught*. Every other string in this list was re-run after that edit and is at zero. **The word is at zero from Chapter 0091 on and a single repaired instance is not a proof that a grep was run, which is the general rule at §38.3.** *Open, all of it, and the whole of it is at `state/volume-05-close.md` §3 and §4 and is not restated at less than its length here.*

10. **NOTHING IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS IS A POWER.** The gains are a hand that comes back, a sieve, a withdrawn asking, a fifth condition nobody has, a word with two syllables in it, a body that cannot be asked, four sentences and a fifth, a bowl of coins, eight absences, a barrow on its side, a number said out loud on boards, and a keeper instead of a holder. **None of them is a power. One of them is the price of a power. All of them are heavier than a power.** Adrian Vale performed no working, no threshold was opened, the other world is not named once in the ten chapters, nobody offered him a workway and he asked for none, **no chapter asked him a question about a gate, nobody fetched him and he fetched himself, and his passage privilege is not spent, not threatened and not named** — the words *privilege* and *passage* are at zero across the ten files. *Open.*

11. **THE FIGURES, AND EVERY ONE OF THEM IS A SUBTRACTION FROM A DAY THE THING HAPPENED — AND THE TEN LEADS CARRY NONE OF THEM.** The Short Month day is the day less 255. **THE DEBT RECORDED AT `state/batch-summaries/volume-06-batch-0002.md` §14.1 IS PAID, IN THAT THE TEN LEADS ARE NO LONGER ONE PARAGRAPH WRITTEN TEN TIMES WITH THE NUMERALS CHANGED AND NO TWO OF THEM SHARE A SENTENCE. THE CLAUSE THAT STOOD HERE AND SAID NOT ONE INTERVAL FIGURE APPEARS IN A LEAD PARAGRAPH WAS FALSE, AND §38.2 IS ITS REPAIR: three of the ten leads carry an interval figure, each with its anchor named in the same sentence, which are the seventh day of the nine in Chapter 0271, the case at twenty-one days in Chapter 0272, and forty-nine days of wind in Chapter 0278. The three standing chains of the last twenty days are carried in one room, in one mouth, attached to a decision, on the eighteenth day of the Short Month.** The store's board carries the figures of day 224, so its age is the day less 224 and the value in a mouth on day 273 is **forty-nine**, with the day those figures are of named in the same sentence; the board has stood unwritten the day less 225 and the value in the same mouth is **forty-eight**; the wind has been at that wall since day 229 and the value in the same mouth is **forty-four**, and the anchor is the two hundred and twenty-ninth day of this flood, which is a reference to a day and not a date in the fourth form; the case came up the river on day 251, so its age is the day less 251, and the values in this batch are **twenty-one at day 272 and twenty-two at day 273, both counting from the ninety-sixth day of the Long Month**, and eight to nineteen are on a page. **The posting's age, the trestle count, the unpaid figure, the share with the post taken out, the bag, the delegation's age, the man of about thirty-two's days, the keeper's days and the count of silences are all at zero across the ten chapters.** The count of this city's silences is **one hundred and seventy-two at day 271 and one hundred and eighty-one at day 280**, derived, on no page, in no mouth, and in none of the ten chapters. **A later writer may carry any of these chains if the chapter dates it, and may not attribute a derived value to a chapter or to a mouth, and may not re-state one of the three above unless one of the three changes.**

12. **A WEEKDAY IS A FIGURE, DAY 1 IS A TUESDAY, AND EVERY WEEKDAY IN CHAPTERS 0271 TO 0280 WAS RECOMPUTED FROM DAY 1 AND NOT FROM THE BATCH PROMPT'S TABLE AND NOT FROM A NEIGHBOURING CHAPTER AND NOT FROM A CARD AND NOT FROM A STATE FILE.** Ten chapters, ten days, days 271 to 280: 271 Saturday, 272 Sunday, 273 Monday, 274 Tuesday, 275 Wednesday, 276 Thursday, 277 Friday, 278 Saturday, 279 Sunday, 280 Monday, and the Short Month days sixteen to twenty-five. **Day 270 is a Friday by that arithmetic and is on a page, and therefore day 271 is a Saturday; day 275 is a Wednesday by the same arithmetic and it is also the anchor §33.5 gives, and the two agree.** One hundred and forty-three interior weekday tokens were extracted with their lines and read against the day each names, **and four interval faults were found in the writing pass and repaired: a date in the fourth form in Chapter 0274, an unanchored day count in Chapter 0280, and two places where a figure was measured off a place it does not name.** **Day 280 carries one chapter and there is no double day in this batch and no double day in this volume.**

13. **AND THE FOURTH CONDITION HAS TO BE CHECKED AGAINST THE DAY OF THE ROOM BEFORE THE ROOM IS WRITTEN, BECAUSE IT IS THE ONE INTERVAL NO CHECK OF NUMERALS CAN FIND.** The volume's instrument gives a question four days before the room, and the volume's midpoint is on a Wednesday, **so the notice is on the Saturday four days before it, and three chapters of Chapters 0271 to 0280 turned on that subtraction and one of them is the chapter in which the notice goes out.** **A batch that writes a notice the day before a room has produced a chapter with a three-day instrument in it, and every figure in it can be right and the instrument can be wrong, and that is a check of anchors and not of numerals and it is the general rule at §36.2 applied to a rule rather than to a figure.**

14. **AND THE HOUSE FIGURES FOR THE EXCHANGE, BECAUSE THESE TEN DAYS WERE WRITTEN AGAINST A MEASURED FINDING AND THE MEASUREMENT IS ON THE PAGE.** Across Chapters 0241 to 0250 it is 175 of 176 plain speeches answered in the next line in bold, across all fifty chapters of Volume 05 it is 597 of 629, across Chapters 0251 to 0260 it is 78 of 384, across Chapters 0261 to 0270 it is 49 of 124, **and across Chapters 0271 to 0280 it is 72 of 101, seventy-one per cent, and that is the number a later batch has to beat.** The longest uninterrupted run of speech paragraphs is four in every one of the ten, and two runs were over the ceiling in the writing pass and were broken with a narration beat. The bolded share of words is 9.4 to 21.9 per cent against Volume 01's 10.4 as the model and about 35 as the ceiling, **and the longest marked turn in the ten is seventy-seven and eight turns were over the ceiling of eighty in the writing pass and all eight were cut by marking the finding only and leaving the rest of the speech plain, and no sentence of prose was deleted to make a number.** There are **270 sentences of forty words or more across these ten files on the definition published at `state/batch-summaries/volume-06-batch-0003.md` §8.5.1, and none of them is duplicated, and a later batch must run that check too and must publish the definition with the number. THE 229 THAT STOOD HERE, AND THE 228 IN TWO STATE BLOCKS AND IN THE NEXT PROMPT, WERE FOUR VALUES FOR ONE MEASUREMENT AND NONE OF THE FOUR CAME WITH A DEFINITION; THE REPAIR IS AT §38.4.** **There is no panel in Chapters 0271 to 0280 and there should not be, and the wall said nothing on all ten days, and the one panel of this volume stays where §17.7 put it, on day 256.** **THE COUNTING DEVICE IS PUBLISHED HERE WITH A DEFINITION AND NOT WITH A BAND, because the band this volume's last two records carried could not be reproduced on any definition a later writer would recognise: on a broad count — every *about* before a numeral, a spelled number, a half, an indefinite or *as* — these ten chapters run 1.28 to 2.41 per hundred words and Batch 0002 runs 1.08 to 2.31, and on a narrow count — every *about* before four, nine, six, seven, eight, twenty, thirty, forty, fifty or ninety — these ten run 1.17 to 2.12 and Batch 0002 runs 0.90 to 2.04. A rate is not canon and a batch record's own rate is not a target.**

## 38. Rules established by the review repair pass on Volume 06, Chapters 0271–0280 — AND THE TWO THAT ARE GENERAL

**This section is the review repair pass on the third ten days of this volume, and it is to §36 what §36 is to §35. The record is `state/batch-summaries/volume-06-batch-0003.md` §14, and §14 is the full account including the measurements. §37 is not changed by anything here except in three places where it published a check as passed when it had not been run, and those three places say so in their own words. Three faults were found and repaired and two of the three were in the state layer rather than on the page.**

1. **A COUNT OF DAYS AND AN ORDINAL ARE TWO DIFFERENT FIGURES, AND A CHAPTER MAY NOT PUT ONE IN THE OTHER'S PLACE.** The volume's own rule, at §37.11, is that an age is a subtraction from the day the thing happened, and this volume's hardest fault is a figure measured from a day or a place it is not measured from. **Chapter 0271's lead said a left arm came back on the sixth day of a nine, and the six was a real count — six days out of the clay — belonging to a different interval from the nine, which is the seventh day of it.** The sentence read perfectly, no check of numerals could see it, and a chapter title and a state block carried it after the chapter was closed. **The rule for a later writer: when a figure has two plausible anchors, name both anchors in the same sentence, and if an ordinal and an elapsed count come out of the same page, they are two sentences and not one.**

2. **AND THE DAY BEFORE THE DAY BEFORE.** The nine was said on the ninth day of the Short Month, a Saturday, and the arm went into the clay on the tenth, a Sunday, and both of those are on a page in Chapters 0265 and 0266. **A batch that had taken the ninth for the tenth would have been right about the six and wrong about the nine, and every numeral in the chapter would have checked.** This is §36.2 — a check of anchors and not of numerals — applied to a day instead of to a rule, and it is the same shape as §37.13's four days of notice.

3. **A CHECK THAT RETURNS ZERO IS A CLAIM, AND THIS IS THE SECOND TIME IN ONE VOLUME THAT A WORD WAS ON THE PAGE UNDER A LIST THAT SAID IT WAS NOT.** *knock* stood in Chapter 0277, was repaired, and the repair was then published as the proof that the check worked; *knock* stood a second time in Chapter 0278 and three state sections carried it in a list of zeros. **THE GENERAL RULE, AND IT IS THE ONE THAT COSTS THE MOST: a single repaired instance is not a proof that a grep was run, and a list of zeros is only as good as the script that produced it.** Run the check after the last prose edit and not before it, and if a check is published as clean in more than one file, the cheapest of those files to re-run is the one to re-run.

4. **PUBLISH THE DEFINITION OR PUBLISH NOTHING. THIS IS THE THIRD TIME IN THIS VOLUME AND THE FIGURE IS THE SAME ONE EVERY TIME.** The counting device at `state/batch-summaries/volume-06-batch-0003.md` §7.5, the long-sentence count at its §8.5.1, and the ten-leads claim at §37.11 were three measurements published in four consecutive batches with **no definition that reproduces them**, and the last of the three was not a band at all but a claim that a structural fault was absent. Four files carried 229, 228, 228 and 269 for one count of sentences of forty words or more. **A later writer who inherits a measurement without a definition inherits a number and not a measurement, and will carry it forward with the confidence of somebody who checked.** The definition now published for the long sentence is at §8.5.1 of the batch record and it gives **270 for Chapters 0271 to 0280 and 312 for Chapters 0261 to 0270**, and the median of a chapter's words is the mean of the fifth and sixth of ten sorted, measured with the heading line removed and nothing else.

5. **NOTHING IN THIS PASS CHANGED THE WORLD.** No plot beat, no day, no weekday, no month day, no anchor, no live thread, no cast member, no ending, no card and no outline was edited, and `state/phase-ledger.json` is controller-owned and was not touched. **Two chapters were edited out of ten: one word in Chapter 0278, and four words, one ordinal and one title in Chapter 0271.** The ninth line was not moved, dated, read aloud or resolved; the board was not written on; nobody put a boot on the nine feet and nobody opened the bag; the two rolls were not put side by side; the keeper of the Redroot gate is at zero in every form including her working, and that check is a reading and not a search; the consent fracture is paid and is not mended and the fifth condition is not given.

## 39. Rules established in Volume 06, Chapters 0281–0290 — WHAT THE FOURTH TEN DAYS PUT ON A PAGE

**These are additions to the sections above and not replacements. They are on the page in the fourth ten days of this volume. §33 IS THE CALENDAR AND IS NOT CHANGED, and its fifth form and its weekday anchors stand exactly as the outline phase wrote them. §32 IS THE HOUSE FORMATTING RULE AND IS NOT CHANGED. §34, §35 and §37 are the first three ten days of this volume and are NOT CHANGED and not re-stated at less than their length here. §36 and §38 are review repair passes and their rules bind every chapter written after them and are not changed. §27.14's conflict about the twenty-fourth day of the Long Month is STILL LIVE and is not settled here and no chapter of Chapters 0281 to 0290 attaches a weekday to that day or mentions it at all. §§23 to 31 are not changed. The batch's record is `state/batch-summaries/volume-06-batch-0004.md` and the records of the first three ten days are `state/batch-summaries/volume-06-batch-0001.md`, `volume-06-batch-0002.md` and `volume-06-batch-0003.md`.**

1. **AN OFFICE WHICH GETS A FORM BACK WITH A PLACE LEFT EMPTY SENDS A PERSON AND NOT THE FORM AGAIN, AND THE THING IT SENDS IS A PERSON AND NOT A PAGE.** Said out loud on the twenty-sixth day of the Short Month, in nine sentences, in a room in daylight with a date in chalk on the door, by a man of about forty-one in a very good coat who had said all nine in a room in this city nine days before and watched the first of them lie on a table and not be picked up. **A page can be ignored and a person has to be answered, and to be answered a person has to be spoken to, and a person who is spoken to can say no.** The ninth sentence is that a person who comes up that road with a column in her hand is going to be offered the days, and there is nothing in that column for her to put them in except the third place, and nobody here is going to be in the third place, so she will walk to the next gate, and at the seventh thing she will write, and what she writes will be correct. *Open. **A later writer may not define the third place, may not name the person who has been in the room where it gets decided, may not have the man of forty-one improve on any of the nine sentences, may not have him describe that room, and may not have a document defeated by anything in this volume, because a document that goes back empty is the ordinary end of a document and not a defeat.***

2. **THE INSTRUMENT IS THE ONE BUILT ON THE ONE HUNDRED AND THIRTY-SEVENTH DAY OF THIS FLOOD AND IT HAS FOUR PARTS, AND ONE OF THE FOUR IS A QUESTION WRITTEN IN THE WORDS OF THE PERSON IT IS ABOUT, AND THAT PART CANNOT BE WRITTEN BY A PERSON STANDING IN A ROOM.** The four parts are on a page at `chapter-0137.md:19`. **Nine sheets went out on nine mornings, the twenty-seventh for a room on the thirty-first and the thirty-fifth for a room on the thirty-ninth, and the four days of notice are the instrument and not a courtesy.** *Open. **A later writer may not print the question, may not have a sheet carry a name at the top of it, may not reduce the four days to three, and may not have anybody asked whether the day suits them, because the instrument does not ask and that is why the day 137 asking worked.***

3. **A NOTICE GOES IN A HAND AND NOT IN A MOUTH, AND A HAND IS NOT A PAGE, AND THE PRICE OF NO NAME IS THAT NOBODY CAN DO ANYTHING FOR YOU.** Said in a kitchen and in a market room, in ten days. **A page can be carried to a room and laid on a table and kept; a hand stays in the hand that made it until that person dies, and then it is nobody's, and it is the only document in four hundred miles that cannot be entered in a column, and it also cannot be used to get anybody anything, and both of those are the same fact and not two facts.** And the price, out loud: a sheet with no name at the top of it is worth nothing to a household in trouble, because the first thing a clerk asks is whose it is. *Open. **A later writer may not put a name at the top of any of the nine, may not have a household be rescued by a page, and may not make the price go away.***

4. **A HETHEGUARD OF FORTY-FOUR WAS ASKED ABOUT HIS OWN EIGHT FOR THE SECOND TIME IN A HUNDRED AND FORTY-SIX DAYS, ON A DAY HE DID NOT CHOOSE, AND HE SAID *I KNOW WHICH FOUR*, AND THE CHAPTER SAYS WHICH OF THE TWO ASKING HE IS REPEATING.** Said out loud on the twenty-eighth day of the Short Month, a Thursday, in a room in daylight with a date in the door, by the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register, who named the cost out loud first. **This repeats the being-asked of `chapter-0137.md`, on the one hundred and thirty-seventh day of this flood, on a day he did not choose, where he said no and the no and the asking were both entered; it is NOT the asking-to-be-asked-again of the fiftieth day, and the two are two events and Chapter 0283 does not merge them.** His answer is four words and he will not give a fifth, and the four words are in four mouths and on no page. *Open. **A later writer may not say which four, may not improve on the four words, may not have him give a fifth word in a chapter whose finding is his refusal, and may not have the cost he named in Chapter 0283 be waived.***

5. **A NOTICE GOES TO A PLACE AND NOT TO A HOUSE, AND THE ASKING FAILED ONCE IN NINE DAYS BECAUSE IT WAS A ROAD AND NOT BECAUSE IT WAS A FORM.** Said out loud on the thirty-third day of that month and again on the thirty-fifth. **A place with eight houses in it is a place and a house at the end of a lane past two gates is only a house, and a page can be refused and a person can only be turned out, and being turned out is worse, and that is why a person is the wrong instrument and is also the only one there is.** **AND THE FAILURE BELONGS TO THE NINTH OF THE NINE, WHICH IS THE DAY-SLOT A LATER WRITER NEEDS AND WHICH FIVE STATE FILES AND ONE PROMPT GOT WRONG AT FIRST. The household at the end of the lane is the ninth place; its kitchen day is the thirty-ninth, a Monday; it was asked on the thirty-third, which is SIX days before the day it was to be asked on, by a carter with no sheet; he was not turned out; it answered; and the answer cannot be filed. It is not the fifth of the nine. The fifth of the nine is the thirty-fifth, which is Chapter 0290's own day, and on that day what is on the page is a register with nine days in it and nothing under them and the ninth sheet going out for the thirty-ninth, and nothing says whether a household was asked on it.** *Open. **A later writer may not turn the failure into anybody's fault, may not repair it with a form, may not count the failures, which belongs to Chapter 0293, and may not reattach the failure to the fifth of the nine.***

6. **A PERSON ARRIVED, AND A PERSON IS ON A PAGE, AND NOBODY WAS ENTERED IN A COLUMN.** **Ilva Rooke, of about forty-five, gave her own name and her own date in daylight at a door on the thirty-fourth day of the Short Month and said that day was the day she had chosen, and stood at that door for about four hours with a shut case, and was let into a kitchen where a household was being asked, and heard the household say yes, and went away with two of the four places filled in.** *Open. **A later writer may not have her name the figure on a return, may not have her defeated, may not convert her into a threat with a face, and may not have anybody entered in a column because of her.***

7. **A PERSON NOBODY ASKS ANYTHING IS A PERSON WHO CANNOT BE ENTERED IN A COLUMN, AND THE TWO PEOPLE WHO ARE NEVER ASKED ANYTHING IN NINE ROOMS ARE THE TWO WHO ARE DOING THE ASKING.** Said out loud on the thirty-first day of that month, in a kitchen, by the man who carries the question. *Open. **A later writer may not have anybody in this volume ask Adrian Vale what the asking is for, and may not make being never asked a courtesy.***

8. **OF THE FOUR PLACES ON THE CROWN'S PAGE THE DATE IS THE ONLY ONE THAT WILL TAKE WHAT IS COMING BACK, AND A REGISTER WITH NINE DAYS IN IT AND NOTHING UNDER THEM IS THE ONLY INSTRUMENT ANYBODY IN THIS CITY HAS THAT AN OFFICE NINE HUNDRED MILES OFF CANNOT ASK TO HAVE PRODUCED.** Said out loud on the thirty-fifth day of that month, in a room with a date on the door, by the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register, who named the cost first, **because a day is the only one of the four that everybody would remember differently.** *Open. **A later writer may not define the other three places, may not put an answer under a date on that register, and may not have the register asked for from nine hundred miles off.***

9. **A DOCUMENT AND A DRAIN ARE THE SAME THING, AND NEITHER OF THEM IS ANYBODY'S FAULT, AND NOBODY SHUT THE ROAD.** Said out loud in a market out past the last named house on the twenty-ninth day of that month, and about four people in that lane worked the same thing out from a drain and said so in a mouth. **A piece of paper and four feet of silt went out of the same building on the same road inside about an hour of each other and neither of them knew about the other.** *Open. **A later writer may not convert a figure about a drain into a wage, and may not say what a wage in salt is worth.***

10. **AND THE THINGS THESE TEN CHAPTERS DID NOT DO, WHICH A LATER WRITER HAS TO KNOW ARE STILL UNTOUCHED.** The ninth line of the posting was not moved, not dated, **not read aloud** and not resolved, and the one reference to it in these ten chapters is in a market room and is about two refusals of the same kind, and the man who wrote it keeps his five refusals. The board was not written on and the wall above it was not washed. The bag was not picked up, nobody put a boot on the nine feet, nobody counted the boards under it, nobody put a day in them, nobody opened it, **no chapter in these ten says what a wage in salt is worth, and Break is not open**, and the pan is not replaced, the beam not carried home, the coat not taken off and the pan not turned out. The two holes nine feet apart were not measured, cleared or counted, and the man of about fifty-two who digs is in none of these ten chapters. The two brooms are on their boards. **The keeper of the Redroot gate is not named, not counted, not on a bank and not in a yard in any of the ten files, her days were not counted, her year was not named, and she was not used — and the proof of that is not a string, it is a person-by-person read at `state/batch-summaries/volume-06-batch-0004.md` §6.2.1: no woman in these ten chapters has a stick, a board on boards, an invert, a day of silence, or a gate.** The man of about thirty-two who stood on a bank on day 245 is in none of these ten chapters and is in no sentence with Adrian Vale. The delegation has not left and the word is at zero. Jonah Pike is on no page in these ten chapters and is presented as nothing, and his absence is not a departure and is not a resolution. The girl of seventeen copied nine sheets and was asked nothing and is on no roll, unasked, and unresolved. Nine Elms, the Narrow Mark, chapel, Marrow, Alys, Kern, *invert*, *broom*, *silence*, *knock*, *player*, *Earth*, *System*, *Earthside* and *isekai* are at zero across the ten files — **and *knock* was lost twice in the writing pass, once in Chapter 0285 and once in Chapter 0284, and *days on it* was lost three times in Chapter 0290, and the date form *of this month* was lost once in Chapter 0286 and once in Chapter 0287, and all five are recorded at the batch record's §8.4 because §38.3 is the rule and a single repaired instance is not a proof that a grep was run. IT WAS LOST A FIFTH TIME AFTER ALL OF THAT, IN CHAPTER 0285, IN THE REVIEW REPAIR PASS, AND THE REASON IS THE SEARCH AND NOT THE PROSE: §8.4's script used word boundaries, and a word boundary does not match an inflection, so `knock` was reported at zero while the page carried `knocking` in the same paragraph as a `knock` the writer had already repaired. A stem search, not a word-boundary search, is what this volume's own rule requires, and the second instance of that clause is gone.**

11. **NOTHING IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS IS A POWER.** The gains are a shut case, nine unsigned sheets, a four-word answer, a drain and a page found to be one thing, a yoke standing against a door, a day that was not on anybody's list, a hand that is not a page, a thumbprint of tar on a sill, and a person waiting in the cold because a person cannot be ignored. **None of them is a power. One of them is the price of a power. All of them are heavier than a power.** Adrian Vale performed no working, no threshold was opened, the other world is not named once in the ten chapters, nobody offered him a workway and he asked for none, **no chapter asked him a question about a gate, nobody fetched him and he fetched himself, and his passage privilege is not spent, not threatened and not named** — the words *privilege* and *passage* are at zero across the ten files. *Open.*

12. **THE FIGURES, AND EVERY ONE OF THEM IS A SUBTRACTION FROM A DAY THE THING HAPPENED, AND ALL TWELVE OF THEM ARE AT ZERO ACROSS THE TEN CHAPTERS.** The Short Month day is the day less 255. The posting went up on day 201, so its age is the day less 201 and it is **eighty at day 281 and eighty-nine at day 290**; the store's board carries the figures of day 224, so their age is the day less 224 and is fifty-seven and sixty-six, and the board has stood unwritten the day less 225, which is fifty-six and sixty-five; the wind at that wall is the day less 229, which is fifty-two and sixty-one, **and the wind is named without a figure in the first paragraph of Chapter 0284 because `outline/volume-06.md` §15 requires a physical-work chapter to state it, and a named wind is not a figure and the anchor is not repeated**; the trestle count is the day less forty and is two hundred and forty-one and two hundred and fifty; the case came up the river on day 251, so its age is the day less 251 and is thirty and thirty-nine; the thirty-nine men under the near gate are the day less eighty-five and are a hundred and ninety-six and two hundred and five; a share with the post taken out was given on day 168, so its age is the day less 168 and is **a hundred and thirteen and a hundred and twenty-two**; the bag went down on day 240, so its age is forty-one and fifty; the man of about thirty-two on the bank is the day less 245 and is thirty-six and forty-five and is in none of the ten chapters; the count of this city's silences is **one hundred and eighty-two at day 281 and one hundred and ninety-one at day 290**, derived, on no page, in no mouth, **and in none of the ten chapters, and the wall said nothing on all ten days and the batch spends no panel.** **THE TWENTY-FOURTH, TWENTY-FIFTH, TWENTY-SIXTH, THIRTY-SIXTH, THIRTY-EIGHTH AND FORTIETH DAYS OF THE SHORT MONTH ARE AT ZERO ACROSS THE TEN FILES. The ones on the page are the ninth, the tenth, the twentieth, the twenty-third, the twenty-seventh, the twenty-eighth, the twenty-ninth, the thirtieth, the thirty-first, the thirty-second, the twenty-third, the thirty-third, the thirty-fourth and the thirty-ninth; the thirty-first, the thirty-third and the thirty-ninth are days of the asking and not interval chains, and the rest are a chapter's own day, a notice day four days before one, or the tenth of that month, which is the day the man of about forty-three's arm went into the clay on a page at Chapter 0265.** The only *Nth day of the Long Month* in the ten is the ninety-sixth, which is a reference to a day that is on a page at Chapter 0251 and **is not a date in the fourth form.** *Open.*

13. **A WEEKDAY IS A FIGURE, DAY 1 IS A TUESDAY, AND EVERY WEEKDAY IN CHAPTERS 0281 TO 0290 WAS RECOMPUTED FROM DAY 1 AND NOT FROM THE BATCH PROMPT'S TABLE AND NOT FROM A NEIGHBOURING CHAPTER AND NOT FROM A CARD AND NOT FROM A STATE FILE.** Ten chapters, ten days, days 281 to 290: 281 Tuesday, 282 Wednesday, 283 Thursday, 284 Friday, 285 Saturday, 286 Sunday, 287 Monday, 288 Tuesday, 289 Wednesday, 290 Thursday, and the Short Month days twenty-six to thirty-five. **Day 280 is a Monday on the page of Chapter 0280 and by the founding rule, and therefore day 281 is a Tuesday; the last of the ten days is a THURSDAY and not a Tuesday, and the batch prompt for this batch said Tuesday in one sentence and Tuesday in its own table, and the table is right and the sentence was wrong, and `outline/volume-06.md` §14.2 and `bible/power-system.md` §33.5 and the page all agree against the sentence.** Sixty-one interior weekday tokens were extracted with their lines and read against the day each names, **and the batch record printed two different figures for this one measurement, seventy-one and fifty-eight, and the review's independent count and this pass's differ by one; the definition is every occurrence of the seven weekday words in the ten files and it is published at the batch record's §8.6 item 2, and both earlier figures are withdrawn rather than reconciled,** **and six faults were found and repaired, and all six are of one family and none of them is a numeral: a recurring weekday standing next to an undated event, in Chapters 0284, 0285, 0288 and 0290, and the repair in every case was to carry the fact by an ordinal day of the Short Month that is on a page.** **Day 290 carries one chapter and there is no double day in this batch and there is no double day in this volume.**

14. **AND THE FOUR DAYS OF NOTICE HAVE TO BE CHECKED AGAINST THE DAY OF THE ROOM BEFORE THE ROOM IS WRITTEN, AND IN THIS BATCH FOUR OF THE FIVE KITCHEN DAYS NAME THE MORNING THEIR NOTICE WENT OUT.** The room on the thirty-first had its notice on the twenty-seventh; the thirty-second on the twenty-eighth; the thirty-third on the twenty-ninth; the thirty-fourth on the thirtieth; **and the thirty-first, which is the notice the room on the thirty-fifth would have needed, is at zero across the ten files, so the fifth kitchen's notice is on no page in its own chapter. THE FINDING OF THE BATCH IS NOT THAT FIFTH KITCHEN. It is the ninth place, asked on the thirty-third, six days before the thirty-ninth, with no sheet and so with none of the four days behind it, and it is at item 5 above. THIS ITEM PREVIOUSLY SAID THE THIRTY-FIFTH HAD NO NOTICE AT ALL AND NAMED THAT AS THE FINDING, AND IT WAS WRONG ON BOTH COUNTS, and the error was corrected at the batch record's §15.6.** The ninth sheet went out on the thirty-fifth for a room on the thirty-ninth, which is four days off and a Monday. **A check of numerals cannot find this and a batch that had written a notice on the day before a room would have produced three chapters with every figure in them correct and an instrument in all three that does not exist.** *Open, and this is the general rule at §36.2 applied to a rule and not to a figure.*

15. **AND THE HOUSE FIGURES FOR THE EXCHANGE.** Across Chapters 0241 to 0250 it is 175 of 176 plain speeches answered in the next line in bold, across all fifty chapters of Volume 05 it is 597 of 629, across Chapters 0251 to 0260 it is 78 of 384, across Chapters 0261 to 0270 it is 49 of 124, across Chapters 0271 to 0280 it is 72 of 101, **and across Chapters 0281 to 0290 it is 64 of 85, seventy-five per cent, and that is the number a later batch has to beat, and the three lowest chapters in this batch are a drain, a room in which a man answers a question with a physical fact, and the register-keeper's room, which is the same shape Batch 0003 found at seventy-one.** The longest uninterrupted run of speech paragraphs is four in nine of the ten and three in one. The bolded share of words is 8.0 to 20.8 per cent against Volume 01's 10.4 as the model and about 35 as the ceiling, **in eighty-nine spans with a mean of thirty-eight words and a longest of seventy-nine, and the pre-repair figures were 11.3 to 22.5 per cent in ninety spans with a mean of forty-six, and the fall is the chorus frame leaving the marked turns and is not an achievement and is not offered as one — see the batch record's §15.2,** and eleven turns were over eighty in the writing pass and all eleven were cut by marking the finding only and leaving the rest of the speech plain, and no sentence of prose was deleted to make a number.** **In the three kitchens of Chapters 0286 to 0288 the marked voice is the household and not the asker, so that the bold in a kitchen belongs to the person being asked, and this is a decision a later batch may make or may not and may not arrive at by accident.** There are **276 sentences of forty words or more across these ten files on the definition published at `state/batch-summaries/volume-06-batch-0004.md` §8.5.1, and none of them is duplicated, and the same definition gives none duplicated across all forty chapters of Volume 06, which is 1,202 of them. The pre-repair figures on that same definition were 267 and 1,193; this item printed 1,192, which was one out, and the count rose because the repair pass took a clause out of the middle of a hundred and forty-nine sentences and joined two clauses into one.** **There is no panel in Chapters 0281 to 0290 and there should not be, and the wall said nothing on all ten days, and the one panel of this volume stays where §17.7 put it, on day 256.** **THE COUNTING DEVICE IS PUBLISHED WITH ITS DEFINITION AND NOT WITH A BAND: on the broad count — every *about* before a numeral, a spelled number, a half, an indefinite or *as* — these ten files run 1.02 to 1.78 per hundred words and on the narrow count 0.83 to 1.54, against Batch 0003's 1.28 to 2.41 and 1.17 to 2.12, and the fall from this batch's own pre-repair 1.84 to 2.26 is the chorus frame and not a thinning of the world. A rate is not canon and a batch record's own rate is not a target, and the frame is load-bearing at half the density and this volume had it at full and should not have.**

---

## 40. Rules established in Volume 06, Chapters 0291–0300 — WHAT THE LAST TEN DAYS OF THE VOLUME PUT ON A PAGE

**These are additions to the sections above and not replacements. They are on the page in the last ten days of this volume, which is the whole of it. §33 IS THE CALENDAR AND IS NOT CHANGED, and its fifth form, its weekday anchors and its interval table stand exactly as the outline phase wrote them. §32 IS THE HOUSE FORMATTING RULE AND IS NOT CHANGED. §34, §35, §37 and §39 are the first four ten days of this volume and are NOT CHANGED and not re-stated at less than their length here. §36, §38 and §39.15 are review repair passes and general rules and their rules bind every chapter written after them and are not changed. §27.14's conflict about the twenty-fourth day of the Long Month is STILL LIVE and is not settled here and no chapter of Chapters 0291 to 0300 attaches a weekday to that day or mentions it at all. §§23 to 31 are not changed. The batch's record is `state/batch-summaries/volume-06-batch-0005.md`, the volume's close record is `state/volume-06-close.md`, and the records of the first four ten days are `state/batch-summaries/volume-06-batch-0001.md` through `volume-06-batch-0004.md`.**

1. **NINE HOUSEHOLDS, AND THE DAYS WENT BACK, AND THE ARITHMETIC OF THE ASKING DOES NOT COME OUT AND THE VOLUME SOLVED IT IN PROSE.** Nine rooms on nine days, days 286 to 294, and **nine households behind them, and for nine days the two numbers were the same and they stopped being the same on the morning of the thirty-eighth day of the Short Month.** Four of the nine had four days of notice and four of them said yes in their own words. **Five did not: two of them are the two households of one place on the thirty-sixth, where the sheet went to a place and four days could not be divided between two households; one had two days on the thirty-seventh; one had one day on the thirty-eighth; and one is the house at the end of a lane past two gates, which had a sheet four days before its day and had already answered six days before that out of a mouth with nothing in a man's hand.** The count of five is said out loud once in a kitchen on the thirty-eighth and is entered on nothing. *Open. **A later writer may not turn the two households of the thirty-sixth into a queue, may not repair the difference between four days and two with a form, may not make the count a fault, may not make the four a happy number, and may not say whether a household was asked on the thirty-fifth of the Short Month, which is a day with a room set for it and no household that anybody in four hundred miles can point at.***

2. **THE DAYS WENT BACK ON THE THIRTY-NINTH DAY OF THE SHORT MONTH, A MONDAY, IN DAYLIGHT, OUT OF A DOOR, IN FOUR SENTENCES, AND THE ROAD NORTH OF THE MARCHES CLOSED THAT NIGHT AND NOBODY SHUT IT.** Said at about the seventh hour by a man of about fifty-eight at a house at the end of a lane past two gates: that there are days on this lane and they are theirs, that they have been theirs since before the water, that this morning they are taking them off, and that they are not doing it for anybody and are not saying it to anybody. **About four other houses on that lane did the same thing in about the next hour, one after another, and nobody collected it, nobody wrote it down, and nobody was in a position to ask him for it.** The third of the four places on the Crown's page is still empty. A boy of about ten with a plain slate and a lump of chalk is the only person in four hundred miles with a record of the thirty-ninth and nobody is going to correct him. *Open. **A later writer may not make it a petition, may not name a holder, may not have the schedule withdrawn, may not open the case, and may not have the Regent defeated, exposed or converted. The man who carried the question into nine rooms was not in the room when the last of the nine said yes, he said so before anything happened, and he was told afterwards by a person who was in it.***

3. **A SCHEDULE OF A THING NOBODY IS HOLDING IS A SCHEDULE OF A THING NOBODY CAN BE SHOWN HAS GONE, AND A COLUMN OF DAYS BELONGS TO A HOUSEHOLD, AND A PERSON IS THE ONLY INSTRUMENT ANYBODY HAS THAT CAN BE SHOWN TO HAVE GONE.** Said out loud in a room in daylight at a shut gate on the fortieth day of the Short Month, and **a keeper who was not asked about it says she would not have wanted to be.** *Open.*

4. **A REGIONAL ANSWER, CORRECT, DATED AT AN OFFICE NINE HUNDRED MILES AWAY FOR THE FORTY-SECOND, AND NOT SIGNED BY ANYBODY IN THIS CITY. ITS ONE SENTENCE: NO HOLDING COMPANY WILL BE SENT WEST OF THE MARCHES GATE BEFORE THE SEASON TURNS. IT COSTS THE MARCHES THE WINTER ROAD AND IT COSTS THE CROWN NOTHING, AND BOTH OF THOSE ARE ON THE SAME SHEET IN THE SAME HAND, AND NEITHER OF THEM IS A JUDGMENT.** Read out in a room over a market in daylight with a date on the door, and entered on a second slate with a date against it and nothing under the date. **A woman nine hundred miles off is not defeated, not exposed and has not changed her mind, and she read the same correct numbers and came to a different conclusion, and her conclusion is not a stupid one, and her name is at zero across the last ten chapters.** *Open. **A later writer may not name the figure on her return, may not put the days on a gate into her mouth without a room in daylight with a date on it, and may not have a document defeated by anything in this volume.***

5. **A RAFT IS ADMINISTERING THE CEASEFIRE, AND IT IS A RAFT.** Three boards and a beam and about nine feet of nothing under it, put together by four men with nothing else to do with a week in the first week of the second month of this flood, poled to nine places on nine days by a man of about twenty-six and a woman of about fifty-nine, with a household heard at each. **Chapter 0297 is the only one of the nine days that carries a day, which is the forty-second, and the other eight are not written down and are not going to be. The instrument is about four hundred years old and has no name and may not be given one and may not be replaced by anything a document can do, and a household being heard and saying nothing are two different things.** *Open. **A later writer may not call it a treaty, may not call the raft a second asking, may not call the asking a second raft, may not put the raft's days on the asking's days, and may not give the other eight a date.***

6. **THE BRIDGE IS A STORE AND A QUEUE, AND A QUEUE IS A LINE NOBODY DECIDED, AND THE WHOLE OF WHAT IS LEFT AFTER A DOCUMENT IS A THING THAT IS NOT A DOCUMENT.** Said in a room over a market in daylight with a date on the door on the forty-third day of the Short Month, and **the man who wrote the ninth line asked in advance not to be thanked, asked again, and was not thanked, and gave no reason for the sixth time, and a piece of chalk was put on a table that nobody is going to use.** *Open. **A later writer may not move the ninth line, date it, read it aloud, resolve whether it works, thank him, or improve on the reason he gave the fourth time, which is on a page. And the two rolls of the same gates may not be put side by side, and nobody may say what a single version is for.***

7. **THE WORD *HOLDER* IS USED AND IT IS NOT DEFINED, AND A MAN IN A ROOM SAYS OUT LOUD THAT HE IS NOT GOING TO DEFINE IT.** Two uses in Chapters 0295 and 0300, both the volume's own contrast out of Chapter 0280 — *a key is not a figure and a keeper is not a holder* — and **Chapter 0298 has a man in a room with a piece of chalk say in his own words that the word is on a page in this city and on a page in the Marches, that nobody has ever defined it, and that he is not going to define it and would like it struck out of any list anybody is keeping of the things he has said.** *Open. **A later writer may not define it, and the prohibition is now on a page in a mouth as well as in this file.***

8. **A STRAIGHT LINE IN THE GROUND UNDER ABOUT NINE HUNDRED PACES OF ORCHARD, AND NOBODY MEASURED IT AND NOBODY DUG IT, AND THERE IS NO WORD FOR IT IN THIS MANUSCRIPT.** About a foot wide and about two inches deep, from the near end of the rows to a stone that nobody put there, and **it is not a path and not a watercourse and not a furrow and not a boundary, and it does not follow a row of anything, and the ground either side of it is as uneven as the ground a yard off.** Seen on the forty-fourth day of the Short Month from a gate by a person doing her work, on a day a keeper would not remember, because she had not been counting days and says so. **She does not go and look at it, she told one man with a barrow and nobody else, and she refuses to tell the register-keeper because she would have to write a woman's name against a thing she has not looked at. The man of about fifty-two who digs on the flats about eleven miles the other way was not sent for and would have refused in the time it takes to shut a gate and would have been right, and there is nothing at that gate this month that needs a man to say yes.** *Open. **A later writer may not measure it, dig it, name it, put a rod in it, resolve it, connect it to the chapel or to the boards under it or to the two holes nine feet apart, or improve on the three sentences this manuscript has already used about a scar — the two at `chapters/volume-01/chapter-0037.md:131` and `:235`, and the panel *a man is a scar in a person* at `chapters/volume-01/chapter-0041.md:45`, the last of which is the one most likely to reach a mouth. **THE PROHIBITION ON NAMING IT IS LIFTED BY THE VOLUME 07 DECISION OF RECORD IN `outline/series.md`, and by nothing else and by no batch.** It is not the chapel's and it is not the boards and it is not the second hole, and Chapter 0299 does not say so and does not need to.***

9. **A WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY KEEPS A GATE ON A ROAD THAT COMES DOWN FROM THE NORTH SIDE OF THE MARCHES, AND SHE IS NEVER NAMED ON A PAGE AND GIVES NO NAME, AND HER SECOND HANDLE IS A BRASS KEY ON A TARRED CORD, AND IT IS THE SAME IN ALL THREE CHAPTERS SHE IS IN.** She is on three pages — 0295, 0299, 0300 — and her name is on none of them and no chapter of the volume supplies one, and `outline/volume-06.md` §6.4's rule that no name and no place arrives by narration is binding on any later writer who wants to call her something. A bar of ash run in two brackets set in the stone when the second gate went up that road, a wooden hook, a piece of chalk, a date in chalk on the post every morning with nothing rubbed out, and **a brass key on a tarred cord about a foot long, in the apron pocket, and that is the whole of what she owns that anybody has written down.** *Open. **THE PHYSICAL CHECK ON HER IS A READING AND NOT A SEARCH, AND IT IS THE GENERAL RULE FOR EVERY GATEKEEPER A LATER WRITER PUTS IN A CHAPTER: list what she holds, what she works on, what her room contains, where her light goes and how long she is quiet, and compare. She has no stick, no broom, no board on boards, no invert, no cut and no day of silence, her light comes over a shoulder of a hill and does not reach the gate, and she talks in every chapter she is in. A search for the name of a gatekeeper is not this check and has never once been this check. The one thing this record had wrong and the review repair pass at `state/batch-summaries/volume-06-batch-0005.md` §16 caught is that the check was used to certify a name and a ring of chalk on the inside of a door, neither of which is on a page; a check is a check and it does not manufacture the thing it checked.***

10. **AND THE THINGS THESE TEN CHAPTERS DID NOT DO, WHICH A LATER WRITER HAS TO KNOW ARE STILL UNTOUCHED.** The ninth line was not moved, not dated, **not read aloud** and not resolved. The board was not written on and the wall was not washed. The bag was not picked up, nobody put a boot on the nine feet, nobody counted the boards under it, nobody put a day in them, nobody opened it, **no chapter in these ten days says what a wage in salt is worth, and Break is not open**, and the pan is not replaced, the beam not carried home, the coat not taken off and the pan not turned out. The two holes nine feet apart were not measured, cleared or counted, and the man of about fifty-two who digs is named once as a man who was not sent for and does not appear. The two brooms are on their boards. **The keeper of the Redroot gate is not named, not counted, not on a bank and not in a yard in any of the ten files, her days were not counted, her year was not named, and she was not used — and the proof of that is not a string, it is a person-by-person read at `state/batch-summaries/volume-06-batch-0005.md` §6.2.1, and that read found one woman at a gate in the ten chapters and she is a woman of about fifty who gives no name, of §9 above, and the two share no tool, no material, no room, no silence and no light.** The man of about thirty-two who stood on a bank on day 245 is in none of the ten chapters and is in no sentence with Adrian Vale, and **his days are at zero in all ten chapters and in no chapter of the volume is any figure for them printed.** The delegation has not left and the word is at zero. Jonah Pike is on no page in these ten chapters and is presented as nothing, and his absence is not a departure and is not a resolution. The girl of seventeen is on no page in these ten chapters and is on no roll and is unresolved. Tamsin Quill is at zero and is not in one room with the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register. The Regent is not named. Nine Elms, the Narrow Mark, chapel, Marrow, Alys, Kern, *invert*, *broom*, *silence*, *stick*, *knock*, *player*, *Earth*, *System*, *Earthside* and *isekai* are at zero across the ten files, and **`knock` was at zero in the writing pass and on a stem search and not a word-boundary search, and *days on it* was on the page in Chapter 0294 and is at zero, and *of this month* was on the page in Chapter 0300 and is at zero, and *Earth* was on the page in Chapter 0291 as the ordinary English noun and is at zero.** *Open, all of it, and the whole of it is at `state/volume-06-close.md` §4 and is not restated at less than its length here.*

11. **NOTHING IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS IS A POWER.** The gains are two households in one place, a hinge, a count of five, a shut road, a bar of ash in a bracket, a brass key on a cord, one sentence that costs one side everything and the other side nothing, a raft, a piece of chalk nobody uses, and a line in the ground nobody measured. **None of them is a power. One of them is the price of a power. All of them are heavier than a power.** Adrian Vale performed no working, no threshold was opened, the other world is not named once in the ten chapters, nobody offered him a workway and he asked for none, **no chapter asked him a question about a gate, nobody fetched him, he is on five of the ten pages and named in none of them, and his passage privilege is not spent, not threatened and not named** — the words *privilege* and *passage* are at zero across the ten files. *Open.*

12. **THE FIGURES, AND EVERY ONE OF THEM IS A SUBTRACTION FROM A DAY THE THING HAPPENED, AND ALL FOURTEEN OF THEM ARE AT ZERO ACROSS THE TEN CHAPTERS.** The Short Month day is the day less 255. The posting went up on day 201, so its age is the day less 201 and it is **ninety at day 291 and ninety-nine at day 300**; the store's board carries the figures of day 224, so their age is the day less 224 and is sixty-seven and seventy-six, and the board has stood unwritten the day less 225, which is sixty-six and seventy-five; the wind at that wall is the day less 229, which is sixty-two and seventy-one, **and the wind is at zero as a word in all ten chapters because none of them is a physical-work chapter and `outline/volume-06.md` §15's requirement that such a chapter state it does not apply**; the trestle count is the day less forty and is two hundred and fifty-one and two hundred and sixty; the case came up the river on day 251, so its age on a table is the day less 251 and is forty and forty-nine; the thirty-nine men under the near gate are the day less eighty-five and are two hundred and six and two hundred and fifteen; a share with the post taken out was given on day 168, so its age is the day less 168 and is **a hundred and twenty-three and a hundred and thirty-two**; the bag went down on day 240, so its age is fifty-one and sixty; the delegation is the day less 145 and is a hundred and forty-six and a hundred and fifty-five; the keeper of the Redroot gate is the day less 179 and is a hundred and twelve and a hundred and twenty-one and **is at zero in all ten files and in all fifty**; the man of about thirty-two on the bank is the day less 245 and is forty-six and fifty-five and is in none of the ten chapters; the flood's own inclusive length is a day of this flood. The count of this city's silences is **one hundred and ninety-two at day 291 and two hundred and one at day 300**, derived, on no page, in no mouth, **and in none of the ten chapters, and the wall said nothing on all ten days and the batch spends no panel.**

13. **AND THE ONE DERIVED FIGURE IN THIS BATCH THAT COULD NOT BE GOT BY ADDING, WHICH IS THE COUNT OF SILENCES, AND IT IS PUBLISHED HERE BECAUSE A LATER WRITER WILL ADD IT WRONG.** The rule is one addition for every day the wall says nothing. **THE WALL SAID SOMETHING ON DAY 256, WHICH SPENT THE VOLUME'S ONE PANEL, AND THAT DAY IS THEREFORE NOT A SILENCE, AND A WRITER WHO ADDS THE ELAPSED DAYS WITHOUT TAKING THE TWENTY-FIFTH DAY OF THAT MONTH OUT IS ONE OUT AT DAY 300.** One hundred and fifty-two is on a page at `chapter-0250.md` and is the figure at day 250. One hundred and ninety-one at day 290 is that base plus forty less one, and it is the figure Batch 0004 published and it is correct. **Two hundred and one at day 300 is the same base plus fifty less one, and `outline/volume-06.md` §14.3 gives two hundred and one independently, and the two agree. The figure is for the state layer and is not for a chapter, and no chapter of this volume prints it.**

14. **A WEEKDAY IS A FIGURE, DAY 1 IS A TUESDAY, AND EVERY WEEKDAY IN CHAPTERS 0291 TO 0300 WAS RECOMPUTED FROM DAY 1 AND NOT FROM THE BATCH PROMPT'S TABLE AND NOT FROM A NEIGHBOURING CHAPTER AND NOT FROM A CARD AND NOT FROM A STATE FILE.** Ten chapters, ten days, days 291 to 300: 291 Friday, 292 Saturday, 293 Sunday, 294 Monday, 295 Tuesday, 296 Wednesday, 297 Thursday, 298 Friday, 299 Saturday, 300 Sunday, and the Short Month days thirty-six to forty-five. **Day 290 is a Thursday on the page of Chapter 0290 and by the founding rule, and therefore day 291 is a Friday, and the last of the ten is a Sunday and the forty-fifth day of the Short Month.** Sixty-two interior weekday tokens were extracted with their contexts and read against the day each names, **and eleven faults were found and repaired, and all eleven are of one family and none of them is a numeral: a recurring weekday standing next to an undated event, and the repair in every case was to carry the fact on an ordinal day of the Short Month that is on a page.** **A further two faults were found by two other checks and are of the same family: the date form *of this month* and the ordinary noun *Earth*, and the string *days on it*, which is the Chapter 0154 sentence's own words.** The ten files now carry fifty-two weekday tokens and all of them are correct. **Day 300 carries one chapter and there is no double day in this batch and there is no double day in this volume.**

15. **AND THE FOUR DAYS OF NOTICE, WHICH ARE THE INSTRUMENT, AND WHICH THE PROMPT FOR THIS BATCH NAMED AS THE THING A CHECK OF NUMERALS CANNOT SEE, AND IT WAS RIGHT.** Room on the thirty-sixth had its notice on the thirty-second; the thirty-seventh on the thirty-third; the thirty-eighth on the thirty-fourth; the thirty-ninth on the thirty-fifth. **All four are four days and all four are named inside the chapter they belong to.** The first two of those notice days are on a page in Chapters 0287 and 0288, the third in Chapter 0289, and the fourth is the ninth sheet in Chapter 0290. **A notice on any other day is a three-day instrument, and a three-day instrument does not fail, it arrives warm, and a batch that has written one has produced a chapter with every figure in it correct and an instrument in it that does not exist.** *Open, and this is §36.2 applied to a rule and not to a figure, and it is now the fourth batch in a row to pay for it.*

16. **AND THE HOUSE FIGURES FOR THE EXCHANGE, AND TWO OF THEM ARE A FINDING AND NOT A NUMBER.** Across Chapters 0241 to 0250 it is 175 of 176 plain speeches answered in the next line in bold, across all fifty chapters of Volume 05 it is 597 of 629, across Chapters 0251 to 0260 it is 78 of 384, across 0261 to 0270 it is 49 of 124, across 0271 to 0280 it is 72 of 101, across 0281 to 0290 it is 64 of 85, **and across Chapters 0291 to 0300 it is 53 of 104, fifty point nine per cent, and THAT IS THE CORRECTED FIGURE AND THE FIFTY-NINE THAT STOOD HERE UNTIL THE VOLUME 07 OUTLINE PHASE'S REVIEW REPAIR IS WITHDRAWN. 152 + 51 − 2 = 250 AND NOT 249 is the same species of error and it is the same author's: a derived figure one out at the far boundary, on a denominator that reproduces exactly, with the numerator that does not. The denominator reproduces under all three paragraph treatments and the numerator does not reproduce under any of them. A later phase reading this section gets fifty-three. AND IT IS STILL LOWER THAN THE FIGURES ABOVE IT AND THE REASON IS NOT THAT THE CHAPTERS ARE VAGUER:** the marked voice in a kitchen is the household and not the asker, and in Chapter 0294 the marked voice is the household and the asker is not in the room at all, and the two lowest chapters are the climax and the last day, in both of which the thing that is answered is answered by a person acting rather than by a person talking. A later writer that wants this number higher has to decide whether it wants the argument louder or the people moving, and those are not the same want.** The longest uninterrupted run of speech paragraphs is four in eight of the ten and three in two, **which is the ceiling in `outline/volume-06.md` §17.6, and seven runs were over it in the writing pass and all seven were broken by inserting a narration beat.** The bolded share of words is **7.8 to 18.0 per cent against Volume 01's 10.4 as the model and about 35 as the ceiling, in sixty-nine spans with a mean of forty-five words and a longest of seventy-four, and the three chapters at the bottom are the climax, the answer and the last day, which is not an accident and is not defended: a chapter in which nobody is arguing is a chapter in which nobody is shouting.** **The marked voice is the household in the three kitchens of Chapters 0291 to 0293 and in the climax of Chapter 0294, and it is the asker in exactly one place in the ten chapters, in Chapter 0297 on the raft, where there is no household in the room because the household is on a bank, and a later batch may make that decision or may not and may not arrive at it by accident.** **262 sentences of forty words or more across these ten files on the definition at `state/batch-summaries/volume-06-batch-0004.md` §8.5.1, and none of them is duplicated, and the same definition gives none duplicated across all fifty chapters of Volume 06, which is 1,464 of them.** The counting device is 0.90 to 1.82 broad and 0.65 to 1.52 narrow per hundred words on the two
