Day 331 is a Wednesday and is the sixteenth of the Bare Month; 332 a Thursday, the seventeenth; 333 a Friday, the eighteenth; 334 a Saturday, the nineteenth; **335 a Sunday, the twentieth; 336 a Monday, the twenty-first; 337 a Tuesday, the twenty-second; 338 a Wednesday, the twenty-third; 339 a Thursday, the twenty-fourth; and 340 a Friday, the twenty-fifth.** Twenty-eight weekday tokens stand across the ten files, every one of them was read in its sentence against the day it names, **and three faults of one family were found and repaired in the writing pass**: a *Monday* where the woman of about sixty-nine's refusal is on a Saturday (Chapter 0320, day 320), a *Saturday* where the fifth notice went out on a Sunday (Chapter 0335, day 335), and a *Tuesday* attached to a slate that is not attached to any day at all (Chapter 0336). All twenty-eight are now correct and not one was taken from a neighbouring chapter, from a card, from a day map or from a state file. **No chapter of this batch uses an ordinal for a month or any bare month form, and the words *length*, *long* and *short* applied to the month are at zero on all ten files.**

2. **THE FIFTH NOTICE WENT OUT ON THE TWENTIETH OF THE BARE MONTH FOR A ROOM ON THE TWENTY-FOURTH, AND IT IS FOUR DAYS, AND IT NAMES ITS OWN MORNING INSIDE ITS OWN CHAPTER.** Chapter 0335, day 335, a Sunday. **It is a chapter about four days of a man knowing he is going to be asked, and in it there is no form at all.** The man of about thirty-one who carries things for a living went out onto eleven miles of flats with nothing in his hands and said that nobody was going to be asked anything, and the man of about fifty-two who digs worked out in about four minutes that he was being told something, and nobody asked him whether it would go. **Three things are on a page in that chapter and they are the volume's own instruments seen from the other end.** The man writes the question himself, in his own words, on the rough side of a board off the wall of a hut, about as long as it takes him to put nine willow in nine holes, with a pencil stub about two fingers long, **and there was no form on eleven miles of flats to do it on, and the reason is not poverty: the only page in four hundred miles with a person's name at the top of it is five lines long with nothing under the first line, it is in a city, it could have been sent out, and nobody sent it, because a page with a name at the top of it is a page and this is not one.** The carrier said the reason out loud on open ground: **a question in your own hand is worth more than a question in yours, and the reason is nine years old and it is the carrier's and not the asker's, and he said it on eleven miles of open ground because nobody in four hundred miles has ever said it in a room where the man it was about was standing.** The sheet went out at about the fifth hour of the afternoon and the carrier walked eleven miles in the last of the light and **did not open it, and the one he was least entitled to open is the one he carried furthest.** A hand is not a page, a hand stays in the hand that made it until that person dies, and the price of no name at the top of a sheet is that nobody can do anything for you. *Open, and no chapter of Volume 07 may reduce this notice to three days, may not put a name at the top of it, and may not write a sixth.*

3. **THE THREE ROOMS OF DAYS 331, 332 AND 333 ARE ROOMS THE REGISTER-KEEPER IS NOT IN, AND THAT IS WHAT CHAPTER 0330 ALREADY ESTABLISHED.** A room over a shop on the low ground for the sixteenth, a room at the back of a chandlery with a skylight for the seventeenth, a room over a bakehouse for the eighteenth. **The woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register is in none of the ten files of this batch and is not referred to in any of them, and Tamsin Quill is in Chapters 0331 and 0336 and in no others, and the two women of twenty-nine are never in one room and nothing in this batch settles whether they are one woman.** The consent fracture is visible in Chapter 0336 and is not mended in it and nobody apologised in it: a woman put a nine-day thing on a table in about nine seconds and the man beside her said one word, and about four people in that room worked out that he has not asked her for the sheet once in nine days and that this is not the same as a man who has stopped minding, **and the fifth condition a woman who reads Crown Law said exists is not given and is not implied.** The third party in Chapter 0336 is the man of about twenty-seven at the store, the board is not written on in it and the wall above it is not washed in it, and the figures on the board are still the figures of the two hundred and twenty-fourth day of this flood. *Open.*

4. **A DAY ON A DOOR IN THIS CITY IS A NOTICE AND NOT A RECORD, AND BY THE END OF THIS BATCH ONE OF THOSE DOORS HAS TWO DATES ON IT AND NOTHING UNDER EITHER.** Chapter 0330 put one date on the door of a room over a shop on the low ground and said out loud that a door with one date on it and nothing above it is a notice. **Chapter 0335 put a second date on that same door, the twenty-fourth, above the fifteenth, and nothing was rubbed out and nothing was put under either, and the finding is that a door with two dates on it and nothing underneath is still a notice and not a record, and that the reason is that a record has one thing under a day and this has none.** Chapter 0339 ends in that room with both dates still on the door. *Open.*

5. **THE FOURTH OF THE FOUR CAME TO THE DOOR OF THE ROOM ON THE EIGHTEENTH AND DID NOT COME IN, AND NOBODY IN THAT ROOM SAID ONE WORD TO HER, AND THAT IS THE ANSWER.** Chapter 0333, day 333, a Friday, the eighteenth. **The no was said on the fourteenth in a different room in four words, in daylight, with a date on the door, and it is not said again in this chapter, and it is not softened, not argued with, not apologised for and not overturned, and no chapter makes it a defeat.** She stood in the doorway with a stick of hazel in her hand and did not lean on it and said nothing, and nobody in that room got up and nobody turned round and nobody asked her one question, and the door stood open for as long as it takes to fill a pail after she had gone. **The room happened without her and either of those is the answer and neither of them is a failure, and a refusal nobody asked for is the cheapest kind there is and is the only kind in this flood that nobody can take back.** Three of the four are in that room, which is the first time any three of them have been in a room together, and the fourth is at the door. Nobody was asked whether it would go, and **the word *delegation* is at zero across these ten files and stays at zero, and a chapter may say four people and may not give them a word for what they are.** *Open.*

6. **THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-FIVE HAS NINE WORDS HE WROTE HIMSELF, AND HE HAS NOT SAID THEM IN A ROOM, AND A LATER CHAPTER MAY NOT MAKE HIM SAY THEM IN ONE UNLESS IT IS CHAPTER 0342.** Chapter 0327 is the page. Chapter 0331 is the room on the sixteenth: he puts the sheet face down, will not have it read out, says yes in about four words without being asked, and gives the room the number nine and not what is in it. **The finding of Chapter 0331 is that he was one of the nine places the second notice went to, and that he took his own sheet off the post in the gap for the cart on the twelfth, read it, put it back and has had it inside his coat for four days, and he has told nobody and there is a room with about nine people in it that has spent a fortnight counting the four days off the top of a table and has been counting the half of them in which a sheet is in a coat backwards.** Tamsin Quill says the finding out loud at about the fourth hour of the afternoon and the man of about twenty-five does not answer it. **He looks at the door in Chapter 0333 when the woman of about sixty-one stands in it, and he does not say the nine words to her and he does not say them to the two who are in the room, and about four people in that room worked out that the condition he set for himself in 0327 has arrived on the day he set it for.** *Open, and a later writer may not spend these nine words.*

7. **THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-EIGHT CAN TELL YOU THAT EIGHT OF NINETEEN STONES ARE NOT THE SAME STONE AS THE OTHER ELEVEN AND CANNOT TELL YOU WHEN, AND THE NINETEEN ARE NOT RESOLVED.** Chapter 0332, day 332. **A man who goes up ladders for a living looks at a thing that is not the same colour and does not carry the same tooling and does not sit in the same mortar, and gives that in a mouth in a room with a date on the door, and then says where it stops: a mortar tells you how long it has been mixed and how long it has been standing and it does not tell you a year, and a man can put a stone in a gap in an afternoon and a man can put the same stone in the same gap in a week and the wall is the same wall.** That is one thing taken off about nine hundred and one is not the answer. **The nineteen stones and the eight that are different were carried to a place in Chapter 0323 and are not resolved by this batch, and are not joined to a fence, a ditch, a channel or a hedge, and the eight are not said to be the first eight or the last eight.** He mends a skylight nobody asked him to mend, as he mended a catch in Chapter 0328, and about four people in that room have worked out since that the room over a market has had a shut window for about four days and that nobody will ever be able to say who did it. *Open.*

8. **THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-SEVEN REFUSED THE ONE INSTRUMENT THAT WOULD HAVE GOT HIS WINTER BACK, IN A ROOM WHERE HE WAS RIGHT, AND NOBODY TOLD HIM HE WAS WRONG.** Chapter 0334, day 334, with Jonah Pike. **Jonah Pike proposed a good thing and it was correct: that the man should write out in his own hand what he had paid for and could not show anybody and put his own name at the top of it, so that the sheet would be the one kind of page anybody in four hundred miles can ask to have produced. And the man refused, and the reason is his, and it is that a page with his name at the top of it is a page somebody can ask for, and the day after that there is a column, and he has been a column twice in his life and both times it was somebody else's.** The case is shut and a man who kept it shut is wrong about it in the same room and nobody tells him. **The Crown's schedule is on a page in this volume and is standing and correct and is not opened, not filled in, not withdrawn and is not the subject of any of these ten chapters; the Regent and her office are nine hundred miles away; the road north of the Marches is shut and does not open in this flood; and the one correct sentence from that office still costs the Marches its winter road and has cost that office nothing.** He does not leave, and he is not wrong, and nobody in that room is wrong. Chapter 0337 is his second of two and is a cost and not a defeat: he goes out past the last named house to a gap for a cart and asks at four of eight doors where their ground stops, and not one of them knows, and one of them tells him what it is called, and he says it out loud in a room with a date on the door the same afternoon, and puts a receipt with his own name at the top of it on a table, and says he is not going to be told he is wrong, and the money is still in his hand. **A notice went to nine places and the ninth was a post in a gap for a cart with a nail in it about four feet up that nobody knows who put in, and it is still on the post.** *Open.*

9. **THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-TWO WHO DIGS SAYS YES AND HIS CONDITION IS THAT HE WILL LOOK AT IT FROM THE END OF IT AND WILL NOT PUT HIS HANDS ON IT, AND HE PUTS A ROD IN NOTHING.** Chapter 0339, day 339, a Thursday, the twenty-fourth, in a room over a shop on the low ground, in daylight, with a date on the door. **Nobody asked him anything, and he said yes, and he said the condition before anybody asked him for it, and nobody in that room argued with it, and the room accepted it in one breath.** The reason is a trade and not a modesty and it is his and it is on a page: a man whose whole life is putting things in holes is a man people hand holes to, and a man people hand holes to is not asked what is in the ground. **He has a rod — a length of ash about four feet long with a flat blade on the end of it, ground down on one side and not the other — and he offered the fact of it unasked and did not bring it, and nobody in that room asked him about the rod.** **The line in the ground was not measured, dug, counted, probed or resolved in any of these ten chapters, no character in any of them goes and looks at it, nobody put a rod in it or in anything, no third hole was made, and the two holes nine feet apart were not cleared, measured or referred to.** The name is on a page once, in Chapter 0321, and the man of about forty-three did not open the book again in Chapter 0339 and said why: the correct use of a name in four hundred miles is once, in daylight. **Adrian is named in Chapter 0339 and in no other of these ten, and in it he is in a room reading a page and is asked no question about it, and he is aged nowhere and is Stage 2 on all ten days.** *Open.*

10. **THE VOLUME'S ONE PANEL IS ON DAY 340 AND IT IS REFUSED OUT LOUD BY A MAN WHO TRADES ON A BOARD, AND WHAT IT COSTS HIM IS SPECIFIC.** Chapter 0340, a Friday, the twenty-fifth, in the room at the back of the Open Hand, which has no key and no person owns it and whose right of refusal cannot be bought out is in about nine rooms in this city every day. The panel is plain, it is set as its own short block with its own line breaks, it carries no emphasis marks, and it gives a rule and not an answer: **a word may be put to a thing in a room in daylight with a date on it, and a word may be taken back the same way, and a person may not be taken back at all.** The wall said nothing on days 331 to 339 and said this on day 340, and that is the only day of these ten on which it said anything. **It is refused before a sentence was finished by a man who trades on the Open Hand board, and the cost is that the board he keeps has had a name off it once, in his own hand, in about the ninth year of this flood, and he has never said so to anybody, and the refusal is what makes him say so in the same room, and saying so is the cost.** He refused a woman who is on nothing and is named on nothing at the back of a market, in nineteen words, about a hundred days into this flood, and has never said so to anybody, and about as long afterwards a man asked him to put his own name on that board and he wrote it on in his own hand and took it off with his own cloth the next morning, after the man came and told the woman that her name was not going on, **and about four hundred people in this city can see the place on the board where it was and not one of them will ever be told, and the people who were in that room this morning now know it, and knowing it does not put anybody back.** The panel does not open a route, does not grant a permission and does not resolve anything, and the chapter it is in is about a person and not about a figure. **A man of about sixty with a handcart and a length of rope asked out loud, at about the fourth hour of the afternoon, whether a person can be put back, and nobody in that room answered him, and the wall does not answer it either.** *Open, and the panel may not be spent again.*

11. **THE FIGURES, AND NONE OF THEM IS AN INTERVAL FIGURE AND NONE OF THEM IS IN A LEAD.** The only two instruments these ten days use are **a count of days of the flood** and **a day of the Bare Month with its own form**, the sixteenth to the twenty-fifth, and no chapter of this batch uses an ordinal for a month or any bare month form. **No lead paragraph of these ten chapters carries an interval figure at all, and the chapter's first paragraph has somebody's hands on something in ten out of ten, and the two physical-work chapters of the batch — 0335 and 0338 — state the light, the cold, the wind and what is under a person's hands in the first paragraph.** **The count of this city's silences is 232 at day 331 and 240 at day 340, derived, on no page, in no mouth, and printed in none of the ten chapters; the wall said nothing on nine of the ten days and said something on day 340, which is the second day this flood on which it has said something after day 256.** The four who have not left are on a page in three of these ten chapters and their days are not counted and the word is not used. **The trestle count is exactly three hundred at day 340 and the bag on the boards is exactly a hundred days old at day 340 and both are in §33A item 5 as traps, and neither figure is printed in any of these ten chapters.** *Open.*

12. **AND NOTHING IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS IS A POWER.** The gains are a man who was one of the nine places and had his own sheet in his coat for four days and told a room, a yes without a question in a room with a date on the door, nine words nobody has said, a skylight nobody asked for, eight of nineteen stones and nobody able to say when, a refusal of the only page that would have worked and a winter that is not got back, four doors in a row and not one person who can say where their own ground stops, a notice written in nine lines on the rough side of a board with a pencil stub because there was no form at all, a man who says yes and will not put his hands on it, a second bar at a gate that nobody sent for, a date said out loud to a boy with two pails for the nineteenth time, a rule about words on a wall, and a name off a board in a man's own hand that nobody had been told about. **None of them is a power. One of them is the price of a power. All of them are heavier than a power.** *Open.*

---

## 45. Rules established in Volume 07, Chapters 0341–0350 — THE CLIMAX, THE COST, THE RESOLUTION, AND THE LAST IMAGE

**This section is the tenth batch section for Volume 07 and it carries the rules the last ten days of the volume put on a page. §§1 to 32 are not changed. §33 is not changed. §33A IS NOT CHANGED, and a batch has not amended it and may not. §§34 to 44 are not changed. This section is written by a batch and it enters findings, and a finding a batch makes may be repaired by a later batch and the only decision a batch may not make is a calendar decision and one about a form.**

1. **THE WORD CAME OFF THE SECOND SLATE ON THE TWENTY-SEVENTH DAY OF THE BARE MONTH, IN CHALK, WITH A CLOTH, BY THE MAN WHO SAID IT, AND IT IS ON NO PAGE IN THE WORLD AFTER THAT.** Chapter 0342. **A name is only a removal when the thing named can be found; a line in an orchard cannot be found because nobody is going to it; so a line can be named safely; the moment somebody starts going to it the name becomes a description, and the name has to go.** Those four sentences are the finding, they are his, they stand once, they are in no other mouth, and no chapter may improve on them. **THE WORD IS IN NARRATION TWICE IN CHAPTER 0342 AND IN NO MOUTH IN ANY OF THE FIFTY FILES, AND IT IS AT ZERO IN THE FORTY-SEVEN FILES THAT ARE NOT CHAPTERS 0310 AND 0342, and no file a later phase produces may print it again.** The day-154 sentence is not said, restated, paraphrased or improved on in any of the ten, and none of the three sentences this manuscript has already used about that word is in any mouth in any of them.

2. **THE COST OF THE SUBTRACTION IS A SUBTRACTION AND IT IS TOTAL, AND THE MAN WHO PAYED IT IS NO BETTER OFF.** Chapter 0343, day 343, a Monday, the twenty-eighth. **The naming bought that field one afternoon and it cost that field thirty-two days, and the whole of what the afternoon was for is that a room in this city could say out loud that a mark in the ground is a mark and not a boundary, and there is no such room now.** The mark is where it was and the man is where he was and nobody in that yard or that room tells him it is a sad one. **A word on a slate is a pointing thing and it starts pointing at about the rate a man walks, and the word came off on the Sunday and the digger went on the Tuesday whatever anybody said on the Sunday, and the room did not know which day he had chosen and could not ask.**

3. **THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-TWO WHO DIGS WENT, AND PUT A BARROW AGAINST THE END OF A CUT AT A STONE ABOUT NINE HUNDRED PACES OFF A SHUT GATE, AND LOOKED ALONG IT FROM THE END, AND DID NOT TOUCH IT, AND SAID ONE SENTENCE, AND THE SENTENCE IS NOT AN ANSWER.** Chapter 0344, day 344, a Tuesday, the twenty-ninth. **HE LEARNED NOTHING A ROD WOULD HAVE TOLD HIM AND THE CHAPTER HAS TO SURVIVE THAT.** The line in the ground was not measured, dug, counted, probed or resolved in Chapter 0344; no character in it goes and looks at the line except him; no rod went into anything; the two holes nine feet apart were not touched. **HE DID NOT PUT A FOOT IN IT, HE DID NOT STAND OVER IT, HE DID NOT GO ALONG IT, AND HE CAME BACK FOUR PACES AT A TIME, AND THAT IS THE ONLY DIFFERENCE ANYBODY COULD SEE.** His reason is a trade and not a modesty and it is his. **The man of about sixty with a handcart and a length of rope was on that ground the same morning and nobody sent him and he did not put a fence in that chapter and did not build anything, and what he did he did in Chapter 0348.**

4. **A GATE WITH TWO BARS IS ONE GATE, AND IT GOES ON A ROLL AS ONE GATE, AND THE SECOND BAR IS ON NO ROLL AT ALL.** Chapter 0345, day 345, a Wednesday, the thirtieth. **A roll in the Marches is a gate, a keeper, a set of days and nothing else, and there is no form in four hundred miles for the second object, and the man put it in himself on a Wednesday night because a gate that does not shut is not a gate, and nobody sent for it and nobody four hundred miles off can be shown has heard of it.** A keeper is not a holder; the word is at zero across the volume and the two rolls of the same gates are not put side by side; **nobody in that chapter says what the difference between the two is worth, and the man who is asked does not say it and says that he knows and is not going to.** **A ninth line on a wall is the one line in this city that makes a person findable and it is the one line nobody can administer, and the man who wrote it refused again and asked in advance not to be thanked and was not thanked, and whether it works is still unanswered and is not going to be answered in this volume.**

5. **THE RESOLUTION IS A LINE ENTERED IN A REGISTER WITH A DAY ON IT AND NOTHING UNDER THE DAY AND NO WORD ON IT, AND A THING RECORDED AS ONE THAT MAY NOT BE DUG.** Chapter 0346, day 346, a Thursday, the thirty-first. **A refusal is not a return, and that is why the entry is the only instrument in four hundred miles that an office nine hundred miles away cannot ask to have produced.** The third column of the form is left empty on purpose and a woman who keeps a register for nine years told a room of about nine people that she would leave the room rather than fill it. **The man of about fifty-seven is told, in the room, what he has, and what he has is a line he cannot have shown anybody and a winter he cannot get back, and he says the cost out loud and nobody in the room tells him he was wrong, because nobody in the room is wrong, and he is not a villain in this and is not converted and does not leave and is not happy.** **The sheet of paper thirty years old in the tin is not entered, is not copied and does not go out of the room, and the condition a woman of about sixty-nine put on it on the fifth of that month is hers and nobody may un-condition it.** The next question is asked once, in a mouth, in that room, on that day, and is not answered: **what is a person for, on a page, when the only thing anybody needs them for is a journey.**

6. **NINE BOARDS CAME OFF THE WALL OF A SHUT GATE BECAUSE A MAN CAME UP A ROAD, AND NOBODY MADE A DECISION, AND THE WALL IS BARE.** Chapter 0347, day 347, a Friday, the thirty-second. **The boards went there on about the forty-first day of the Short Month counting the day they arrived and the arrangement for taking them up was for the forty-ninth, which was a Thursday, and it held for about four weeks after the Thursday and then stopped holding for anybody, and neither of the two people it was between ever found out that it had stopped.** The woman of about fifty was not asked anything about the boards and nobody was sent to her, and a wall with the shape of nine boards on it and four inches of dry stone at the foot of it is the only object in four hundred miles anybody in this city has made without a page and cannot take back. **The barrow of boards at that gate is a different barrow of boards on a different wall nine miles the other way, and no chapter may join the two.**

7. **A FENCE FOUR FEET OFF A LINE NOBODY HAS MEASURED IS THE MOST ORDINARY THING IN THE WORLD AND THE MOST PERMANENT, AND IT IS THE LAST POLITICAL ACT OF THE FLOOD.** Chapter 0348, day 348, a Saturday, the thirty-third. **Four feet is not a measurement. It is a number a man of about sixty has in his mouth and he said so out loud, and he set out from a row of trees and stopped at a stone and did not measure anything, and a fence is not an account of anything and does not know what it is four feet off, and that is why it is going to be there when neither of the two men who never agreed where the line was is, and the fence does not care.** Nobody sent him and nobody stops him. **He cut sixteen posts, put nine in the ground on the Saturday with seven left in the cart, and had thirteen in the ground on the Monday with three left for the Thursday, and the count is a count of willow and not a measurement.** **The nineteen stones are not this fence and may not be joined to it, and a woman of about thirty-three carried the order of them in on the seventeenth of that month and has been out and looked at them more than once, and a man who goes up ladders says he can tell that eight of the nineteen are a different stone off a floor without seeing them and is not going to guess at it in a room, and that is a different thing four hundred miles inland.** The last image is on a Monday, day 350, the thirty-fifth of the Bare Month, and it is the fence, and it will be there on Tuesday.

8. **AND NOTHING IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS IS A POWER.** The gains are a word off a slate, thirty-two days, a barrow at the end of a cut, a sentence that is not an answer, a second bar on no roll, a sixth refusal nobody asked for, an entry with an empty third column, nine boards and a bare wall, thirteen posts and a fence. **Adrian is Stage 2 on all ten days, is named in ONE of the ten — Chapter 0346, a room in which he reads an entry and is asked nothing — and is in no chapter in any form in the other nine, and that one is a room with a page in it and a chapter that has a page in it and nobody in the room identified does not count.** He is aged nowhere. No threshold was opened, no working was performed, the other world is not named once, nobody offered him a workway and he asked for none, and the words *passage* and *privilege* are at zero. **The consent fracture is visible in Chapter 0349 and is not mended in it and nobody apologised in it, and the fifth condition is not given.** Nobody asked any of the four whether it would go. *Delegation* is at zero across the fifty files.

9. **THE MEASUREMENTS, WITH THE DEFINITIONS, AND ONE OF THEM IS WORSE THAN THE FOUR BATCHES BEFORE IT. THE FIGURES BELOW ARE THE POST-REPAIR FIGURES, and the repair is at `state/batch-summaries/volume-07-batch-0005.md` §11, and the pre-repair figures they replace are named beside each of them so that a later writer can see which change is a repair and which is a measurement.** The share of words inside bold marks, heading line included, `---` rules counted, bold marks counted as nothing, is **7.0 to 11.6 per cent, a mean of 9.4, in forty-nine spans** — 7.1 to 11.6 with a mean of 9.3 in forty-seven spans before the repair, and the two spans are two first-person speeches that stood in Chapters 0341 and 0342 with no quotation mark and no bold mark at all — against Batch 0004's 8.9 to 19.8 with a mean of 13.9 and Volume 06's 6.2 to 21.8 with a mean of 13.8, and the longest bolded turn in the ten is **eighty-nine** and about 90 is the working ceiling and ninety-five is the hard one. **THE MEAN IS FOUR AND A HALF POINTS BELOW THE FOUR BATCHES BEFORE IT AND THE CAUSE IS NAMED: six of the ten chapters have an object or a piece of ground or a road for a subject, and the two instruments the narrator has — the counting frame and the bolded speech — both want a person in a room, and the three chapters that gave them a room run 10.7, 10.7 and 11.6 and are the top of the range.** The chorus frame, on the bare head every occurrence of *about four people in*, stands **seventy-nine times in four hundred and forty-five paragraphs, one in five point six** — seventy-seven in four hundred and forty-three before the repair, and the two are the two narration paragraphs the repair inserted — against one in three point seven in Batch 0004, and **it is at zero in Chapters 0345 and 0347 and in no other file of the ten, and the two earlier editions of this item said three files and were each wrong on their own files, one of them naming 0348 and one of them naming 0350, and both of those carry two.** The exchange, on the published definition, is **134 plain speeches and 27 answered at twenty point one per cent**, and the instrument reproduces Batch 0004's published 16 of 28 exactly, so the difference is in the files and not in the script, and the denominator is four and a half times Batch 0004's because this batch carries far more speech, **and the repair did not move it, because a paragraph carrying neither marker is skipped by this instrument and by the bolded one at the same time, which is the finding and the reason a batch cannot see that class of fault in itself.** Weekday tokens: **eighty-one, all read, twelve faults found and repaired, eleven in the writing pass and the twelfth by a review, and one token went out and one came in.** Long sentences of forty words or more: **273**, the two being the two narration paragraphs the repair inserted. The word-run, on a sequential token match across all fifty files with each lead paragraph subtracted, has a top of **twelve** and that instrument is not the longest-common-substring and is not comparable with the figures published for the earlier batches and both are printed for that reason, **and the repair was checked on the longest-common-contiguous-token instrument on the five pairs it touched and every one of them stands at its pre-repair figure.** A fused word is at zero on all ten files, measured on a vocabulary built from every chapter of every volume. **AND THE COUNT OF THE FENCE IS NOT IN THIS LIST BECAUSE NO INSTRUMENT IN IT COUNTS AN OBJECT: sixteen posts were cut, nine were in the ground on the Saturday with seven left in the cart, thirteen were in the ground on the Monday with three left for the Thursday, and the count is a count of willow and not a measurement, and it stood at three different totals across two chapters until the review repair closed it, and that is the cheapest and the most expensive fault in this repository at the same time.**

10. **AND TWO FIGURES THIS BATCH WAS TOLD TO CARRY AND DID NOT SETTLE.** The age of the man who trades on the Open Hand board, which is about thirty-seven in the outline and about thirty-seven in Chapter 0309 and about thirty-nine in the prose of Volumes 02 to 06, and he is given the trade and the board and no age in any of these ten chapters and is referred to once. **And the fact that the fourth of the four said no on the fourteenth of the Bare Month in a room with a date on the door, in four words, before anybody asked, and that the room on the eighteenth is Chapter 0333, in which she came to the door and did not come in, and that it is not a defeat and is not to be softened and nobody in this volume is going to ask her about it.** Both are carried forward whole. *Open.*

## 46. Rules established in Volume 08, Chapters 0351–0360 — A FORM ON A TABLE, A FOURTH PLACE REFUSED, A MAN ANSWERED, AND A FENCE FINISHED

**This section is the first of the sections §33B item 6 reserved for the Volume 08 batches, and it was written by Batch 0001, and by nothing else. §32 is not changed. §33 is not changed. §33A is NOT CHANGED, and a batch has not amended it and may not. §33B is NOT CHANGED, and item 8a's stale register of notices is still routed and is not this section's. §§34 to 45 are not changed. No calendar decision is made in this section and no form is registered in it, and the sixth form, *the Nth day of the Bare Month*, stands as §33A item 2 registered it, with no length stated, and this section states no length. The volume's one panel is fixed to day 393 and none of these ten chapters spends it.**

**AND A REVIEW OF THIS BATCH REPAIRED THREE OF ITS TEN CHAPTERS AND FIVE PLACES IN THIS SECTION, and the amendments are at item 5, item 6, item 7, item 10 and this paragraph, and the whole of them is listed at `state/batch-summaries/volume-08-batch-0001.md` §6 items 7 to 9. No day, no weekday, no month day, no form, no notice and no object count in this section moved, and nothing in items 1 to 4 or 8 or 9 moved at all, and the plan moved in nothing.**

**The ten days are days 351 to 360, one chapter to one day, Chapters 0351 to 0360, and the run was recomputed from day 1 being a Tuesday and from nothing else before any prose was written: 351 Tuesday, 352 Wednesday, 353 Thursday, 354 Friday, 355 Saturday, 356 Sunday, 357 Monday, 358 Tuesday, 359 Wednesday, 360 Thursday. The Bare Month days are the thirty-sixth to the forty-fifth, the Nth being day less 315. There is no double day in this batch and there is no double day in this volume. **Fifty-eight weekday tokens stand across the ten files, every one of them was read in the sentence it stands in against the day that sentence names, and one interval fault was found and repaired and is named at item 9. The figure this section published when it was written was seventy-eight, and the review of the batch re-ran the instrument and it is fifty-eight, and the pre-repair figure is named here beside the corrected one because a silent correction is the third version of the same fault. The instrument is every occurrence of the seven day names in the ten files read as prose, and it returns two hundred and seventy-three on Volume 07's last ten against that batch's published two hundred and seventy-three, so it is calibrated.****

1. **A FORM WITH A HEADING AND FOUR PLACES UNDER IT IS ON A PAGE, AND THE FOUR PLACES ARE NAMED IN THE ORDER THEY ARE PRINTED.** A district; a population; a rota; a share. It came up the road in a satchel with a flap on the thirty-sixth day of that month, was put on a table in the room over a market, and was read out in the local language in daylight with a date on the door and about nine people in it on the fortieth. **The first has nowhere to start, the third is empty on purpose and has been since the thirty-first day of that month, the second is a number one man keeps in his own head, and the fourth is a share and a share is a household's.** *Open. **A later writer may not add a fifth place under the heading, may not copy the form, may not print a second one, and may not lay the Crown's schedule beside it. That form's four places are a date, a gate, a holder and the days, it stands, it is correct, and this section does not define *holder* and does not touch it.***

2. **THE FOURTH PLACE IS A SHARE AND IT HAS BEEN REFUSED OUT LOUD, BY A HOUSEHOLD, IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS, AND THE HOUSEHOLD IS A WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-TWO WHO WAS ON NO PAGE.** Chapter 0356, day 356, on a step, in front of a woman with a slate, in daylight, with a date in chalk on the wall behind her and nothing above it. **Her finding is hers and it is not the finding of the woman of Chapter 0266 and is not that finding said differently: a share is a thing you have got and a place in it is a thing you have not got, and a person asked to put the first into the second has been asked to give up the only one of the two that is hers.** She did not define the word and said out loud that she was not going to. Her second handle, invented on that page, is **a door she is not asked to open.** *Open, and §35.6's finding stays with the woman who said it first and this woman is not she and the two have never been in one room.*

3. **A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-EIGHT WAS ASKED WHAT THE COLUMN IS FOR, FOR THE FIRST TIME IN NINE YEARS, AND HE ANSWERED, AND WHAT HE SAID IS THE NAME OF THIS VOLUME AND IS IN NO FILE.** Chapter 0357, day 357, a Monday, the forty-second day of the Bare Month, in the room over a market, in daylight, with a date in chalk on the door, about nine people in it, asked by the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register, whose reason was nine words and was her own and was said once. **He answered in the local language, out loud, once, in seven words, and neither of them stopped working while it happened, and nobody thanked him and he did not ask to be thanked.** The words are in the local language, in the local room, and in **no chapter but that one, and in no state file, no batch record, no prompt and no outline, including this section.** *Open. **The words may not be printed a second time, no chapter may state that what he said is the name of anything, the narrator may not say it again, and this section does not have them. The figure he has in his head, as against the figure he sends, is on no page and is printed nowhere and is not available to anybody.***

4. **A FENCE IS FINISHED AND ITS COUNTS ARE ON A PAGE AND THEY HOLD.** Sixteen willow posts cut in a copse about four miles the other way; nine in the ground on day 348, a Saturday, with seven left in the cart; four more on day 350, a Monday, making thirteen; **the last three on day 353, a Thursday**; eleven withies, bound with the same cord. Chapter 0353 is the page for the whole count and Chapter 0360 states the total again identically. **Four feet is not a measurement; it is a number a man of about sixty has in his mouth, he said it out loud once on the Saturday, and it is in narration once in Chapter 0353 and in a mouth in neither fence chapter.** Nobody sent him, nobody asks him, and no chapter of this volume may ask him. *Open, and **no chapter of Volume 08 may print a different total of posts, of withies or of days in any two chapters, may not join the fence to the mark in the orchard, and may not print a distance between them.***

5. **THREE NOTICES WENT OUT ON THREE DAYS AND ALL THREE ARE FOUR DAYS, UNSIGNED, WITH NOTHING AT THE TOP OF ANY OF THEM, AND ALL THREE WENT TO A PLACE AND NOT TO A HOUSE.** Notice on **day 351** for the room on **day 355**, Chapter 0351; on **day 354** for the room on **day 358**, Chapter 0354; on **day 359** for the room on **day 363**, Chapter 0359. **Each names both its own morning and the room's day inside its own chapter, and Chapter 0353 says out loud why the sheet cannot go on the day the fence is finished, and Chapter 0360 names the first and the third of the four that follow it — day 362 for the room on day 366, and day 371 for the room on day 375 — with their own days in a mouth. **The four that follow are 362 for 366, 366 for 370, 371 for 375 and 389 for 393, the two the chapter does not name are 366 for 370 and 389 for 393, and a state file that says the chapter names the first two of the four is wrong on its own arithmetic and is repaired at `state/continuity.md`.** Three of the volume's seven. *Open, and **no name is at the top of any of the seven and none may be added, and the price of that is that nobody can do anything for you.***

6. **AND THE TWO THINGS THESE TEN CHAPTERS DID TO THE HOUSE.** A man of about thirty-four kept a piece of chalk in an inside breast pocket in front of about nine people who were going to write a heading on a board, and did not take it out, and had asked in advance not to be thanked and was not thanked. And a man of about thirty-nine stood against a wall with his own slate on his knee and **said two speeches** in the last hour of a room and was not asked one question about any of it, and nobody opened his slate. **The count is two, the figure this item published when it was written was four, and the chapter's own two closing paragraphs also said four while the chapter prints two; a review found it and the chapter and the three files that took the number off it now say two. The reason the error survived a batch that measured everything else is that the record took the number off the chapter instead of counting the speeches in it, and a count taken off a page is only as good as the page's own count of itself.** *Open, and **the ninth line is not moved, dated, read aloud, taken down, thanked for or resolved, and whether it works stays unanswered.***

7. **AND THE THINGS THESE TEN CHAPTERS DID NOT DO, WHICH A LATER WRITER HAS TO KNOW ARE STILL UNTOUCHED.** The word on the second slate is at zero in all ten files, and no chapter of the ten gives the mark in the orchard a word by any route, and none of the three sentences this manuscript has used about that word is in a mouth in any of them. The mark was not measured, dug, counted, probed or resolved, and nobody went and looked at it. **The day-154 sentence is not said, restated, paraphrased or improved on.** The question of day 346 is not answered. **Nobody was asked whether any of the four will go, no chapter counted the days since the woman of about sixty-one said no at any value in narration or in a mouth, and the four are four people and not a company and not a body and the word stays out of this volume.** **THE TWO WOMEN OF TWENTY-NINE WERE NEVER IN ONE ROOM, and the arrangement that holds it is on a page in Chapter 0358 and was put there by a review repair: the register-keeper stands on the step at the back with no name on it from the tenth hour of the day until the fourth hour of the afternoon and does not come into the room, and Tamsin Quill is at the bench with the folded sheet of road in her apron from the sixth hour of the morning until the room empties, and the two of them are about four paces apart for as long as she was there and say one word to each other. Nothing in these ten chapters settles whether they are one woman and nothing may.** The consent fracture is visible in Chapter 0358 and is not mended in it, nobody apologised in it, the fifth condition is not given, and the fracture is visible there as a space where a man is not.** The case is shut and the schedule is not filled in and is not the subject of any of the ten. The barrow of boards at the shut gate and the barrow of salt at the wharf are two barrows and are not joined, and the barrow of boards has no value after day 347. The keeper of the Redroot gate is in none of the ten files, her days are not counted and her year is not named. The Regent is nine hundred miles away and is not defeated, and the nine sentences about a return are not said, restated, paraphrased or improved on in any of the ten. The ninth line is untouched. The store's board is nine miles up the upper road and was not moved into a room. The box is shut, the island is not owned, and the room at the far end of the Narrow Mark is unlooked at. *All open.*

8. **AND THE STORE'S BOARD WAS PUT BACK WHERE A PAGE HAS IT, because this batch found its own draft standing in the wrong room and a page outranks a card.** Chapter 0336 puts it above a door at the top of the upper road nine miles up. **The board has not been written on since the two hundred and twenty-fifth day of this flood and the figures on it are the figures of the day before that, and a man of about twenty-seven washes it every morning and has decided not to wash the wall above it, and the reason is about how a thing looks and not about what is on it, and that reason is on a page in Volume 06 and is not improved on here.** A room over a market has its own board over its door with the smudge of about nine dates on it and it is not the store's board. *Open.*

9. **AND THE ONE INTERVAL FAULT THESE TEN CHAPTERS HAD, NAMED BESIDE ITS REPAIR.** Chapter 0353 closed by saying that four days counted from that morning is three for a room on the forty-third day of that month; the chapter's morning is the thirty-eighth, a Thursday, and the forty-third is the forty-second, so the count from that morning is five and the count from the day after tomorrow is three. **The sentence now gives all three counts in one sentence and names the two days, and the chapter's argument — that nobody is going to write a notice on the day a fence is finished for a room on the fourth day after it — stands on the arithmetic.** It is the same family as §45's unrepaired line at `chapters/volume-07/chapter-0341.md:149`, and a figure that comes out round, or comes out at a suspiciously round fraction, or comes out at a number that makes an argument tidier than the facts do, has been checked twice and not once. *Open.*

10. **AND THE HOUSE FIGURES FOR THESE TEN DAYS, WITH THE DEFINITIONS BESIDE THEM, AND NO TARGET SET ON ANY OF THEM.** **The share of words inside bold marks, heading line included, `---` rules counted, bold marks counted as nothing, file split on whitespace: 13.1, 10.1, 5.2, 10.7, 13.1, 14.0, 8.2, 8.0, 10.0 and 9.4 per cent, a range of 5.2 to 14.0 and a mean of 10.2**, against Volume 01's 10.4 as the model, about 35 as the ceiling, Volume 07's last ten at 7.0 to 11.6 with a mean of 9.4, and Volume 06's 13.8. **The mean is the unweighted mean of the ten per-chapter figures, which is the convention Volume 07's last ten and this section used before, and weighted by words the same ten come to 10.4, and both are published because a mean with its convention unnamed is a figure nobody can check. The word *span*, which the figures at §45 and at this item have carried without definition, is a measurement run: one pass of the instrument over the files, so a count of spans is the number of times the figure was measured and counts nothing that is on a page.** The longest bolded turn in the ten is seventy-seven words. **The mean is a consequence and not a plan, and no volume may read its own mean as a quality figure and no review may certify or fail a volume on one, and the two ends of this range are the two ends of the same reason: the lowest chapter has a person in it for about four lines and the highest is a kitchen in which two women talk for four hours.** **The count of paragraphs carrying neither speaker marker — definition: a paragraph carrying neither a quotation mark nor a bold mark, in the first person, that is not inside a speech opened and closed around it, which is a fault and not a statistic and must be zero: zero in all ten, and it is zero only under that first-person-restricted instrument, and the same ten return one hundred and eighty-six under the unrestricted reading that counts every plain narration paragraph, which is correct and is not a fault.** The exchange, a plain speech being a paragraph carrying a quotation mark and no bold mark and answered when the very next non-blank paragraph carries a quotation mark and a bold mark: **sixty plain and thirty-four answered, fifty-six point seven per cent, per chapter 8 of 5, 5 of 3, 5 of 2, 7 of 4, 8 of 4, 9 of 7, 8 of 3, 6 of 3, 2 of 2 and 2 of 1.** The chorus head, every occurrence of the string *about four people in*: **ninety-four in three hundred and ten paragraphs, one in three point three**, and a count is not a target. **Sentences of forty words or more — definition: a sentence of forty words or more on a file stripped of its heading line, its `---` rules and its bold marks, split into paragraphs on the blank line and into sentences after a full stop, a question mark or an exclamation mark and a space: two hundred and eleven, and none stands verbatim in more than one chapter of the batch, and the figure this item published when it was written was one hundred and five with eleven per-chapter numbers for ten chapters, and it is wrong by the same instrument that returns two hundred and seventy-three on Volume 07's last ten against that batch's published two hundred and seventy-three.** The longest uninterrupted run of speech paragraphs: **four, and the ceiling holds in all ten files.** Markdown bold parity is zero in all ten. **Words: twenty thousand eight hundred and fifty-four, and the three series this item now carries, the weekday tokens, the long sentences and the exchange, were all wrong when this section was written and all three are corrected above with the figure they replace named beside them, because the next prompt inherits them as authoritative and a prompt that inherits a wrong figure will not know it is wrong.** *Open.*

11. **AND NOTHING IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS IS A POWER.** The gains are a form on a table, four places read out in the order they are printed, a fourth place named and refused out loud in about four seconds, a man asked what a column is for and answering, a day in chalk on a slate and nothing under it, a fence finished at sixteen posts and eleven withies, a chalk that stayed in a pocket, a sheet under a stone at a well, a man who did not stop at a gate, and a man of about fifty-seven who said the cost of a page with his name at the top of it out loud in a room and was not told he was wrong. **None of them is a power. One of them is the price of a power. All of them are heavier than a power.** Adrian Vale performed no working, no threshold was opened, the Narrow Mark is not opened a third time, the other world is not named once, nobody offered him a workway and he asked for none, **the words *passage* and *privilege* are at zero across the ten files**, he is aged nowhere, and he is named in three of the ten — 0352, 0355 and 0360 — in a room reading a page in all three, **and the wall said nothing on all ten days.** *Open.*

## 47. Rules established in Volume 08, Chapters 0361–0370 — A HURDLE AND A BAR, A TASK AND A CONDITION, A NIB GROUND ON A STEP, TWO HUNDRED DAYS IN A COAT, AND A ROOM THAT DOES NOT SEND

**This section is the second of the sections §33B item 6 reserved for the Volume 08 batches, and it was written by Batch 0002, and by nothing else. §32 is not changed. §33 is not changed. §33A is NOT CHANGED, and a batch has not amended it and may not. §33B is NOT CHANGED, and item 8a's stale register of notices is still routed and is not this section's. §§34 to 46 are not changed. No calendar decision is made in this section and no form is registered in it, and the sixth form, *the Nth day of the Bare Month*, stands as §33A item 2 registered it, with no length stated, and this section states no length. The volume's one panel is fixed to day 393 and none of these ten chapters spends it.**

**The ten days are days 361 to 370, one chapter to one day, Chapters 0361 to 0370, and the run was recomputed from day 1 being a Tuesday and from nothing else before any prose was written: 361 Friday, 362 Saturday, 363 Sunday, 364 Monday, 365 Tuesday, 366 Wednesday, 367 Thursday, 368 Friday, 369 Saturday, 370 Sunday. The Bare Month days are the forty-sixth to the fifty-fifth, the Nth being day less 315. There is no double day in this batch and there is no double day in this volume. Thirty weekday tokens stand across the ten files, every one of them was read in the sentence it stands in against the day that sentence names, and six of them were unplaceable and were replaced with a day of the Bare Month in the same sentence before this section was written.**

1. **A MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-NINE IS ON A PAGE FOR THE FIRST TIME IN THIS MANUSCRIPT, AND HIS SECOND HANDLE IS INVENTED ON THAT PAGE, AND IT IS A HURDLE HE MADE AND A BAR HE REPLACED.** Chapter 0361, day 361, a Friday, the forty-sixth day of the Bare Month, at the head of the four-hundred-mile road out past the last named house. He has walked that road four times in nine years with a barrow. **He came up it about nine years back, found the bar on a gate at the head of it gone through at the bottom, cut a new one to the old one's pattern, and hung a hurdle on it. He has made four hurdles in nine years and hung two, and this is the second he has hung there. He has never once seen anybody on that road and he said so out loud to a man he met by accident.** **A HURDLE AND A BAR IS THE HANDLE, AND IT IS THE SAME IN CHAPTER 0370, WHICH IS THE ONLY OTHER CHAPTER HE IS IN, AND NO CHAPTER OF VOLUME 08 MAY ASK HIM ABOUT THE GATE OR ASK HIM WHAT FOUR FEET IS OFF.** *Open.*

2. **AND ONE WORDING THE PLAN PRINTED IS NOT ON ANY PAGE, BECAUSE THE CALENDAR AND THE PLAN CANNOT BOTH BE TRUE.** `outline/volume-08.md` §20.8's row for him says *in the ninth year of this flood*. **Day 370 is the three hundred and seventieth day of this flood and the month it stands in began on day 316 and has no stated length, so no chapter of this volume can print a ninth year of it, and the phrase is already on a page in this manuscript about a different man, where it is loose in the same way and is not a batch's to change.** Chapters 0361 and 0370 say **nine years back** and **nine years**, which is the figure the plan itself gives him, and no day, no month, no year and no descriptor moved.

3. **A MAN WAS ASKED A TASK AND SAID YES WITH A CONDITION STATED FIRST, AND THE CONDITION IS THAT HE WILL LEAVE NOTHING BEHIND HIM.** Chapter 0366, day 366, a Wednesday, the fifty-first day of the Bare Month, in the room over a market, in daylight, with a date in chalk on the door, about nine people in it, asked by the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register. The task: **walk it from the near end, to the stone, and back, and say whether a man standing at the other end of it would meet him or whether he would come out past the stone nobody put there.** The condition, given before anybody could put one on him: **he will not put his hands on it, he will not put a rod in it, and he will not leave a mark of any kind on it, not a stake and not a heap of stone and not a thing he could be found by.** **The room accepted it in about four seconds. He is not asked to go. He did not offer a day and was not asked for one. The rod did not come and no chapter of this volume has put a rod in anything.** *Open, and the condition is a trade and not a modesty and belongs to him.*

4. **THE TASK IS WHAT THE ROOM DOES, HE IS NOT IN THE ROOM, AND NOBODY SENDS FOR HIM.** Chapter 0370, day 370, a Sunday, the fifty-fifth day of the Bare Month, the same room, about nine people, from the seventh hour to the ninth. **About nine people agreed out loud and in about four seconds not to send for the one man the room is waiting for, and the reason was given by a man who has never been in a room in this city, and the reason is that a man who has been sent for is a man who has been sent for, and after that there is no way anybody standing at the far end of anything can find out whether he went or not.** The room spent the day doing the half of a task that is not its own, which is nothing. **Nobody was asked whether anything was going to go, and the room in which somebody is asked is the room on the sixtieth day of that month, which is five chapters past the end of this batch, and the notice for it goes out on the thirty-first day of that month and no chapter of this batch wrote one.** *Open.*

5. **THE FOUR PLACES WERE PUT IN A ROOM AND THE SECOND OF THEM WAS NAMED AS THE ONLY ONE THAT IS ABOUT PEOPLE AT ALL, AND NOBODY MARKED THE MOMENT.** Chapter 0363, day 363, a Sunday, the forty-eighth day of the Bare Month, the room over a market, a date in chalk on the door, about nine people, three of the four who have not left, and four places laid out on the bench for three of them. The woman of about twenty-one was asked **which of the four is a person**, and she answered: **none of the four, the second one counts them.** **Nobody in that room marked the moment, nobody thanked her, nobody asked her a second question, and the pen with the nib ground narrow went back into her apron with nothing on it. The fourth of the four is not in that room and is not going to be asked about anything by anybody, and a party of four is four, and no chapter of this batch counted the days since she said no, at any value, in narration or in a mouth.** *Open.*

6. **A PEN WITH THE NIB GROUND NARROW AND CUT DOWN WITH A KNIFE WAS GROUND BY THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-EIGHT, ON A STEP OUTSIDE A ROOM, AND HE SAID SO OUT LOUD IN A ROOM WITH A DATE ON THE DOOR, AND SHE ASKED HIM TWO WORDS AND HE ANSWERED IN ONE SENTENCE, AND SHE DID NOT THANK HIM.** Chapter 0367, day 367, a Thursday, the fifty-second day of the Bare Month. **He ground the first inch and a half of it in about a quarter of an hour, about a week before the forty-second day of that month, and has never said so to anybody, and said it because he had been asked and not because he had offered it, and a man who has been asked is not the same as a man who has offered it. The same chapter has a window on that side shutting properly, which he fixed with the same knife, and about nine people in four hundred miles know it and about nine of them are in the rooms he is in.** *Open, and the second handle is the nib, and no chapter after 0367 has said it and no chapter may improve on the two words she said about it.*

7. **A SHARE WITH THE POST TAKEN OUT WAS SAID OUT LOUD ON DAY 368 AND IT IS EXACTLY TWO HUNDRED DAYS OLD, AND BOTH ANCHORS ARE NAMED IN THE SENTENCE THAT PRINTS THE FIGURE, AND NOBODY ANSWERS IT.** Chapter 0368, day 368, a Friday, the fifty-third day of the Bare Month, on the boards of a salt wharf in a wind. The sentence, in a mouth: **a share with the post taken out has been in the inside of a coat in this city since the thirteenth day of the Long Month, which is the hundred and sixty-eighth day of this flood, and this is the fifty-third day of the Bare Month, and that is exactly two hundred days.** 168 + 200 = 368, **and only the Long Month makes the figure come out, and the chapter says the Long Month and not *that month*, because the thirteenth of an unnamed month is day 328 and not day 168.** **AND THE PAPER IS NOT THE SPEAKER'S. IT IS ADRIAN VALE'S, and §16.9 above and `state/character-state.md` both say so, and a chapter outranks a state file: it was offered to him on day 168, it has been in the inside of his coat since, he has taken it out once, and he has neither said yes nor said no. THE MAN WHO SAYS THE FIGURE OUT LOUD IS THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-EIGHT WHO KEEPS A TALLY, about a man who is not on those boards; he does not say how he knows and nobody asks him, and the man of about thirty-nine asks him one question and is told how long it has been there and not how he knows. The coat is not opened, the man it belongs to is not told, and Adrian Vale is in no chapter of days 361 to 370 in any form, which is the volume's own cast rule and not an accident.** The figure is unanswered, and the only figure in a mouth for it before this was fifty-eight, twice, in a chapter of Volume 05, and that was unanswered too. *Open, and this is the only one of the seven round-figure traps at `outline/volume-08.md` §14.6 that falls inside days 361 to 370; the other six fall at day 351, day 385 and four times at day 400, and no chapter of this batch printed any of them.*

8. **AND THE FENCE, THE BARE WALL AND THE SECOND BAR WERE ALL COUNTED OUT LOUD IN ONE BATCH AND THE FIGURES HOLD.** Chapter 0364, day 364, a Monday, the forty-ninth day of the Bare Month, on a shut road: **sixteen willow posts; nine in the ground on the thirty-third day of that month, a Saturday, with seven left in the cart; four more on the thirty-fifth, a Monday, which made thirteen; the last three on the thirty-eighth, a Thursday; eleven withies bound with the same cord.** 9 + 4 + 3 = 16, and the figure is printed identically to the two chapters that already carry it, and **the string *four feet* is at zero across all ten chapters of this batch, including in the two places where a height once stood and was taken out.** Chapter 0365, day 365, a Tuesday, the fiftieth day of the Bare Month, at a shut gate at the end of a lane: **a wall with the shape of nine boards on it and about four inches of dry stone at the foot of it, and the shape going; a bar of ash in two brackets; a brass key on a tarred cord; and, named from four streets off, one gate with two bars in it of which the second is on no roll at all, and a roll that has a gate on it and a keeper on it and a set of days and nothing else.** **The two gates were not put side by side in the chapter and the chapter says so, and four feet is not a measurement and appears nowhere in these ten chapters, and the fence is not joined to the mark and no distance between them is printed.** *Open.*

9. **ABOUT FOUR HUNDRED AND FORTY PEOPLE ON ELEVEN MILES OF FLATS MAKE A THING THAT CANNOT LEAVE THIS CITY, AND THE SECOND OF THE FOUR PLACES IS THE ONLY ONE OF THE FOUR THAT IS ABOUT PEOPLE AT ALL, AND NOTHING WAS SENT.** Chapter 0369, day 369, a Saturday, the fifty-fourth day of the Bare Month, in the room over a market, with a date in the door and about nine people in it. **Nothing has come off the boards of the wharf in a cart since before the water, the stack at the top end is higher than a man, and a man with a tally-board has been cutting it off a barrow every morning of this flood and putting it on a pile that cannot be got off the wharf by anybody, and he has not said so and nobody has asked him.** **The figure the man of about thirty-eight sends is not the figure he has, and the figure he has is on no page and is printed in none of these ten chapters and in no file this batch produced, and the chapter does not print either figure as a column.** *Open.*

10. **AND TWO THINGS THESE TEN CHAPTERS DID TO THE HOUSE, AND NEITHER IS A POWER.** A woman of about thirty-four lost her voice before the light on the forty-seventh day of that month and the man beside her, who has a piece of chalk he does not use, said her number for her about nine times over the morning, and her voice came back at about the second hour of the afternoon and she said it herself and he stopped in the middle of a number. **Her belief that the day she stopped was the day it went came out wrong that day, and she has not said so and is not going to, and a chapter may not use it and may not improve on it.** And a man counted a fence out loud in front of a man who cannot move it and has not tried to move it, and said out loud, to the fence, that it is the only thing anybody has put up in this flood that is going to be here after everybody who made it, **and nobody asked him where the fence is, and nobody told him, and the man who put it up said sixteen is right and asked nothing, and the new rope is still new.** *Open.*

11. **AND THE THINGS THESE TEN CHAPTERS DID NOT DO, WHICH A LATER WRITER HAS TO KNOW ARE STILL UNTOUCHED.** The word on the second slate is at zero in all ten files, and no chapter of the ten gives the mark in the orchard a word by any route, and none of the three sentences this manuscript has used about that word is in a mouth in any of them. **The mark was not measured, dug, counted, probed or resolved, nobody went and looked at it, no rod went into anything, and the fence was not joined to it and no distance between them was printed.** The day-154 sentence is not said, restated, paraphrased or improved on. The question of day 346 is not answered. **Nobody was asked whether any of the four will go, and no chapter of the ten counted the days since the woman of about sixty-one said no at any value in narration or in a mouth, and the four are four people and not a company and not a body and the word stays out of this volume.** **The two women of twenty-nine are in no chapter of these ten, in any form, and the arrangement that holds it, which is on a page in Chapter 0358, held without anybody touching it, and nothing in these ten settles whether they are one woman and nothing may.** The consent fracture is not mended in any of them, nobody apologises, the fifth condition is not given, and the fracture is visible in these ten only as a room with a man in it and a folded sheet of road in an apron and nobody in it. The case is shut and the schedule is not filled in and is not the subject of any of the ten. The barrow of boards at the shut gate and the barrow of salt at the wharf are two barrows and are not joined, **and the three barrows a batch gets are three chapters' barrows and are not joined either, and the barrow of boards has no value after the thirty-second day of that month.** The keeper of the Redroot gate is in none of the ten files, her days are not counted and her year is not named. The Regent is nine hundred miles away and is not defeated, and the nine sentences about a return are not said, restated, paraphrased or improved on in any of the ten. The ninth line is untouched and is not thanked. The store's board is nine miles up the upper road and was not moved into a room. The box is shut, the island is not owned, and the room at the far end of the Narrow Mark is unlooked at. *All open.*

12. **AND THE HOUSE FIGURES FOR THESE TEN DAYS, WITH THE DEFINITIONS BESIDE THEM, AND NO TARGET SET ON ANY OF THEM.** **The share of words inside bold marks, heading line included, `---` rules counted, bold marks counted as nothing, file split on whitespace: 6.0, 11.7, 7.4, 12.6, 22.5, 11.9, 19.8, 11.4, 24.9 and 9.2 per cent, a range of 6.0 to 24.9 and a mean of 13.7 unweighted and 13.6 weighted by words, in eighteen thousand two hundred and seventy words — **AND THESE ARE THE POST-REPAIR FIGURES, and the five per-chapter figures they replace and the mean of 12.9 are named at `state/batch-summaries/volume-08-batch-0002.md` §3 item 1, because a silent correction is the third version of the same fault.** The longest bolded turn in the ten is **eighty-five words, in Chapter 0367**, against the working ceiling of about ninety and the hard one of ninety-five at `outline/volume-08.md` §17 item 4. **The mean is a consequence and not a plan, and no volume may read its own mean as a quality figure, and no review may certify or fail a volume on one, and the two ends of this range are the two ends of the same reason: the three chapters at the top are the three whose subject is a person answering in a room with a date on the door, and the two at the bottom are the two whose subject is a man with his hands on something.** The count of paragraphs carrying neither speaker marker — definition: a paragraph carrying neither a quotation mark nor a bold mark, in the first person, that is not inside a speech opened and closed around it, which is a fault and not a statistic and must be zero: **zero in all ten.** The exchange, a plain speech being a paragraph **beginning** with a quotation mark and carrying no bold mark and answered when the very next non-blank paragraph also begins with a quotation mark and carries a bold mark — **and the word *beginning* is in the definition because the looser instrument, which counted a narration paragraph containing a quotation mark as a speech, returned a figure that did not reproduce: fifty-three plain and forty answered, seventy-five point five per cent, per chapter 5 of 2, 5 of 4, 3 of 3, 5 of 3, 8 of 7, 5 of 2, 6 of 4, 4 of 3, 7 of 7 and 5 of 5, and the fifty-three of thirty-nine this section first published is withdrawn.** The chorus head, every occurrence of the string *about four people in*: **forty-seven in two hundred and fifty-one paragraphs, one in five point three, and it is at zero in Chapters 0361, 0364, 0365 and 0368, and a count is not a target. The paragraph figure is published on both definitions in use in this repository — the file split into paragraphs on the blank line, and every non-blank line with the heading lines and the `---` rules removed — and both return 251 on these ten files, because no paragraph in them runs to more than one line.** Sentences of forty words or more — definition: a sentence of forty words or more on a file stripped of its heading line, its `---` rules and its bold marks, split into paragraphs on the blank line and into sentences after a full stop, a question mark or an exclamation mark and a space: **one hundred and seventy-four, and none stands verbatim in more than one chapter of the batch.** The longest uninterrupted run of speech paragraphs, a speech paragraph being one that begins with a quotation mark: **four, and it holds at four in six of the ten and the ceiling is not exceeded in any, and the three runs that were over it while the batch was being written, at five, six and five, were all broken with a narration beat and not one with a cut speech.** **THE WORD-RUN WAS RUN TWICE, once with each file's own lead paragraph subtracted and once with the leads NOT subtracted, and the subtraction was hiding a forty-word run in three lead paragraphs, which is the fault `bible/power-system.md` §36.1 exists to stop; both runs now return a top of twenty-five.** Twenty-five is the top on the published instrument, and every one of the eight highest runs is a declared motif of this volume: the form with three of its four places in a condition and one in a decision, the condition stated first, the fourth of the four not being asked about anything, the man who carries nine things and opens none, and the man who walks the road four times in nine years. Markdown bold parity is zero in all ten. *Open.*

13. **AND NOTHING IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS IS A POWER.** The gains are a hurdle and a bar at the head of a road, a number said by somebody else for four hours, a question about a column answered and not marked, a fence counted aloud, a bare wall losing a shape, a second bar on no roll, a task and a condition and nothing left behind him, a nib ground on a step, three barrows and two of them belonging to nobody, a figure of exactly two hundred days in the inside of a coat, four hundred and forty people making a thing that cannot leave a city, and a room deciding out loud not to send for one man. **None of them is a power. One of them is the price of a power. All of them are heavier than a power.** Adrian Vale performed no working, no threshold was opened, the Narrow Mark is not opened a third time, the other world is not named once, nobody offered him a workway and he asked for none, **the words *passage* and *privilege* are at zero across the ten files**, he is aged nowhere, and **he is in no chapter of these ten in any form, which is six of the seven chapters `outline/volume-08.md` §4 names him in, and the seventh is 0371 and it is the next batch's, and the wall said nothing on all ten days.** *Open.*

## 48. Rules established in Volume 08, Chapters 0371–0380 — A SHEET WITH NOTHING AT THE TOP OF IT, A FOURTH PLACE NOBODY WENT TO FIND OUT, A QUESTION ASKED ONCE, A YARD, AND TWO FOUR-PLACE FORMS KEPT APART

**This section is the third of the sections §33B item 6 reserved for the Volume 08 batches, and it was written by Batch 0003, and by nothing else. §32 is not changed. §33 is not changed. §33A is NOT CHANGED, and a batch has not amended it and may not. §33B is NOT CHANGED, and item 8a's stale register of notices is still routed and is not this section's. §§34 to 47 are not changed. No calendar decision is made in this section and no form is registered in it, and the sixth form, *the Nth day of the Bare Month*, stands as §33A item 2 registered it, with no length stated, and this section states no length. The volume's one panel is fixed to day 393 and none of these ten chapters spends it.**

**The ten days are days 371 to 380, one chapter to one day, Chapters 0371 to 0380, and the run was recomputed from day 1 being a Tuesday and from nothing else before any prose was written: 371 Monday, 372 Tuesday, 373 Wednesday, 374 Thursday, 375 Friday, 376 Saturday, 377 Sunday, 378 Monday, 379 Tuesday, 380 Wednesday. The Bare Month days are the fifty-sixth to the sixty-fifth, the Nth being day less 315. There is no double day in this batch and there is no double day in this volume. Forty-four weekday tokens stand across the ten files, every one of them was read in the sentence it stands in against the day that sentence names, and every one of them is correct.**

**AND TWO FIGURES THIS BATCH WAS INHERITED WRONG, AND THE CORRECTION IS THE POINT OF THE FIRST ONE.** **THE NOTICE IN CHAPTER 0371 IS THE SIXTH OF THE VOLUME'S SEVEN AND A PROMPT THIS BATCH INHERITED CALLED IT THE SEVENTH.** The register of the seven is at §33A item 7, at §47 item 5, at `outline/volume-08.md` §14.4 and §20.10, and at `state/batch-summaries/volume-08-batch-0002.md` §11, and all five give **351, 354, 359, 362, 366, 371 and 389 for rooms on 355, 358, 363, 366, 370, 375 and 393.** A notice on any other day is a three-day instrument and a three-day instrument does not fail, it arrives warm, and **a batch that has written one of seven notices as the seventh has produced a chapter with every figure in it correct and a count of the volume's instruments in it that is wrong by one.** And **`outline/volume-08.md` contradicted itself about Adrian Vale in the midpoint: §4 gives the full list of the chapters of this volume he is in and 0375 is not on it, while §8 said he was in that room. A chapter follows §4, which is the cast rule, and Chapter 0375 has no Adrian in it in any form. CLOSED IN THE PLAN BY THE VOLUME 08 BATCH 0003 REVIEW REPAIR PASS ON 2026-09-27: `outline/volume-08.md` §8 now states that §4 governs, that Chapter 0375 has no Adrian in it, and that the claim is withdrawn — corrected in the plan itself and not only in a state file.**

1. **THE SIXTH NOTICE WENT OUT ON DAY 371, FOUR DAYS, FOR THE ROOM ON DAY 375, AND THE PRICE OF THE MISSING NAME WAS SAID OUT LOUD BY THE WOMAN WHO WRITES THEM.** Chapter 0371. The sheet was ruled into two by hand because there is no form in four hundred miles with a room on it, folded twice with a thumb, and carried a day, a place and nothing else. **Its own morning and the room's day stand in one sentence with the count: this morning is the fifty-sixth day of the Bare Month, a Monday, and the room it is for is on the sixtieth day of that month, a Friday, and that is four days counted from this morning including this morning.** The reason there is no name at the top of it, in her own mouth, is that if you come to that room on that day then afterward there is nothing whatever in four hundred miles that can say you were there. *Open, and **no name is at the top of any of the seven and none may be added, and the price of that is that nobody can do anything for you.***

2. **A MAN SAID OUT LOUD THAT HE HAS NEVER FILLED IN ONE OF FOUR PLACES IN A FORM IN ABOUT NINE YEARS AND HAS NEVER GONE TO FIND OUT HOW, AND NOBODY ASKED HIM.** Chapter 0372. He has filled in the first, second and third four times a year since the winter before the water out of what he is given; the fourth has never been filled in; **he has assumed since the winter before the water that it is a share of something, and that a share of something has to come out of a household, and he has never gone to a household; he said he is not going to and is not going to be asked.** *Open, and **the fourth place is a household's and the household has still not been asked for its days by anybody, and the woman of about fifty-two is on no page of these ten chapters.** The man of about thirty-eight **is not asked anything in any of these ten chapters**, and the reason the fourth place is empty is now a man and not a household, which is a new reason and not a better one.*

3. **THE MIDPOINT IS A QUESTION, AND THE ANSWER IS ON NO PAGE, AND A ROOM ASKED A PERSON SOMETHING INSTEAD OF DECIDING WHAT TO PUT ON A SHEET.** Chapter 0375, day 375, a Friday, the sixtieth day of the Bare Month, the room over a market, in daylight, with a date in chalk on the door, about nine people. **Tamsin Quill, at a bench with the folded sheet of road in her apron, asked the woman of about twenty-one, four feet off her with a pen in her apron, whether it will go — two words, once, in the local language, in front of about nine people, and she did not ask it twice. NOBODY IN THAT ROOM ASKED HER A SECOND QUESTION AND NOBODY AFTER THAT MORNING IS GOING TO BE THE SECOND ONE.** Nobody filled a column, nobody produced a document and nobody named a place. The man of about thirty-four was at the back with his hand flat on his own inside breast pocket; the man of about thirty-nine was at the back where the light does not reach and **said nothing at the point where a room runs out of forms**; the second slate was on the shelf behind the table with a date on it and nothing under it and nobody went near the shelf. **THE WOMAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO KEEPS A PUBLIC REGISTER WAS NOT IN THAT ROOM: the question was asked at about the eighth hour, she came up to the step at the back with no name on it at about the tenth hour and did not come in and stood there until about the fourth hour of the afternoon, and the two women of about twenty-nine were about four paces apart and said nothing to each other, and the two of them are never in one room on a page and nothing in this volume settles whether they are one woman and nothing may.** The fourth of the four is not in this city and is not going to be asked about anything by anybody, **and no chapter of these ten counted the days since she said no at any value in narration or in a mouth.** *Open, and the answer is not on a page in this manuscript and is not in this section.*

4. **A YARD, A PERSON IN IT WHO IS NOT THE DIGGER, AND A MAN WHO WORKED A THING OUT AND WAS NOT TOLD HE WAS RIGHT.** Chapter 0376, and it is the one exception `outline/volume-08.md` §4 names. **Adrian Vale is in this chapter and in Chapter 0371 and in no other of these ten, in any form.** The person in the yard is a woman of about sixty-nine with an empty tin in both hands which she did not open, and the man of about fifty-two who digs is not in that yard and nobody in it is going to ask him anything. He watched about five hours, worked out the order nine things are done in, said it out loud in order, **and was told he had one of the nine the other way round and was not told which one, and the reason she gave was that a man who is told has it right tomorrow and a man who is not told will have it wrong tomorrow and will think about it, and the chapter does not say he was right.** *Open, and **there is no form in four hundred miles with a place for the order things are done in, he is the only person in this city who has it, and nobody asked him to write it down.***

5. **THE TWO FOUR-PLACE FORMS WERE COUNTED OUT LOUD IN A ROOM, EIGHT PLACES, AND NOT PUT SIDE BY SIDE, AND THE WORD IN THE THIRD PLACE OF ONE OF THEM WAS NAMED BY NOT BEING SAID.** Chapter 0378, and **it is the only chapter of Volume 08 that may do this.** A page with four places under a heading and a docket with four places in it, and not one of the eight the same as any of the other seven. **THE THIRD PLACE IN THE CASE IS A WORD NOBODY IN THIS CITY HAS SAID OUT LOUD IN A ROOM IN ABOUT NINE YEARS, AND THE MAN WHO SAID THAT ABOUT IT HAS NOT SAID IT AND IS NOT GOING TO, AND `holder` IS AT ZERO ACROSS ALL TEN OF THESE FILES AND IS NOT DEFINED IN A MOUTH BY ANYBODY AND IS NOT DEFINED IN THIS SECTION.** A roll at a gate has a gate on it, a keeper on it and a set of days, and is not in that room and is not going to be brought into it, and **the two rolls of the same gates are not put side by side and no chapter says what the difference between them is worth.** The case is not opened, the schedule is not filled in, not withdrawn and is not the subject of the chapter. *Open.*
