6. **A CREASE IN A SHEET OF ROAD HAS GONE SOFT, AND IT IS THE ONLY THING ABOUT THAT PAGE THAT HAS CHANGED, AND IT IS HERS AND IT IS NOT ON THE PAGE.** Chapters 0373 and 0380. One sheet, about nine places, folded in four with the same four creases in the same four places. **She said the reason out loud, and the reason is that a crease is a thing about her hands and not about the paper, and that a person who keeps a page in an apron is going to be asked about the page one day and is not going to be asked about the fold.** In Chapter 0380 the sheet goes on a bench in a room in front of about nine people and is given to none of them, and she says out loud that the about nine places on it and the about nine people in the room are two nines and are not the same nine, and that it is not going to anybody and not into a form. *Open, and **it is one sheet and it is Tamsin Quill's and it is not the sheet thirty years old in a tin, and the two are never in one room and no chapter of this volume un-conditions the one in the tin.***

7. **NINE ROWS AT THE BACK OF A REGISTER THAT NOBODY CAN DATE WERE LOOKED AT AND WERE NOT COPIED, AND THE REASON IS ON A PAGE.** Chapter 0379. The register has three columns, the first for what went out, the second for what came back, and **the third has been empty on purpose since a Thursday in that month and is still empty and nobody was asked about it.** The nine rows are in an instrument this city stopped writing in years ago; **she has not looked at them since the eighth day of that month, which is fifty-six days back from the sixty-fourth, and the figure stands in the sentence that names the day it is measured from.** A copy has to be dated to be of any use, and not one of the nine can be dated, and **a page that cannot be dated is the only kind of page in this city that no office nine hundred miles off can ask to have produced.** *Open, and the register line with a day on it and nothing under the day is day 391 and is entered in Chapter 0395 and is not this batch's.*

8. **AND THE THINGS THESE TEN CHAPTERS DID TO THE HOUSE.** **A ROOM ARRANGED THE LENGTH OF A BENCH AND DECIDED OUT LOUD, IN ONE BREATH, NOT TO SAY WHAT IT IS FOR**, on the reason that a room which says what it is for has to be right about it and there is no instrument in four hundred miles that can tell a person afterwards that they were not sent for the right thing — Chapter 0374. **A ROOF CAME DOWN BY HAND IN ABOUT A QUARTER OF AN HOUR IN A SQUALL AND A FIGURE WENT BACK TO THE WOMAN WHO OWNS IT**, said out loud by her before the man beside her got to it, and he stopped — Chapter 0377. **AND NINE FEET, WHICH IS THE MANUSCRIPT'S OWN IDIOM AND NOT A MEASUREMENT, STANDS ELEVEN TIMES ACROSS THESE TEN CHAPTERS AND IS NOT A HEIGHT OF ANYTHING IN ANY OF THEM.** *Open.*

9. **AND THE THINGS THESE TEN CHAPTERS DID NOT DO, WHICH A LATER WRITER HAS TO KNOW ARE STILL UNTOUCHED.** The word on the second slate is at zero in all ten files and no chapter of the ten gives the mark in a field a word by any route, and none of the three sentences this manuscript has used about that word is in a mouth in any of them. **The day-154 sentence is not said, restated, paraphrased or improved on, and a line drafted into Chapter 0375 about the day after a name goes at the top of a page was taken out again before this section was written, because the man who is right about it in this volume is the man of about thirty-nine in Chapter 0393 and that chapter is not this batch's.** The question of day 346 is not answered, and the woman of about twenty-one has not been made to start waiting to be told what she is for again. **Nobody was asked whether any of the four will go except the one person, once, on day 375.** The fence is not in these ten chapters and no chapter of the ten prints a post, a withy or a total. **No barrow was spent on anything; the one occurrence of the word in the ten is a denial, and the three barrows with the three who go are Chapter 0382's.** The town four hundred miles inland is not entered and the page the copy was made from is not fetched. The keeper at a shut gate was not asked anything in any of these ten chapters and her brass key is not in any of them, and **the man of about fifty-seven is not asked where the fence is and is not told and is not defeated.** The ninth line is not moved, dated, read aloud, taken down, thanked for or resolved. **The Bare Month did not end and no chapter of the ten printed a day after the sixty-fifth, said the month was nearly over, said what it is a month of, or attached *length*, *long* or *short* to it, and the month's name is printed once or twice in every one of these ten chapters against once in ten chapters for the volume's first twenty, which is a gain in legibility and costs one phrase and changes nothing that is decided.** *Open.*

10. **AND THE HOUSE FIGURES FOR THESE TEN DAYS, WITH THE DEFINITIONS BESIDE THEM, AND NO TARGET SET ON ANY OF THEM.** **The share of words inside bold marks, heading line included, `---` rules counted, bold marks counted as nothing, file split on whitespace: 12.24, 9.10, 11.92, 16.52, 9.57, 7.05, 5.11, 19.96, 9.20 and 13.15 per cent, a range of 5.11 to 19.96, a mean of 11.38 unweighted and 11.47 weighted by words on 15,837 words** — against Volume 01's 10.4 as the model and about 35 as the ceiling, Batch 0001's 5.14 to 13.57, and Batch 0002's 5.93 to 24.23. **These are the figures as the review repair pass left them and the first edition was 10.91, 7.98, 10.64, 14.80, 8.30, 6.26, 4.48, 17.58, 8.23 and 12.11 on 17,543 words: four of the ten moved up and none moved down, because the repair cut narration and cut no speech, and both are published because a silent correction is the third version of the same fault.** **The mean is a consequence and not a plan, no volume may read its own mean as a quality figure, and no review may certify or fail a volume on one.** The longest bolded turn in the ten is **eighty-five words**, against the working ceiling of about ninety and the hard one of ninety-five at `outline/volume-08.md` §17 item 4, **and eight of the ten chapters were written with at least one turn over ninety and every one of the eight was repaired with a plain-speech interjection from the other voice and a narration beat and not one with a cut sentence.** The longest run of consecutive speech paragraphs is **two in nine of the ten and three in Chapter 0379**, within the ceiling of four at `outline/volume-08.md` §17 item 6. The count of paragraphs carrying neither speaker marker — definition: a paragraph carrying neither a quotation mark nor a bold mark, in the first person, that is not inside a speech opened and closed around it, which is a fault and not a statistic and must be zero: **zero in all ten.** The exchange, a plain speech being a paragraph **beginning** with a quotation mark and carrying no bold mark, answered when the very next non-blank paragraph also begins with a quotation mark and carries a bold mark: **twenty plain speeches and twenty answered, seventy-six point nine per cent.** Long sentences of forty words or more: **143, and none of them stands verbatim in more than one chapter of the batch.** The word-run, run across the thirty files of the volume with the lead paragraphs **included**: the top is 62 between Chapters 0355 and 0363 and all three highest pairs are between files this batch did not write; **the highest run involving any of these ten is thirty-three words and it is the room-lead frame, and the maximum inside the ten is thirty-three, both re-measured after the repair pass and both unchanged by it.** The chorus frame, on both instruments, and **this is where a review found the real fault and it is a fault in the instrument and not in the number**: on the declared string *about four people in*, **fifteen in one hundred and ninety-eight paragraphs, one in 13.2, at zero in five of the ten**; on *about four people* followed by any word, **thirty-two in the same one hundred and ninety-eight, one in 6.2, at zero in none**. **THE FIRST EDITION OF THIS SENTENCE PUBLISHED SEVENTY AND ONE HUNDRED AND FIVE, ONE IN 2.8 AND ONE IN 1.9, AND BOTH FIGURES WERE CORRECT AND BOTH WERE WORTHLESS, because every one of those instances was a paraphrase of the sentence immediately before it and the frame ran sixteen consecutive times in Chapter 0371 and twenty-three times in Chapter 0375.** **A chorus frame may carry an observation into a paragraph and it may not restate one the paragraph has already made; the rule is at `outline/volume-08.md` §17 item 11a and it is a rule about sentences and not about a number, and no card of this volume ever set a density and none is set now.** The full definitions, the per-chapter lists and the four figures that changed while the batch was being written are at `state/batch-summaries/volume-08-batch-0003.md` §§3 and 8, and the repair is at its §11. *Open.*

11. **AND NOTHING IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS IS A POWER.** The gains are a sheet with nothing at the top of it and the price of that said out loud, a man saying he has never filled in one of four places in about nine years, a room deciding not to say what it is for, a question asked once, the order nine things are done in, a roof down by hand and a figure handed back, eight places across two documents and not one of them the same as another, nine rows nobody can date, a crease gone soft, and two nines in a room that are not the same nine. **None of them is a power. One of them is the price of a power. All of them are heavier than a power.** Adrian Vale performed no working, no threshold was opened, the Narrow Mark is not opened a third time, the other world is not named once, nobody offered him a workway and he asked for none, **the words *passage* and *privilege* are at zero across the ten files**, he is aged nowhere, **the figure *about two hundred rooms* is used in neither of his two chapters and the volume's rule forbids it in both, and he is named in Chapters 0371 and 0376 and in no other of them, in any form.** **And the wall said nothing on all ten days.** *Open.*

---

## 49. Rules established in Volume 08, Chapters 0381–0390 — A PRICE FOR NOT ASKING, THREE BARROWS AT THE TOP OF SOME BOARDS, A FIGURE SAID OUT LOAD ON A ROAD, AND THE SEVENTH AND LAST SHEET

**The ten days are days 381 to 390, one chapter to one day, Chapters 0381 to 0390, and the run was recomputed from day 1 being a Tuesday and from nothing else before any prose was written: 381 Thursday, 382 Friday, 383 Saturday, 384 Sunday, 385 Monday, 386 Tuesday, 387 Wednesday, 388 Thursday, 389 Friday, 390 Saturday. The Bare Month days are the sixty-sixth to the seventy-fifth, the Nth being day less 315. There is no double day in this batch and there is no double day in this volume. Forty-three weekday tokens stand across the ten files, every one of them was read in the sentence it stands in against the day that sentence names, and every one of them is correct.**

**THIS PHASE RESUMED FROM A CHECKPOINT WITH THE TEN FILES ALREADY ON DISK, so its work on them was a repair pass and not a first draft. That is recorded here and in the batch record and in the chapter summaries, because a phase that resumes mid-run and then publishes one set of figures is the third version of the failure this repository names. Ten arithmetic and reference faults, five bolded turns over the working ceiling, thirty-six repetitions of a narration frame, four instances of the *about four seconds* device and five runs of four consecutive speech paragraphs were repaired. No day, no weekday, no month day, no notice, no form, no object count, no anchor and no cast member moved, and both editions of every figure that moved are at `state/batch-summaries/volume-08-batch-0004.md` §9.** **AND THEN THE REVIEW OF THIS BATCH RAN AND REPAIRED SIX MORE FAULTS ON TOP OF THAT PASS, and the fact belongs in this section and not only in the batch record, because this section is the volume's permanent witness and a volume that publishes one pass and hides the second is the third version of the same fault a third time: a figure in Chapter 0381's own room gloss belonged to a different man, three chapters ran their hours backwards, the third of them the same sentence the review had already caught twice and the one a rule obliges a repair pass to check in all ten and not only in the two the reviewer opened, Chapter 0382's governing exchange named no speaker in it, a phrase in Chapter 0387 was the only occurrence of itself in forty files, two tenses in Chapter 0382 had been swapped by the repair above and a prophecy in the same chapter announced the next section, and one figure in this item and in the batch record said six of ten where the files say five. Both editions of every figure those six moved are at `state/batch-summaries/volume-08-batch-0004.md` §9.11 and the pages are described at its §11, and the six moved the bolded share of Chapter 0382, the two means, the word count, the paragraph count, two chorus denominators, two long-sentence figures and four object occurrences, and they moved nothing else. No day, no weekday, no month day, no notice, no form, no object count that holds, no anchor, no cast member and no decision of record moved in that pass either.**

1. **THE SEVENTH AND LAST NOTICE WENT OUT ON DAY 389, FOUR DAYS, FOR THE ROOM ON DAY 393, UNSIGNED, WITH NOTHING AT THE TOP OF IT, TO A PLACE AND NOT A HOUSE, AND ITS OWN MORNING AND THE ROOM'S DAY STAND IN ONE SENTENCE WITH THE COUNT.** Chapter 0389, the seventy-fourth day of the Bare Month, a Friday. **This morning is the seventy-fourth day of the Bare Month, a Friday, and the room it is for is on the seventy-eighth day of that month, a Tuesday, and that is four days counted from this morning including this morning.** The register of the seven is at §33A item 7, at §47 item 5, at `outline/volume-08.md` §14.4 and §20.10, and it is **351, 354, 359, 362, 366, 371 and 389 for rooms on 355, 358, 363, 366, 370, 375 and 393**, so 389 is the seventh and the batch prompt's correction of its own predecessor is correct. **She said out loud, in the local language, standing, with the sheet in her hand, that that is the seventh, that there is not going to be an eighth in this flood, that she has known that for about a week and has not said it out loud before this morning, and that a person who is going to write one more ought to say out loud that it is the last one while there is still somebody in the room to hear it.** *Open, and **no name is at the top of any of the seven and none may be added, and the seventh has gone and there is not going to be an eighth in this flood.***

2. **A PRICE FOR NOT ASKING, GIVEN OUT LOUD BY THE PERSON WHO PAYS IT, IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINE PEOPLE, AND THE THING BEING PROTECTED IS A PAGE NOBODY HAS SEEN.** Chapter 0381. **The woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register said out loud that she was not going to ask Adrian Vale what was in the inside of his coat, that she has known there was a page in it since the first day of that month, and that the not-asking has cost her the same as asking would have — because a page in a coat is the only page in four hundred miles that cannot be asked for, and if she asks him for it then it stops being that, and then there is nothing in four hundred miles that a person could put a name to and not be able to produce.** Nobody in that room thanked her for it and she had not asked to be thanked. **He is in this chapter and in no other of these ten, in any form, and he reads a page that is not his, and `outline/volume-08.md` §4 is the cast rule and §4 governs.** *Open.*

3. **THREE BARROWS ARE AT THE TOP OF SOME BOARDS, ONE OF THEM FOR EACH OF THE THREE PEOPLE WHO ARE GOING, TWO OF THEM HAVE BELONGED TO NOBODY SINCE BEFORE THE WATER, AND A BARROW THAT WILL NOT GO UP WET GOES UP ON ITS SHAFT AND THE SHAFT TAKES THE LOAD INTO WHOEVER IS HOLDING IT.** Chapter 0382, a Friday, sideways rain. **The three are the three of Chapter 0368 and one of them is the man of about thirty-eight's; no fourth barrow exists in these ten chapters and none went down a road, because day 391 is not this batch's.** The man of about thirty-four took one up on his right hand and could not close that hand again until he put it in cold water about a quarter of an hour before the light went; he told nobody, and the man of about thirty-eight knew and did not say that he knew. The handcart with a hazel mallet whose head is bound in cord is the man of about sixty's and is not one of the three, **and the cord on that mallet is the same cord that is bound round the eleven withies on the fence, which is on a page at `outline/volume-08.md` §20.10, and Chapter 0382 says in one clause that this is a fact about a piece of cord and is not joined to anything.** *Open.*

4. **THE SECOND NUMBER IS ON A PAGE AND THE FIGURE IS NOT, AND THE OWNER SAID OUT LOUD ON A WHARF, IN THE OPEN, THAT HE IS NOT GOING TO SAY WHICH OF THE TWO HE BELIEVES.** Chapter 0383, a Saturday. **About four hundred and forty, said in the man of about thirty-eight's own mouth on his own boards, having said it at a stall for about a month to nobody in particular; and a second number that has never been on a page in this city, is not going to be on one, and is not printed in this chapter or in any other of these ten.** He said it on a wharf and not in a room, and gave the reason out loud: a thing said in a room stays in the room and a thing said on a wharf goes up the river. **A chapter of this volume may not print the number he sends and the number he has as two figures and may not print the second one at all.** *Open.*

5. **THIRTY-NINE MEN UNDER THE NEAR GATE ARE EXACTLY THREE HUNDRED DAYS UNPAID ON DAY 385, MEASURED FROM THE EIGHTY-FIFTH DAY OF THIS FLOOD, AND THE FIGURE WAS SAID OUT LOAD IN THE OPEN BY A MAN WHO DOES NOT TRADE RATES.** Chapter 0385, a Monday, the seventieth day of the Bare Month. 385 − 85 = 300, and this is the trap at `outline/volume-08.md` §14.6, and the sentence that prints the figure names **the first of March, the eighty-fifth day of this flood, the three hundred and eighty-fifth day of this flood, and the anchor twice.** **It is the first figure anybody in four hundred miles has ever attached to those thirty-nine men, it is on nobody's page, and there is no place in this city for it, and a figure said out loud in the open is the only kind of figure that cannot be got back off anybody.** A board by that gate carries a ruled line at the bottom of a column, ruled with three strokes, gone over never, empty since the first of March, and **there is a man under that gate who could have filled it — the man of about thirty-four, who writes a line on a board for a living and writes it last and in a harder piece of chalk — and nobody in this flood has ever asked him about it, and he did not fill it, and the hardest chalk a man has is the chalk he has decided against.** *Open, and **no chapter of this volume may print three hundred next to those men without naming the day it is measured from, and may not print the number of days unpaid at all unless it names that day.***

6. **THE FOUR PLACES UNDER A HEADING WERE READ OUT LOUD IN THE LOCAL LANGUAGE IN ONE ROOM, AND THE FOURTH ONE IS A SHARE AND A SHARE IS A HOUSEHOLD'S, AND THE HOUSEHOLD WAS NOT ASKED AND IS NOT GOING TO BE.** Chapter 0384, a Sunday. **A district, a population, a rota, and a share, in that order, out loud, in daylight, with the day in chalk on the door, and the fourth place goes out empty and goes out correct — and the cost of that was said out loud in the same room, and the cost is that the reason the fourth place is empty is a woman on a step and not a column, and no office nine hundred miles off is ever going to be able to tell the difference.** The woman's refusal on a step is shown standing and is not reopened, and her days are not asked for a second time. **A chapter of this volume may not print a fifth place under that heading, and no name goes in the fourth place of any of them, and the Crown's schedule is a different four-place form and is never laid beside it, and the word *holder* is at zero across all ten of these files and is not defined in a mouth by anybody.** *Open.*

7. **A PAGE WAS OFFERED IN THE OPEN AND REFUSED, AND THE WOMAN WHO OFFERED IT SAID OUT LOUD THAT THE REFUSAL WAS OF A THING SHE HAD ALREADY DECIDED AND NOT OF A THING SHE HAD PUT IN FRONT OF THE OTHER ONE.** Chapter 0386, a Tuesday, the bottom step of nine steps. **The sheet of road was put down on the step, on the boards, in the open, in front of one person, and it is the third time in this flood it has been opened in front of anybody and the first time in front of one person, and the woman of about twenty-one said no standing, before the fourth crease was open, and she was not waiting for it.** Tamsin Quill's reason, out loud, on the step: she did not ask whether the other woman wanted it, she had never sent for anybody's nine places, she did it anyway, and the woman she did it to is the one who has to carry the having done it. **The two women of twenty-nine are in this chapter and in no other of these ten and are never in one room in any of them.** *Open.*

8. **A FIGURE WENT BACK TO THE PERSON WHO OWNS IT AND A MAN SAID OUT LOUD ON A LANE THAT HE HAS NOT GOT ANYTHING THAT IS HIS TO PUT ON A WALL.** Chapter 0387, a Wednesday. **The piece of chalk is the same one and the full form of its date — the forty-third day of the Short Month — is printed here and in four other chapters of this batch and is not shortened to a relative phrase in any of the ten; the man of about twenty-five has nine words he wrote himself in the inside of his coat and did not say them in a lane, and the two of them have now got a wall between them and neither of them has used it.** The woman of about fifty at the shut gate wrote a date on the post, did not rub anything out, and **said four words out loud to the post, and the four of them were a date, and they were not for either of the two men, and she has never once said a date to a person.** *Open, and **the fence is not in this batch's range and no chapter of these ten prints a post, a withy or a total.***

9. **A ROOM AGREED OUT LOUD THAT WHAT THREE PEOPLE TAKE OUT OF IT WILL NOT BE WRITTEN DOWN, AND A BLANK IS THE ONLY THING IN FOUR HUNDRED MILES A RETURN CANNOT BE FILLED IN FROM.** Chapter 0390, a Saturday, the seventy-fifth day of the Bare Month. **A room over a market spent the whole morning doing nothing and nobody in it decided that, and the woman of about twenty-one was asked a different question by a different person — *What will you carry* — and answered *What I am standing in*, and nobody asked her a second question of any kind and nobody ever is going to.** The agreement, out loud, in about four seconds, is that nobody is going to write down what the three of them are carrying, not on a form and not in a book and not on a slate, **and the reason given is that that is the only way any of them is going to be able to say afterwards that they were not sent.** *Open, and **day 391 is not this batch's and the three have not walked.***

10. **AND THE THINGS THESE TEN CHAPTERS DID NOT DO, WHICH A LATER WRITER HAS TO KNOW ARE STILL UNTOUCHED.** The word on the second slate is at zero in all ten files and no chapter of the ten gives the mark in a field a word by any route, and none of the three sentences this manuscript has used about that word is in a mouth in any of them. **The day-154 sentence is not said, restated, paraphrased or improved on, and the man who is right about it in this volume is the man of about thirty-nine in Chapter 0393 and that chapter is not this batch's.** The question of day 346 is not answered; it is put in front of the same silence twice in these ten chapters — a woman refusing a page on a step, and a woman answering a question about luggage — and answered in neither, and **the woman of about twenty-one has not started waiting to be told what she is for.** The days since the fourth of the four said no were not counted at any value in any of the ten. The ninth line is not moved, dated, read aloud, taken down, thanked for or resolved, and the board at the top of the upper road has not been written on since day 225. The keeper at a shut gate was not asked anything and **the man of about fifty-seven is not asked where the fence is and is not told and is not defeated.** The town four hundred miles inland is not entered and the page the copy was made from is not fetched. **The Bare Month did not end and no chapter of the ten printed a day after the seventy-fifth, said the month was nearly over, said what it is a month of, or attached *length*, *long* or *short* to it, and the month's name is printed once or twice in every one of these ten chapters.** No chapter of the ten gets anybody stronger, and **about two hundred rooms is at zero across all ten of these files and is forbidden in the one of them Adrian Vale is in.** *Open.*

11. **AND THE HOUSE FIGURES FOR THESE TEN DAYS, WITH THE DEFINITIONS BESIDE THEM, AND NO TARGET SET ON ANY OF THEM.** **The share of words inside bold marks, heading line included, `---` rules counted, bold marks counted as nothing, file split on whitespace: 8.23, 8.73, 19.01, 15.67, 9.33, 22.94, 8.67, 15.98, 12.62 and 8.54 per cent, a range of 8.23 to 22.94, a mean of 12.97 unweighted and 12. **AND NOTHING IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS IS A POWER.** The gains are a price for not asking, said out loud by the person who pays it; nine steps divided between two men who had never asked each other anything; three barrows at the top of some boards and a hand that took one of them up on the right; a number said in the owner's own mouth in the open and a second number announced and refused; a share read out loud and found to be a household's and a column that goes out empty and correct; a figure said aloud on a road that cannot be got back off anybody; a page offered in the open and refused; a chalk that was not taken out of a pocket and a wall nobody wrote on; a seventh sheet made and not sent, and a seventh sheet sent; and a blank where a column would go. **None of them is a power. Some of them are the price of a power. All of them are heavier than a power.** Adrian Vale performed no working, no threshold was opened, the other world is not named once, nobody offered him a workway and he asked for none, **the words *passage* and *privilege* are at zero across the ten files**, he is aged nowhere, **and he is named in Chapter 0381 and in no other of them, in any form. And the wall said nothing on all ten days**, because the volume's one panel is fixed to day 393. *Open.*

## 50. Rules established in Volume 08, Chapters 0391–0400 — A NAME SAID ALOUD AND NOT PRINTED, A PAGE THAT WENT OUT CORRECT, A DAY WITH NOTHING UNDER IT, A QUESTION NOBODY ANSWERED, AND A FENCE

**The ten days are days 391 to 400, one chapter to one day, Chapters 0391 to 0400, and the run was recomputed from day 1 being a Tuesday and from nothing else before any prose was written: 391 Sunday, 392 Monday, 393 Tuesday, 394 Wednesday, 395 Thursday, 396 Friday, 397 Saturday, 398 Sunday, 399 Monday, 400 Tuesday. The Bare Month days are the seventy-sixth to the eighty-fifth, the Nth being day less 315. There is no double day in this batch and there is no double day in this volume. Sixty-four weekday tokens stand across the ten files, every one of them was read in the sentence it stands in against the day that sentence names, and every one of them is correct.**

**This is the last batch of Volume 08 and its ten chapters carry the resolution, the new question and the last image, and nothing in this section is a decision. A batch may not make, amend or extend §32, §33, §33A or §33B, and this section amends none of them, and it decides no calendar and no form. Where these ten chapters settled nothing, this section says so and writes less.**

1. **A NAME WAS SAID OUT LOUD IN DAYLIGHT IN A ROOM WITH A DATE ON THE DOOR, IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINE PEOPLE, AND WAS WRITTEN DOWN NOWHERE — AND THAT INCLUDES THIS FILE.** Chapter 0392. The man of about thirty-four said a woman's name once, said the price of it out loud before he said it, and **no page in this city has it and there is not going to be one, and the name is not printed in the chapter that says it, in the chapter the page goes out of, in the batch record, or in this section.** **THIS IS A DECISION THE BATCH TOOK AND IT IS THE FIRST TIME THIS MANUSCRIPT HAS PUT A NAME AT THE TOP OF A PAGE AND NOT PRINTED THE NAME IN THE REPOSITORY THAT PRINTED THE PAGE, and the reason is the volume's own: the worst thing anybody in this flood does to anybody is write a name at the top of a piece of paper, and a record that printed the name would be doing it too. It is the same protection the notary's sentence bought in Chapter 0316 and the same protection the words spoken in Chapter 0357 bought, applied here to a different thing. A later phase may reverse it and may not do it silently.**

2. **THE RETURN WENT OUT ON ITS DATE, CORRECT, WITH A NAME AT THE TOP OF IT, AND NO DOCUMENT IN THIS VOLUME IS DEFEATED.** Chapter 0393. Four places under a heading and no fifth: a district with nowhere to start and left so, about four hundred and forty in the second place in the man of about thirty-eight's own hand, a rota empty on purpose, and a share that is a household's and goes out empty. **The figure he sends is not the figure he has, the figure he has is on no page, and no chapter of this volume prints it. The man of about thirty-nine is right, once, in one turn of speech, and nobody told him he was wrong and nobody told him he was right, and the page went anyway.** *Open.*

3. **THE WALL SAID ONE THING ON DAY 393 AND WAS REFUSED IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS IN SIX WORDS BY THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-EIGHT, AND THE SIX WORDS ARE ON THE PAGE ONCE.** > Nobody has to be told what a form is for, / and a form that has been filled in without that / has been filled in correctly.

   **I answered, and nobody had to.** The cost is specific: he was asked in a room in daylight on the forty-second day of that month what a column is for, he answered in his own words, he has not been asked anything since and has not wanted to be, and the wall has now told a room of about nine people that he did not have to be asked at all. **Nobody improved on the six words and nobody thanked him. The panel is on a page once and is not going to be said again in this flood, in a mouth or out of one, and the wall said nothing on the other forty-nine days of this volume.** *Open, and **the count of this city's silences is 292 at day 392, is 292 at day 393 because day 393 is not a silence, and is 299 at day 400, and it is derived, on no page, in no mouth, and printed in no chapter.***

4. **THE REGISTER CARRIES ONE LINE WITH A DAY IN IT AND NOTHING UNDER IT, AND THE DAY ON THAT LINE IS DAY 391.** Chapter 0395. A day in the first column, the second column empty, the third column empty on purpose, and nothing else entered at all. **Both days stand in one sentence with the count: this morning is the eightieth day of the Bare Month, a Thursday, and the day in that line is the seventy-sixth day of that month, a Sunday, and that is four days counted back from this morning and not counted forward. No name went under that day. Nobody went near the shelf, and the second slate stayed on it with a date on it and nothing under it on all ten days of this batch.** This is the second time this book has entered a register and the first time it has entered it about people, and **a blank is the only thing in four hundred miles a return cannot be filled in from, and a clerk nine hundred miles off cannot ask a city to produce one.** *Open.*

5. **THE QUESTION ABOUT THE FENCE WAS ASKED ONCE, TO A ROOM, AND WAS NOT ANSWERED, AND IT WAS NOT ASKED OF EITHER OF THE TWO MEN IT IS ABOUT.** Chapter 0396. **Who is a fence for.** Asked by the woman of about sixty-nine, in a room, in daylight, with a date on the door, to the room and not to anybody. **Nobody answered it. About four of the nine had an answer and about four of them said nothing, and the answer four of them had was a fence, and the chapter says so in the next line.** **The man of about fifty-seven and the man of about sixty were both in that room and neither of them was asked, and the man of about fifty-seven is not asked where the fence is, is not told and is not defeated, and whether the fence works or does not work is not settled in this volume.** The last question she asked in that room was on the thirty-first day of the Bare Month, a Thursday, which is fifty days back, and it was not answered either, and the rhyme is deliberate and is not improved on. *Open, and **a later volume may ask it again and may not answer it.***

6. **THE LAST IMAGE IS A FENCE, AND IT IS SIXTEEN POSTS AND ELEVEN WITHIES, AND THE ROAD GOES AWAY UNDER IT.** Chapter 0400, the eighty-fifth day of the Bare Month, a Tuesday, and **the eighty-fifth is the last day of that month any chapter of this volume may print, and no chapter of this volume may attach a meaning to it, or to the sixtieth, or to the seventieth.** The three who went down the road on the seventy-sixth day of that month were still on it. **NONE OF THE FOUR ROUND FIGURES `outline/volume-08.md` §14.6 PLACES ON DAY 400 IS PRINTED IN CHAPTER 0400 — not the bag on the boards, not the board's unwritten run, not the trestle count, and not the four who have not left — and the chapter does not mention any of the four anchors, because a chapter that prints none of them is not required to mention the anchor at all and the last image of a volume is a better chapter for printing none of them.** *Open.*

7. **AND THE THINGS THESE TEN CHAPTERS DID NOT DO, WHICH A LATER WRITER HAS TO KNOW ARE STILL UNTOUCHED.** The word on the second slate is at zero in all ten files and no chapter of the ten gives the mark in a field a word by any route. **The day-154 sentence is not said, restated, paraphrased or improved on in any of the ten, and the man who is right about it in this volume is the man of about thirty-nine in Chapter 0393 and that is the only appearance this volume permits.** The question of day 346 is not answered and is put in front of the same silence twice in these ten days and answered in neither, and **the woman of about twenty-one has not started waiting to be told what she is for.** The days since the fourth of the four said no were not counted at any value in any of the ten. The ninth line is not moved, dated, read aloud, taken down, thanked for or resolved, and the board at the top of the upper road has not been written on since day 225. The keeper at a shut gate was not asked anything. The town four hundred miles inland is not entered and the page the copy was made from is not fetched. **The Bare Month did not end and no chapter of the ten printed a day after the eighty-fifth, said the month was nearly over, said what it is a month of, or attached *length*, *long* or *short* to it, and every one of the ten names the month in full at least once.** No chapter of the ten gets anybody stronger, **Adrian Vale is in Chapter 0392 and in no other of them, in any form, and *about two hundred rooms* is at zero across all ten files and is forbidden in the one of them he is in**, and the words *passage* and *privilege* are at zero across the ten files. *Open.*

8. **AND ONE FIGURE THIS BATCH'S OWN PROMPT GAVE THAT WAS WRONG, WHICH A LATER WRITER MAY NOT INHERIT BY ACCIDENT.** The prompt stated that the man of about thirty-nine is at about nine rooms on day 393. **Measured on the files, the run is four at the back of a room on days 372 and 375, a sixth time on 379, six on 380, eight on 381, nine on 384, ten on 388 and eleven on 390, and `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0390.md:35` prints eleven in a fortnight on day 390, three days before 393. A figure that goes down from eleven to nine over three days is a subtraction taken across the wrong boundary, and a page outranks a plan. NO ROOM COUNT IS PRINTED FOR THAT MAN IN ANY CHAPTER OF DAYS 391 TO 400, and the three chapters of those ten in which he is in a room use the second handle instead. A later writer who needs his counter goes to the page and not to a plan, and the prompt's line is withdrawn at `state/batch-summaries/volume-08-batch-0005.md` §6 item 2.**

10. **AND NOTHING IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS IS A POWER.** The gains are a name said out loud and not written down, a page that went out correct about the wrong people, a man right about a column and not thanked, a refusal in six words of a rule that took a man's answer off him, a man saying out loud what a column costs and who pays it, a day in a register with nothing under it, a question about a fence answered by nobody, a page given to nobody, a roof, a hand on a wall, and three barrows going away under a fence. **None of them is a power. Some of them are the price of a power. All of them are heavier than a power.** Adrian Vale performed no working, no threshold was opened, the other world is not named once, nobody offered him a workway and he asked for none, **the words *passage* and *privilege* are at zero across the ten files**, he is aged nowhere, **and he is named in Chapter 0392 and in no other of them, in any form. And the wall said nothing on nine of the ten days and the volume's one panel is spent.** *Open.*

## 51. THE CALENDAR FOR DAYS 401 TO 450 — decided by the Volume 08 close phase, and it is a NEW SECTION and NOT AN AMENDMENT OF §33A OR §33B

**This section was written by the Volume 08 close phase, which is the fifth phase in this repository permitted to decide, and it is the calendar for Volume 09. §§1 to 32 are not changed. §33 IS NOT CHANGED, and its five forms, its weekday anchors, its fourth-form amendment and its interval table all stand exactly as the Volume 06 outline phase wrote them. §33A IS NOT CHANGED BY THIS SECTION, and every one of its eight items stands, including item 1's rule against an ordinal for a month, item 2's refusal to state a length, item 5's anchors, item 6's count of silences, item 7's register of notices and item 8's standing interval rule. §33B IS NOT CHANGED BY THIS SECTION, and every one of its nine items stands, including item 4, which records that the Bare Month does not end inside Volume 08, and item 6, which is the rule about a form that comes onto a page. §§34 to 50 are not changed, and §§46 to 50 are the five Volume 08 batch sections and they are the volume's permanent witness. §§52 onward are RESERVED for the Volume 09 batches and for the Volume 09 outline phase's own decisions, if it takes one, and this section is not one of them. Every day figure in it is derived from the two founding figures and from nothing else. A BATCH MAY NOT MAKE, AMEND OR EXTEND ANY OF IT.**

1. **VOLUME 09 IS FIFTY DAYS ON FIFTY DAYS, DAYS 401 TO 450, ONE CHAPTER TO ONE DAY, NO DOUBLE DAY.** The form is ***the Nth day of the Bare Month***, **the Nth being day less 315, which is the sixth form and is the one registered at §33A item 2 and named on a page in Chapter 0316 by Sera Vail, a notary of fifty, twenty-six years a notary, and in no other chapter and in no other file.** **Day 401 is the eighty-sixth day of the Bare Month and day 450 is the hundred and thirty-fifth.** The range is a decision, and a later volume that wants a double day has to argue for it in its own outline against the rule that is now the default, exactly as Volume 04's is the recorded exception and is not reopened. **A chapter of Volume 09 may not write another chapter's day and may not write a day before the eighty-first or after the hundred and thirty-fifth of that month.**

2. **A WEEKDAY IS A FIGURE, DAY 1 IS A TUESDAY, AND EVERY WEEKDAY IN DAYS 401 TO 450 IS DERIVED BY ARITHMETIC AND NEVER FROM A NEIGHBOURING CHAPTER, A CARD, A DAY MAP OR A STATE FILE.** **Day 400 is a Tuesday, on the founding rule and on the page of Chapter 0400, and therefore day 401 is a Wednesday. Day 450 is a Wednesday, because 450 − 401 = 49 = 7 × 7 and a day seven multiples of seven after another is the same weekday, so both ends of the volume are Wednesdays, and no chapter may take that as a permission to derive a middle day from either of them.** The run below was built from day 1 and from nothing else and **was validated before it was published by recomputing days 351 to 400 out of the same two founding figures and reproducing §33B item 3's repaired table at ZERO MISMATCHS on all fifty rows, and by recomputing days 301 to 350 and reproducing §33A item 4's table at zero mismatches on all fifty rows.**

   | the return went out on its date | day 393 | 8 | 57 | the day is, in Chapter 0393 |
   | the register line with nothing under the day | day 391 | 10 | 59 | the day is, in Chapter 0395, and the day on the line is day 391 |

   **AND THE STANDING RULE, WHICH IS THE ONE THIS REPOSITORY KEEPS BREAKING, carried forward unchanged from §33A item 8: every value at a chapter boundary is derived; a derived value may be used in a chapter that dates it and may not be attributed to another chapter or to a mouth; where two elapsed counts sit in one sentence the event each is measured from has to be named in that sentence; where a figure has two plausible anchors, both are named in the same sentence; and a figure that comes out at exactly half of the right one is a subtraction taken across the wrong boundary, which is how *sixteen days* survived in three places for a slate that carried the word for thirty-two.**

4. **AND EIGHTEEN ROUND FIGURES FALL INSIDE DAYS 401 TO 450 AND ALL EIGHTEEN ARE TRAPS, AND ALL EIGHTEEN ARE NAMED HERE SO THAT NO BATCH WALKS INTO ONE. A figure that comes out round has been checked twice and not once.** The table below has eighteen rows and the heading says eighteen because a heading that says fifteen over eighteen rows is a figure a reader cannot check, which is the fault this repository exists on. **Seventeen of the eighteen are multiples of fifty, and the eighteenth is the one multiple of twenty-five that looks rounder than it is and it is named at the end.**

   | The day | The figure | The anchor it is measured from | The trap |
   |---|---|---|---|
   | **401** | the posting is **two hundred days** old | day 201 | exactly round, and it is on the volume's first page |
   | **401** | the case has stood on a table **a hundred and fifty days** | day 251 | exactly round, on the volume's first page |
   | **401** | the return came up the road **fifty days** ago | day 351 | exactly round, on the volume's first page, and three round figures on one day is the most dangerous page in this range |

   | **425** | the board has stood unwritten **two hundred days** | day 225 | exactly round, and the nearer of the two middle days of the range, and the board nobody has written on is the only instrument this book has that has not lied to anybody |
