   **AND A CHAPTER THAT PRINTS ANY OF THESE NAMES THE DAY IT IS MEASURED FROM IN THE SAME SENTENCE. A chapter that prints none of them is not required to mention the anchor at all, and Volume 08's last page printed none of the four that fell on it, which is the better chapter.**

5. **THE BARE MONTH DOES NOT END INSIDE VOLUME 09, AND THIS SECTION DOES NOT GIVE IT A LENGTH, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE OF THE DECISION.** The reason is the notary's sentence and not a figure: **Chapter 0316 is a page on which Sera Vail, a notary of fifty, names the Bare Month and declines to say how long it is and says why, and the reason is hers, and the protection that bought is that no later volume can inherit an end by accident.** A volume that ended the Bare Month on a chapter boundary would print the end of a month whose length was refused, in the second volume to inherit that refusal, and it would have to do it in one of two ways: **by a sentence in a state file, which is the fault this repository has been repairing for four volumes, or by a chapter that guesses, which §33A item 2 forbids.** So Volume 09 writes the eighty-sixth to the hundred and thirty-fifth day of the Bare Month and stops. **A hundred and thirty-five days is the longest stretch any volume in this series has spent inside one month, and no chapter of Volume 09 may say the month is long, and no chapter may say it is short, and no chapter may convert the hundred and thirty-fifth into a span, and no chapter may attach a meaning to any of the four figures this section bolds in its table above.**

6. **AND THE CONSEQUENCES, WHICH BIND EVERY CHAPTER OF DAYS 401 TO 450. The only two instruments Volume 09 uses for a date are a count of days of this flood and a day of the Bare Month with its own form, and the count is a figure a writer can check and the day of the month is a subtraction from 315, which is the cheapest date in this book and the only one nobody has to check twice.** A chapter may write an ordinal for any day of that month from the eighty-sixth to the hundred and thirty-fifth. A chapter may not write a day after the hundred and thirty-fifth. **A chapter may not say the month is nearly over, may not say what it is a month of, may not print *a hundred and thirty-five days* or *four months* or *since the month began* as a figure, and may not say what the month does at its end, how many days it has, or whether it is long or short. The words *length*, *long* and *short* applied to the month are at zero across the fifty chapters of Volume 07 and across the fifty chapters of Volume 08 and stay at zero.**

7. **AND THE RULE FOR WHOEVER COMES AFTER, WHICH IS THE POINT OF DECIDING IT HERE AND IS THE SAME RULE §33B ITEM 6 GAVE THE VOLUME THAT FOLLOWED THIS ONE. A seventh form exists the moment the end of the Bare Month is on a page, and it comes onto a page the same way the sixth one did:** a room, in daylight, with a date on the door, by a person, who says the cost out loud first and is refused by somebody before she does it, and who then declines to say how long the new month is and says why. **The Short Month was made that way in Chapter 0256 and the Bare Month was made that way in Chapter 0316, by the same notary of fifty, and the reason it is restated here is that this is now the third volume to inherit the refusal and the cost of restating it is four lines.** A calendar is not a decision an outline takes and a batch guesses. **It is a decision a person makes in a room, and every form in this book is one, including the ones in this file.** If a later volume needs a seventh form and has not got the end of the Bare Month on a page, it writes inside the Bare Month and **it may not declare the month over.**

8. **THE MONTH MAP IS UNCHANGED BY THIS SECTION AND IT IS THE ONLY THING A DAY MAP MAY BE BUILT ON.** The first month is days 1 to 25, the second 26 to 90, the third 91 to 155, the fourth 156 to 255 and is the Long Month, the fifth 256 to 315 and is the Short Month, and the sixth is 316 onward and is the Bare Month, **of no stated length, and this section changes nothing about any of them. NO CHAPTER OF THIS MANUSCRIPT WRITTEN FROM DAY 301 ONWARD MAY USE AN ORDINAL FOR A MONTH** — not *the second month*, not the third, not the fourth, not the fifth, not the sixth — **and that rule is forward-looking and it is not a verdict on the three hundred chapters already written, no page of which may be re-written.** The one of the seventy-seven existing uses that is load-bearing is a panel at `chapters/volume-04/chapter-0152.md:79` reading *The third month is the Long Month*, a page in a finished volume, is left standing, and is named here for the fourth time so that a later phase does not mistake the rule for a repair it is still owed. *March* is a month and is never a date number. **Never** *of this month*, *of the month*, *of next month*, *of this new month*, or a form like *the last day of the month before this one*.

9. **AND THE COUNT OF SILENCES, WHICH IS THE ONE DERIVED FIGURE THAT CANNOT BE GOT BY ADDING, AND IT IS PUBLISHED HERE BECAUSE A LATER WRITER WILL ADD IT WRONG.** The rule is one addition for every day the wall says nothing. One hundred and fifty-two is on a page at `chapter-0250.md` and is the figure at day 250. The wall said something on day 256, which spent Volume 06's one panel, on day 340, which spent Volume 07's, and on day 393, which spent Volume 08's, **and a day the wall says something on is not a silence.** **Two hundred and ninety-nine at day 400, three hundred at day 401 if day 401 is a silence, and three hundred and forty-nine at day 450 less one for every day inside the range on which the wall says something, and Volume 09 has one panel to spend and has not decided its day.** **The figure is for the state layer and is not for a chapter, and no chapter of Volume 09 prints it.**

10. **AND FOUR THINGS THIS SECTION DOES NOT DO, published because a close phase that only lists what it decided is half a record. It does not amend §32, §33, §33A or §33B, and it amends none of them. It does not move a day, a weekday, a month day, an anchor, a form, a notice, an object count or a cast member of Volume 08, all of which are on a page and not in this file. It does not decide the Volume 09 line, which is the outline phase's, and the candidate this phase audited is at `outline/series.md` under *DECISIONS OF RECORD, VOLUME 09* and is audited and not adopted. And it does not give the Bare Month a length, an end, a meaning, or a seventh form, and the only thing it says about the end of that month is that it is a thing a person does in a room, in daylight, with a date on the door.**

## 52. Rules established in Volume 09, Chapters 0401–0410 — A SECOND SHEET UP THE ROAD, A PAGE WITH A PLACE FOR A PERSON IN IT, A ROOM THAT WAS NOT SUMMONED, AND THE ORDER OF THE FOUR PLACES

**The ten days are days 401 to 410, one chapter to one day, Chapters 0401 to 0410, and the run was recomputed from day 1 being a Tuesday and from nothing else before any prose was written: 401 Wednesday, 402 Thursday, 403 Friday, 404 Saturday, 405 Sunday, 406 Monday, 407 Tuesday, 408 Wednesday, 409 Thursday, 410 Friday. The Bare Month days are the eighty-sixth to the ninety-fifth, the Nth being day less 315. There is no double day in this batch and there is no double day in this volume. Thirty-one weekday tokens stand across the ten files, every one of them was read in the sentence it stands in against the day that sentence names, and every one of them is correct.**

**This is a batch section and it is the volume's first one. Nothing in it is a decision. A batch may not make, amend or extend §32, §33, §33A, §33B or §51, and this section amends none of them, and it decides no calendar and no form. §51's fifty rows and twenty-one anchors are unchanged and every day figure below is derived from the two founding figures and from nothing else.**

1. **A SECOND SHEET CAME UP THE ROAD ON DAY 401, OUT OF THE SAME OFFICE NINE HUNDRED MILES OFF, ON THE SAME QUARTERLY ROUND, IN THE SAME SATCHEL THE RETURN CAME IN FIFTY DAYS EARLIER, AND IT WAS PUT ON A TABLE IN A ROOM OVER A MARKET BY A MAN WHO CARRIES THINGS FOR A LIVING AND WAS NOT OPENED BY HIM.** Chapter 0401. **THE THREE ROUND FIGURES `bible/power-system.md` §51 ITEM 4 NAMES FOR DAY 401 ARE PRINTED NOWHERE IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS — not the two hundred days, not the hundred and fifty, not the fifty — and the chapter that carries the volume's first document prints none of them, which is the better chapter, and it names day 351 in the sentence that says the return came up the road.** The man has now carried seven things in this flood with somebody else's writing on them — five in a coat, a return in a satchel on day 351, and this sheet — **and has opened none of the seven, and the last of them is the last sheet of paper in this flood that has gone past him.** The sheet has a heading and four places ruled under it and **one crease pressed with a bone down the third of them, and the crease is the only thing on that page anybody in this city has ever touched.** *Open.*

2. **THE SHEET WAS READ OUT ON DAY 405, IN THE LOCAL LANGUAGE, IN DAYLIGHT, WITH A DATE IN CHALK ON THE DOOR, IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINE PEOPLE, IN THE ORDER THE FOUR PLACES ARE PRINTED ON, STOPPING AT EACH ONE LONG ENOUGH FOR THE PEOPLE AT THE BACK TO HEAR IT, BY THE WOMAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO KEEPS A PUBLIC REGISTER, WHO SAID OUT LOUD WHAT IT WAS FOR BEFORE ANYBODY ASKED.** Chapter 0405. **THE THIRD OF THE FOUR PLACES IS A PERSON, AND IT IS THE FIRST PAGE IN THIS FLOOD THAT HAS EVER ASKED ANYBODY TO PUT THEMSELVES ON IT.** **Nobody filled anything in. The sheet is not filled in and is not sent, it did not go back anywhere and it did not come back, and all four of its places were empty when it came up the road and all four were empty when the reading finished.** The day on the door was written in chalk before anybody came up the nine steps and not written after. *Open.*

3. **AND THE PRINTED ORDER OF THE FOUR PLACES IS A PLACE, A LINE, A PERSON, AND A DAY — AND THE PLAN ENUMERATES THEM IN A DIFFERENT ORDER, AND THE ENUMERATION IS A LIST OF THE FOUR THINGS THE FORM HAS AND NOT OF THEIR PRINTED ORDER.** **THIS IS THE ONE FINDING IN THIS SECTION AND IT IS PUBLISHED BECAUSE A BATCH THAT SILENTLY PICKS A SIDE OF A CONTRADICTION IN ITS OWN PLAN IS DOING THE THING THIS FILE EXISTS ON.** `outline/volume-09.md` and `outline/batches/volume-09-batch-0001.md` enumerate the four as *a place, a line, a day, and a person*, and in nine separate places state that **the third of the four places is a person**: §1, §6.7, §8 (the third place, the one with a person's name in it, is left empty), §10 (the third column, the one with a person in it), §12, §19.5, the object inventory at §20.6 (one fold down the third column, the third column empty on all fifty days), the day-425 decision, and the batch prompt's own second item. **A list of four that puts a day third and a person fourth cannot be a list of what is printed on the page in the order it is printed, and every one of those nine statements puts a person third.** Chapters 0402 and 0405 put the four on a page in the order set out here and Chapter 0405's heading says the third of them is a person. **THE OUTLINE IS NOT AMENDED, because a batch may not amend an outline, and the contradiction stands in the outline; the full statement with the reasoning is at `state/batch-summaries/volume-09-batch-0001.md` §3.11 and in the newest blocks of the four state files, and the next four batches of this volume inherit the resolution and not the contradiction.** **And the standing rule at `outline/volume-09.md` §16.5 holds and is not weakened: a chapter that reorders the four places has got a different document, and no chapter of these ten prints a fifth place under that heading.** *Open, and **a later volume's four-place forms are three and no two of them are the same form, and none of them is ever laid beside another.***

4. **THERE IS NO EIGHTH NOTICE IN THIS FLOOD AND THESE TEN CHAPTERS WROTE NONE, AND EVERY ROOM IN THEM IS A ROOM ABOUT NINE PEOPLE WERE ALREADY IN.** The page is `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0389.md:35` and this section does not restate it. **WHAT IS LOST WITH THE NOTICE IS NAMED ONCE ACROSS THE TEN CHAPTERS, IN PLAIN NARRATION, IN CHAPTER 0401, AND IN NO MOUTH: the four days are the cooling, and the cooling is the time a room has in which to find out what it is being asked.** The word *cooling* stands three times in the batch and all three are in that chapter. **Two other chapters refer to the same absence in other words and use neither the sentence nor the word**, and there is therefore no eighth notice in this volume and no room in it was summoned and every day on a door in it is still written in chalk. *Open.*

5. **AND THE TWO MEN WHO ARE RIGHT ARE RIGHT IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS, AND THEY ARE TWO MEN ON TWO DAYS, AND NEITHER IS THANKED.** Chapter 0406, in the room over a market, is the man of about thirty-nine who trades on a board, and he is the point of view, and what he says is four words and it is true, and nobody in that room told him he was right and nobody told him he was wrong and the chapter does not say he was right. Chapter 0408, on the boards of a salt wharf, is the man of about thirty-eight at the wharf, and he is the point of view, and the man of about thirty-nine is on those boards for about an hour as the marked voice and is not the man who is right that morning, and nobody thanked him there either. **IN BOTH CHAPTERS THE MAN WHO IS RIGHT IS A PLAIN SPEECH AND IS UNMARKED, and each card declares that departure from the house pattern in advance, and the beat stands once in each of the two and twice in no chapter.** **The day-154 sentence is not said, restated, paraphrased or improved on in any of the ten, and a chapter may not be read as having said it by the fact that a man is right.** *Open.*

6. **AND THE TWO MEN THIS BATCH PUT IN TWO CHAPTERS EACH AND ASKED NOTHING OF EITHER.** The man of about fifty-seven is in Chapters 0402 and 0410, **was asked nothing in either, is not told where the fence is, does not move the fence, does not touch the rope, and in Chapter 0410 he stands at a fence along the top of a shut road out of this city and says four things, all four of them to a barrow and none of them about where the fence is, and the length of new rope is over the back of a chair in a room in this city and is nowhere near that road and has still never been used.** The man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall is in Chapters 0407 and 0409, **was asked nothing in either, was thanked in neither, has a piece of chalk in his own inside breast pocket that has not been taken out since the forty-third day of the Short Month, which is day two hundred and ninety-eight, and the full form of that day is printed at full length in Chapter 0407 and Chapter 0409 names the chalk with no date at all rather than shortening it, and the ninth line is not moved, not dated, not read aloud, not taken down and not resolved in any of the ten, and whether it works stays unanswered.** *Open.*

7. **AND THE FIGURE THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-EIGHT CARRIES IS NOW ON A PAGE IN BOTH OF ITS HALVES AND NEITHER HALF IS PRINTED.** Chapter 0408 stands the fact that the figure he sends is not the figure he has, **and prints no number at all, neither the one that is on a page nor the one that is on no page, and the object inventory at `outline/volume-09.md` §20.6 lists Chapter 0408 among the chapters that will name the first figure and this chapter deliberately does not, and the reason is published in the batch record.** The one figure in this volume that is on a page for the flats is therefore on no page in these ten chapters, which is the first time in four volumes that a figure a man sends four times a year is absent from a chapter about the page he sends it on. *Open.*

8. **AND THE REGISTER AND THE SECOND SLATE.** The second slate is on its shelf in five of the ten chapters and is **read where it lies in two of them, in Chapter 0402 and in Chapter 0405, and is never picked up, never turned over and never written on, and it carries a date and nothing under the date.** **The register's third column has been empty on purpose and is empty on purpose after these ten days, and no chapter of the ten entered a line in that book, and the line entered on day 443 is a later chapter's.** The register-keeper is the one who has kept it empty, she said so out loud in that room once, and **she has never been asked about it and this batch did not ask her about it and did not put her earlier sentence in a second mouth.** *Open.*

9. **AND THE THINGS THESE TEN CHAPTERS DID NOT DO, WHICH A LATER WRITER HAS TO KNOW ARE STILL UNTOUCHED.** The word on the second slate is at zero in all ten files and the common noun is not used of anybody in any of them, and **no chapter of the ten gives the mark in a field a word by any route, measures it, digs it, counts it, probes it or resolves it, and no chapter goes and looks at it and no chapter joins it to the chapel, to the boards under the chapel or to the two holes nine feet apart.** **The shut road is out of this city and no chapter of the ten puts it above this city or at the top of it, the fence is along the top of that road, and the lane of nine houses is at the foot of that road and is not in this city, and Chapter 0410 says all three of those things in its own words.** The fence is sixteen willow posts and eleven withies and the count is printed once in the batch, in Chapter 0410, in a form that names the three days of this flood and not three Bare Month ordinals, **because §51 item 1 puts this volume's Bare Month range at the eighty-first day and after and the three days in question are before it.** The day-154 sentence is not said in any of the ten. The questions of day 346, day 396 and day 445 are not asked in any of the ten and are not answered. **The days since the fourth of the four said no are not counted at any value in any of the ten, in narration or in a mouth, and a party of four is four and the fourth is not on that road.** Nobody in this city can be shown one person on the four-hundred-mile road and the town four hundred miles inland is not entered, described from inside, or brought anybody back from. The keeper of the Redroot gate is not named, not counted and not used; the woman of about fifty at a shut gate was not asked anything; the girl of seventeen is not fetched; the box is shut, the island is not owned, nobody picks the bag up, nobody counts the boards under the chapel and Break is not open. **The Bare Month did not end and no chapter of the ten printed a day after the ninety-fifth, said the month was nearly over, said what it is a month of, or attached *length*, *long* or *short* to it, and every one of the ten names the month in full at least once.** **No chapter of the ten gets anybody stronger, Adrian Vale is in Chapters 0402 and 0405 and in no other of them in any form, and *about two hundred rooms* is at zero across all ten files, no chapter of the ten prints a room count for anybody, and the words *passage* and *privilege* are at zero across the ten files.** There is no panel in any of the ten and **the wall said nothing on all ten of these days, and this volume's one rule is fixed to day 438 and this batch neither wrote it nor anticipated it.** *Open.*

10. **AND ONE FIGURE INHERITED THAT A LATER WRITER MAY NOT INHERIT BY ACCIDENT, WHICH A BATCH FOUND BY CHECKING A WEEKDAY AGAINST THE FOUNDING RULE AND THEN REFUSING TO PRINT.** `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0389.md:3` calls the seventy-fourth day of the Bare Month a Friday. **That day is day 389, and day 389 is a Saturday on the founding rule, and a page outranks a plan.** It is a page in a finished volume and it was neither repaired nor restated: **Chapter 0401 refers to that morning three times and names no weekday in any of the three**, and a later batch that reads Chapter 0401 will not find a weekday to check there and needs to know there was one. Routed with the other inherited debts and not repaired here.

11. **AND THE HOUSE FIGURES FOR THESE TEN DAYS, WITH THE DEFINITIONS BESIDE THEM, AND NO TARGET SET ON ANY OF THEM.** **The share of words inside bold marks, heading line included, `---` rules counted, bold marks counted as nothing, file split on whitespace: 14.45, 5.97, 17.22, 14.55, 13.44, 14.38, 20.99, 9.42, 11.67 and 12.88 per cent, a range of 5.97 to 20.99, a mean of 13.50 unweighted and 13.35 weighted by words on 15,749 words** — against Volume 08's 5.11 to 24.72 and mean 12.39 on 87,216 words and Volume 07's 7.01 to 25.37 and mean 13.55. **The first pass of these ten files measured 5.97 to 21.07 and a mean of 13.75 on 15,776 words, and three turns were written over the ninety-word working ceiling and all three were repaired with a plain-speech interjection and a narration beat and not one with a cut sentence.** **The mean is a consequence and not a plan, no volume may read its own mean as a quality figure, and no review may certify or fail a volume on it.** The longest bolded turn in the ten is **eighty-three words**, under the hard ceiling of ninety-five and under the working one of about ninety, and it is in Chapter 0410 and it is a turn the review repair of that chapter wrote in place of a turn lifted whole out of Volume 08. **The count of paragraphs carrying neither speaker marker is ZERO in all ten**, on the first-person-restricted instrument, and 162 on the unrestricted reading, which is correct and is not a fault. The exchange is **52 plain speeches and 31 answered at fifty-nine point six per cent**, and the first pass was 46 and 32 at sixty-nine point six, **and the fall is the chapter work and not a defect in the instrument: the man who is right is unmarked in both chapters that have one, and four plain speeches close Chapter 0402.** **The chorus frame, word-bounded, on the bare head: 25 case-sensitive and 28 case-insensitive in 263 paragraphs, one in 10.5 and one in 9.4, and 36 on the variant-agnostic head, and a maximum of one per `---` section in all ten files on all three heads**, and the first pass was 33 and 38 in 248 paragraphs. The longest run of consecutive speech paragraphs is **four**, against the ceiling of four, and three runs were over it on the first pass and all three were broken with a narration beat. There are **179 sentences of forty words or more** on the definition published in the batch record — a file stripped of its heading line, its `---` rules and its bold marks, split into paragraphs on the blank line and then into sentences — and **166** on the reading that joins a sentence running across a blank line into one, which is where the first figure of this section came from and which is named here so that a reader can reproduce either; the longest of them is **151 words, in Chapter 0410, in that chapter's lead**. **None of them stands verbatim in another chapter file, inside the batch or across the four hundred, and the first edition of this section published that as zero across the four hundred and it was one: a fifty-seven-word sentence in Chapter 0410 stood verbatim at `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0400.md:9`, and it is varied in place with every fact kept and the check was re-run after the repair.** The longest word-run inside the batch is **42**, between Chapters 0403 and 0408, on the wharf-boards frame; **the longest against the four hundred pre-existing files is 47, between Chapter 0405 and `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0355.md`, on the room-coda lead, and the first edition of this section published that as 21, and 21 was the seventh figure in the list and not the first. Chapter 0410 was carrying a sixty-four-word run out of `chapter-0400.md` in three lifts, and all three are varied in place.** *Open.*

12. **AND NOTHING IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS IS A POWER.** The gains are a second sheet that came up a road and was read out, a page with a place for a person in it read aloud in a room in daylight, a man right about a column in four seconds and thanked by nobody, a woman who said in a room that she is not the one who is going to decide, a man with a piece of chalk in his pocket who did not use it, a barrow put down on the low side of a fence, and a man standing in front of a fence who said four things to a barrow. **None of them is a power. Some of them are the price of a power. All of them are heavier than a power.** Adrian Vale performed no working, no threshold was opened, the other world is not named once, nobody offered him a workway and he asked for none, **the words *passage* and *privilege* are at zero across the ten files**, he is aged nowhere, **and he is named in Chapters 0402 and 0405 and in no other of them, in any form. And the wall said nothing on all ten of these days.** *Open, **and the third column is still empty and the third place is still empty and this batch settled nothing.***

13. **AND THE REVIEW REPAIR OF THIS SECTION, WHICH CORRECTED FOUR FIGURES HERE AND ONE DAY ON A PAGE, AND WHICH AMENDS NOTHING ABOVE IT.** A review of Volume 09's Batch 0001 found eleven faults, nine of them in the batch's own record and two on its ten pages, and the full list with the old figure beside every new one is at `state/batch-summaries/volume-09-batch-0001.md` §11. **This section is corrected in place at item 11 and at the lead paragraph and nothing else in it moved, and no section above this one was touched and §32, §33, §33A, §33B and §51 are unamended as they were.** The weekday tokens are **thirty-one** and not thirty-four. The bolded shares are **14.45, 5.97, 17.22, 14.55, 13.44, 14.38, 20.99, 9.42, 11.67 and 12.88**, a mean of **13.50** unweighted and **13.35** weighted on **15,749** words, and the third of them was published here as 17.27 and is 17.22. The longest bolded turn is **eighty-three words**. The long sentences are **179** on the definition published in the batch record and **166** on the joined-blank-line reading, the longest is **151 words in Chapter 0410's lead**, the manuscript-wide duplication count is zero **because of the repair and not before it**, and the longest run against the four hundred pre-existing files is **47** and not 21. **AND THE ONE PAGE FAULT IN THE BATCH IS IN ITEM 1'S OWN SENTENCE: Chapter 0401 printed the return as coming up the road on the fifty-first day of this flood, which is day 51, and it is day 351, and both places now read the three hundred and fifty-first day of this flood, so §51 item 4 is satisfied on a page and item 1's claim that the chapter names day 351 in that sentence is true rather than asserted.** The three stretches of words Chapter 0410 had lifted out of `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0400.md` are varied in place with every fact kept, and no page in a finished volume was touched, and the printed order of the four places under the sheet's heading is unchanged, and **not one of the nine inherited debts was repaired and none was rerouted.**

## 53. Rules established in Volume 09, Chapters 0411–0420 — A LINE THAT IS GOING TO BE HERS SAID FIRST, A PAGE THAT CAME OUT OF A FOLD, A COUNT OF THREE AND ONE, A WALL THE RAIN WASHES, A FIRST THING A MAN IS NOT GOING TO SAY, A PAGE NOBODY IN THE ROOM HAS READ, AND THE VOLUME'S ONE NEW PERSON

**The ten days are days 411 to 420, one chapter to one day, Chapters 0411 to 0420, and the run was recomputed from day 1 being a Tuesday and from nothing else before any prose was written: 411 Saturday, 412 Sunday, 413 Monday, 414 Tuesday, 415 Wednesday, 416 Thursday, 417 Friday, 418 Saturday, 419 Sunday, 420 Monday. The Bare Month days are the ninety-sixth to the hundred and fifth, the Nth being day less 315. There is no double day in this batch and there is no double day in this volume. Twenty-four weekday tokens stand across the ten files, every one of them was read in the sentence it stands in against the day that sentence names, and every one of them is correct.**

**This is a batch section and it decides nothing. A batch may not make, amend or extend §32, §33, §33A, §33B or §51, and this section amends none of them and decides no calendar and no form. §51's fifty rows and twenty-one anchors are unchanged and every day figure below is derived from the two founding figures and from nothing else. NOTHING IN THIS SECTION IS A MOUTH. A state file may not copy a mouth at all, and every act below is paraphrased in the narrator's own words for that reason and not for the length of it.**

**AND THE FIRST ITEM IS ABOUT THIS BATCH'S OWN BRIEFING, published before anything else because a witness that hides what it was not given is not a witness.**

0. **THE PROMPT FOR THIS BATCH INSTRUCTED THIS BATCH TO READ ITS TEN CARDS AT `outline/batches/volume-09-batch-0002.md` AND THAT FILE DOES NOT EXIST.** The directory holds six card files and there is no Volume 09 second-batch card file in it. **These ten chapters were therefore written from the prompt's own five declared purposes and from `outline/volume-09.md` §7, §7.2, §7.3, §9, §14.3, §15, §16, §17 and §20.6, and from the ten chapters immediately before them.** The card-level instructions were reconstructed and not read, **two departures from the object inventory's chapter list are published in the batch record §4, and the card file was not created, because a batch may not create an outline file and a card file is one.** Routed to the next phase, which is told to look for its own card file before it reads it and to say so in its own record if it is not there.

1. **A WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-TWO IS IN A ROOM ON DAY 411 AND IS NOT ASKED ANYTHING, AND SHE SAYS OUT LOUD, UNPROMPTED, THAT ONE OF THE FOUR LINES ON THAT PAGE IS GOING TO BE HERS.** She sits furthest from the door, reads the sheet from where she is sitting without being given it and without being asked to look at it, and says first that nobody in the room asked her to look at it and is not going to, and then that one of the four lines is going to be hers, and that she has said it before anybody said it so that nobody in that room can afterwards say that she was asked. **Nobody asks her anything in that chapter or in any other of the ten, nobody tells her she is right and nobody tells her she is wrong, nobody thanks her, and her own door is not knocked on.** Her second handle is a door she is not asked to open, and it is the same handle as before and is not a second one. **The finding that a household's days are not a rota is not in her mouth anywhere in these ten chapters and is not improved on and is nowhere in this batch.** *Open, and the four lines are drawn on day 432 and this is not a resolution of that day.*

2. **AND ON DAY 413 IT IS ON A PAGE THAT THE SHEET WAS FOLDED TWICE AND THAT ONE OF THE TWO FOLDS IS THE ONE PRESSED DOWN THE THIRD OF THE FOUR PLACES, AND THE OTHER FOLD IS ALONG THE TOP.** Chapter 0413. The man who works it out has had the page in front of him for six days without opening or touching it, and the woman who reads forms out loud for a living objects that the page is lying open on the table, and he answers that he is not saying it is folded now. **A crease pressed hard enough to break the surface of a paper is a mark and not a bend, and no hand in any of these ten chapters opened the sheet, folded it, wrote on it or turned it over, and a crease is not an entry.** **Nobody in any of these ten chapters traces who folded it, asks, guesses, or says in any mouth that anybody folded it, and the observation is a fact about the paper and not an answer.** *Open.*

3. **AND ON DAY 416 THE ARITHMETIC OF THE FOUR PLACES IS ON A PAGE, AND IT IS A COUNT AND NOT A DECISION.** Chapter 0416. The woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register counts the four places out loud in front of about nine people and names the count: the first of the four is a place and any person in the room can write it, the fourth of the four is a day and any person in the room can write that too, the second of the four is a line and a line is a thing a person can put down whether or not anybody is standing on either side of it — so that three of the four can be answered by the office or by the room or by a man with a pen, and the fourth cannot be answered by any of those. **She says in her own mouth that she is not saying what any of them is for, and she names no day, and the man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall answers that he is not going to be the one that puts a mark on any of them, and she then says that the one of the four the room cannot do is the one the fold is pressed down.** **The third column stays empty and the third place stays empty and no chapter of these ten fills either.** *Open.*

4. **AND ON DAY 415 A ROOM WORKS OUT, IN NARRATION AND IN NO MOUTH, THAT THE OFFICE THAT SENT THIS SHEET UP THE ROAD SENT ANOTHER PAGE DOWN THE SAME ROUND IN THE SAME QUARTER, THAT THE ONE WHICH WENT OUT HAD A PERSON'S NAME AT THE TOP OF IT AND WAS CORRECT, AND THAT THIS ONE HAS A PLACE IN IT THAT A PERSON COULD BE PUT INTO, AND THAT NEITHER OF THE TWO HAS ANYWHERE ON IT TO SAY WHOM THE OFFICE IS ACTUALLY ASKING ABOUT.** Chapter 0415. **The two are never laid side by side, the four places of the other forms are not named in that chapter or in any other of the ten, and the word *holder* is at zero across the ten files and is defined by nobody in them.** The man of about forty-one in a very good coat is in that room and **does not describe the room where anything got decided, because he has not been in it, and the nine sentences about a return that are his are not said, restated, paraphrased or improved on in any of these ten chapters.** *Open.*

5. **AND ON DAY 417 THE STORE'S BOARD IS SAVED IN A SQUALL AND THE WALL ABOVE IT IS WASHED, AND NEITHER OF THEM IS THANKED.** Chapter 0417. The board is on the outside wall of a store and has not been written on since the two hundred and twenty-fifth day of this flood and carries the figures of the two hundred and twenty-fourth, and a man of about twenty-seven washes it every morning and had decided about a month ago not to wash the wall above it. **A squall washes the wall in about a quarter of an hour without him lifting a bucket, and the man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall, who has been under his own board with a stone on the foot of it, comes out and washes the board from the top down inside the eave, and the man the board belongs to refuses out loud to let it get about that a man washed a board he did not have to wash, on the ground that he did not stand there and ask.** **The board still carries the figures of the two hundred and twenty-fourth day and the wall above it is clean, and neither man is thanked, and the sentence that a board nobody has written on is the only instrument anybody in this city has said out loud has not lied to anybody is not said again in these ten chapters and is not improved on.** *Open, and the wall being washed is a change to a standing object and it is on a page.*

6. **AND ON DAY 418 THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-SEVEN SAYS OUT LOUD, UNASKED, THAT THERE IS A FIRST THING HE COULD SAY TO ANYBODY WHO ASKED HIM AND THAT HE IS NOT GOING TO SAY IT, AND THAT HE HAS SAID SO THAT NOBODY CAN AFTERWARDS SAY HE LED ANYBODY UP TO IT.** Chapter 0418. **Nobody asks him where the fence is, nobody tells him, he does not move the fence, he does not touch the rope, the length of new rope has still never been used, and no chapter of these ten says what the first thing is or asks him anything at all.** The man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall says it back to him and neither of them thanks the other, **and the two men at the two ends of one bench are the only two people in that room who have been asked nothing as often as each other.** *Open.*

7. **AND ON DAY 419 IT IS ON A PAGE THAT THE MAN THE SHEET CAME FROM HAS BEEN IN THAT ROOM ON MOST OF THE MORNINGS SINCE IT CAME UP THE ROAD AND HAS NOT READ ONE WORD OF IT, AND THAT THE ROOM HAS BEEN TALKING ABOUT THE THIRD OF THE FOUR PLACES IN FRONT OF A MAN WHO CANNOT FOLLOW ONE WORD OF THE LOCAL LANGUAGE.** Chapter 0419. **It is said out loud at the back of the room and not to him, and the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register is the first in that room to admit that the room was wrong about it, and NOBODY IN ANY OF THESE TEN CHAPTERS ARRANGES FOR HIM TO HAVE THE LOCAL LANGUAGE AND NOBODY OFFERS TO READ THE FOUR PLACES OUT AGAIN IN A SECOND LANGUAGE, and a room which has worked out that it has been talking in front of a man for a week does not get to fix it by talking in front of him again.** He is not exposed, not converted and not put down, and the language is not mended in this volume. *Open.*

8. **AND ON DAY 414 A MAN READS THE REGISTER WHERE IT LIES AND FINDS THE MARGIN WORN PALE IN A CURVE ABOUT THE WIDTH OF A THUMB BESIDE THE THIRD COLUMN AND NOT BESIDE THE OTHER TWO, AND SAYS ONE TRUE THING ABOUT THE PAPER AND STOPS ONE STEP SHORT OF IT.** Chapter 0414. He does not lift the book, does not turn a page and does not put a hand on the frame of the shelf it lives on, **and nobody in that room asks him for the rest of it and nobody thanks him and the woman who keeps that book has never once asked him anything and is not going to start.** **ABOUT FOUR HUNDRED AND FORTY IS ON A PAGE ONCE IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS, ON ELEVEN MILES OF FLATS, AND THE FIGURE THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-EIGHT HAS IS PRINTED NOWHERE IN THEM AND THE DIFFERENCE BETWEEN THE TWO FIGURES IS STATED NOWHERE IN THEM, and no chapter of these ten prints the two as two numbers and no chapter of these ten asks him what a column is for.** *Open.*

9. **AND ON DAY 420 VOLUME 09'S ONE NEW PERSON IS PUT ON A PAGE, AND THE WHOLE OF THE INSTRUMENT IS THE ASKING.** Chapter 0420. A woman of about fifty-four is asked, in a room over a market, in daylight, in the local language, with a date on the door, where her line is, and she answers correctly. Her line is where the water stops on her own step, at the second board, and it is not the same line twice in the same week, and she has never once put it on a page. **Nobody asked her a second question, nobody in that room asked her where her step is, how long it is, who is on the other side of it, or how she came to know a length, nobody said thank you, and she is thanked by nobody in any of these ten chapters.** The woman who asked her then said out loud in her own mouth that there is nowhere in four hundred miles to put an answer that somebody gave out loud in a room, and that it is on no page in the world, and that she is not going to complain about it in front of the woman she asked. **HER SECOND HANDLE IS A LENGTH OF BLUE THREAD TIED ROUND HER OWN WRIST, WHICH IS HOW SHE HAS ALWAYS KNOWN A LENGTH BY. IT IS ON NO PAGE BEFORE CHAPTER 0420 AND IT IS THE SAME IN EVERY CHAPTER SHE IS IN AFTER IT, AND SHE IS IN NO CHAPTER OF THIS VOLUME AFTER THIS ONE, and her line is one of the four and it is the one that comes back moved on day 440 and nobody is ever going to ask her about it.** **No chapter of this volume traces a move, investigates one, asks who made one, reports one to a room, guesses at one, or says in any mouth that anybody moved anything, and nothing has moved because nothing has been drawn.** The descriptor `woman of about fifty-four` stands at four occurrences in Chapter 0420 and at **zero in the other nine files of this batch**, at 3 in 1 file across the four hundred written chapter files, and at zero in Volume 07's fifty and Volume 08's fifty. *Open, and she is the one new person this volume adds and Volume 09 does not get a second one.*

10. **AND THE THINGS THESE TEN CHAPTERS DID NOT DO, WHICH A LATER WRITER HAS TO KNOW ARE STILL UNTOUCHED.** There is no eighth notice in this flood and these ten chapters wrote none, and every room in them is a room about nine people were already in. **The four days are the cooling and the cooling is the time a room has in which to find out what it is being asked, and that sentence is on a page once in this volume, in Chapter 0401, in plain narration and in no mouth, and the word *cooling* stands at ZERO across these ten files.** The word on the register-keeper's second slate is at zero in all ten and the common noun is nowhere used of anybody in them, and **the second slate is not named in any of these ten chapters and is never picked up, never turned over and never written on.** The register's third column has been empty on purpose and is empty on purpose after these ten days, **no chapter of the ten entered a line in that book, and the line entered on day 443 is a later chapter's.** The day-154 sentence is not said, restated, paraphrased or improved on in any of the ten, and **the four-second beat is at zero in all ten files, the exact string and the beat, and the beat stands in two chapters of this volume and both are earlier than these.** *Holder* is at zero, *claim* and its two inflections are at zero, and no chapter of the ten names the contents of any of the other two four-place forms. The fence is sixteen willow posts and eleven withies, the count is printed twice in these ten and in the same form both times, **the shut road is out of this city, the fence is along the top of that road, the lane of nine houses is at the foot of it and is not in this city, and the mark in a field is not named, not measured, not dug, not counted, not probed and not resolved in any of the ten and the word *field* stands at zero across the ten files.** The ninth line is not moved, not dated, not read aloud and not taken down. The days since the fourth of the four said no are not counted at any value in any of the ten and the count is zero. **NO ROOM COUNT IS PRINTED IN ANY OF THE TEN, and a word-bounded search for a spelled-out number, optionally hyphenated, followed by *rooms* returns zero across all ten files.** Nobody in this city can be shown one person on the four-hundred-mile road and the town four hundred miles inland is not entered, described from inside, or brought anybody back from, **and neither of the two elapsed counts for the three, which come out at exactly twenty and exactly twenty-nine on the two ends of this window, is printed in any of the ten.** The keeper of the Redroot gate is not named, not counted and not used; the woman of about fifty at a shut gate was not asked anything; the girl of seventeen is not fetched; the box is shut, the island is not owned, nobody picks the bag up, nobody counts the boards under the chapel and Break is not open. **The Bare Month did not end and no chapter of the ten said what it does at its end, how many days it has, or whether it is long or short, and the hundredth day of that month, which is day 415, is written as a day like any other with no meaning attached to it and the chapter says in its own words that nothing was said about it by anybody.** *Open.*

11. **AND THE HOUSE FIGURES FOR THESE TEN DAYS, WITH THE DEFINITIONS BESIDE THEM, AND NO TARGET SET ON ANY OF THEM.** **The share of words inside bold marks, heading line included, `---` rules counted, bold marks counted as nothing, file split on whitespace: 12.94, 13.08, 21.54, 9.91, 9.63, 20.14, 10.88, 14.91, 8.43 and 14.44 per cent, a range of 8.43 to 21.54, a mean of 13.59 unweighted and 13.56 weighted by words on 11,698 words, and the first edition of this list was 12.99, 14.33, 21.62, 10.26, 9.63, 20.14, 10.96, 15.20, 8.67 and 14.44, a range of 8.67 to 21.62, a mean of 13.82 and 13.81 on 11,378 words, and both are published because a silent correction is the third version of the same fault** — against Volume 08's 5.11 to 24.72 and mean 12.39 on 87,216 words and Volume 07's 7.01 to 25.37 and mean 13.55. **Three turns were written over the ninety-five hard ceiling on a first pass, at 111, 100 and 97 words, and all three were repaired with a plain-speech interjection from the other voice and a narration beat and not one with a cut sentence. The mean is a consequence and not a plan, no volume may read its own mean as a quality figure, and no review may certify or fail a volume on it.** The longest bolded turn in the ten is **eighty-nine words**, under the hard ceiling of ninety-five and under the working one of about ninety. **The count of paragraphs carrying neither speaker marker is ZERO in all ten**, on the first-person-restricted instrument, and **one was found and repaired**, and 169 on the unrestricted reading, which was 165 and is correct and is not a fault. The exchange is **21 plain speeches and 16 answered at seventy-six point two per cent**, where the first edition published 20 and 15 at seventy-five point zero, and the five unanswered ones are a marked voice that ends a scene in two chapters, a chapter with no plain speech in it at all, and a chapter that opens with two unmarked turns before its marked voice has spoken. **The chorus frame, word-bounded, on the bare head: 24 case-sensitive and 26 case-insensitive in 169 paragraphs, one in 7.0 and one in 6.5, and 29 and 31 on the variant-agnostic head, one in 5.8 and one in 5.5, and a MAXIMUM OF ONE PER `---` SECTION IN ALL TEN FILES ON ALL FOUR HEADS; the four totals and that maximum are unchanged from the first edition and only the paragraph count moved, and ten instances were cut and rewritten in the narrator's own voice in eight of the ten chapters in the writing pass.** The longest run of consecutive speech paragraphs is **four**, in two of the ten, against a ceiling of four, and two runs of five were broken with a narration beat. The long sentences are **132** on the definition published in the batch record and **121** on the reading that joins a sentence across a blank line, where the first edition published 127 and 116, the longest is **140 words in Chapter 0416, unmoved**, and **none of them stands verbatim in another chapter file, inside the batch or across the four hundred, before the repair or after it**. The word-run is **39 inside the batch on the maximal-extension reading of the run and 37 on this section's own first reading of it, between Chapters 0416 and 0418, and 36 against the four hundred pre-existing files on the first of those readings and 35 on the second, between Chapter 0415 and `chapters/volume-09/chapter-0403.md`, and both readings of both figures are published because a review of this batch ran the instrument independently and returned the maximal-extension list exactly; and two runs of forty words or more were found on a first pass and both were lifts and both were varied in place with every fact kept and the check was re-run after the repair and returns zero.**

12. **AND THE TWO UNDECLARED FRAMES THIS BATCH CARRIES ONWARD, because a motif is a decision and a batch may not take one.** **The chalk frame at thirty-seven words between Chapters 0416 and 0418 is Volume 08's second-handle frame and not one of Volume 09's six declared motifs, and it is a frame and not a lift: the man, the pocket and the day the chalk went in are the same in both chapters and the house requires that day at full length in every chapter that carries it. The very-good-coat arrival frame at thirty-five words between Chapter 0415 and `chapters/volume-09/chapter-0403.md` is a person and location frame and not one of Volume 09's five. Batch 0001 handed on two more of the same kind, the wharf-boards frame and the room-coda lead, and this batch varied both of those in its own ten without declaring either — the wharf-boards frame does not appear in these ten at all and the room-coda lead appears once and was varied on the one occasion it was used. Four undeclared frames are now live for a later phase, which may declare them or vary one occurrence of each, and a batch may not take that decision.** *Open.*

13. **AND NOTHING IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS IS A POWER.** The gains are a woman who said before anybody asked that a line is going to be hers, a page that turned out to have been folded on its third place, a count of three and one, a board saved and a wall washed, a man who announced a thing he was not going to say, a room that found out it had been talking in front of a man, and a woman asked where her line is. **None of them is a power. Some of them are the price of a power. All of them are heavier than a power.** Adrian Vale performed no working, no threshold was opened, the other world is not named once, nobody offered him a workway and he asked for none, **the words *passage* and *privilege* are at zero across the ten files**, he is aged nowhere, **he is named in Chapter 0414 and in no chapter of the ten in any form, and in 0414 he is in a room reading a page.** **And the wall said nothing on all ten of these days.** *Open, and the third column is still empty and the third place is still empty and this batch settled nothing.*

14. **AND THE REVIEW REPAIR OF THIS SECTION, WHICH CORRECTED EIGHT FIGURES IN ITEM 11 AND THREE FAULTS IN THE RECORD ABOVE IT, AND WHICH AMENDS NOTHING ABOVE IT.** A review of Volume 09's Batch 0002 found five things: an upstream gap in `outline/batches/`, one ambiguous speaker attribution, three chapters it held under-powered, and a monotony in the leads, the codas and the time-measurement similes. **Four were repaired on the ten pages and in the batch record, the fifth is escalated and not repaired, and this item is the section's account of all five plus three faults the review did not raise, and it decides nothing.** **§32, §33, §33A, §33B and §51 are unamended as they were, no section above this one was touched, `bible/power-system.md:1686` was not read into any file this pass wrote, `state/character-state.md:223` was not touched and is still line 223, and no day, no weekday, no Bare-Month day, no notice, no form, no object count that holds, no anchor, no cast member and no decision of record moved.**

    **(a) THE ESCALATED ITEM, WHICH IS NOT REPAIRED AND IS NAMED AS A REMEDY INSTEAD OF BEING ROUTED A FIFTH TIME.** The prompt for that batch ordered it to read its ten cards at `outline/batches/volume-09-batch-0002.md` and that file does not exist on disk, and **there is no `volume-09-batch-0003.md` either, so Batches 0003, 0004 and 0005 of this volume will all write from the outline alone unless an outline phase generates the three missing card files.** The gap is disclosed at `state/batch-summaries/volume-09-batch-0002.md` §0, at item 0 of this section, in the four state files, and at the next prompt's own line 50, which tells Batch 0003 to look for its own card file before it reads it, to work from the six named sections of the outline if it is not there, to say so at the head of its own record, and **not to create the file. THE REMEDY IS ONE OUTLINE PHASE THAT WRITES THE THREE MISSING CARD FILES FOR VOLUME 09's SECOND, THIRD AND FOURTH BATCHES, and no card file was written by this pass, in whole or in part, because a card file is an outline file and a batch may not create one.** *Open, and it is the one item in this volume that cannot be discharged by a batch.*

    **(b) A PRESENT-TENSE CONSEQUENCE ON A PAGE, AND THE WHOLE OF IT IS A LENGTH OF WOOD.** Chapter 0412, which a review held to be the one chapter of the ten that ends where it begins, now has one. **A man of about thirty-nine lays a board of his own down on a wharf at the bottom of this city, the man of about thirty-eight at the salt wharf cuts one length off it without being asked how long, it comes off about a forearm long, the man of about thirty-nine says out loud that he did not tell him how long and that he was not asked, and the man of about thirty-eight answers that he has got it too long and will have to saw it himself, and the man of about thirty-nine goes up off the wharf with two boards under his arm instead of one.** **This is the second thing the man of about thirty-eight at the salt wharf has given away in one volume, the first being a barrow on day 391, and no chapter joins the two and no chapter says the two are the same kind of thing and no chapter says why either of them happened, and he is thanked in none of the three chapters he is in and asked nothing in any of them.** A length of board is a piece of wood and is not a page and is on nobody's list, and the strip of clear board at the bottom of the same stack is a different piece of wood, did not move, and is not joined to it. **Nothing in this item is a mouth, and the two sentences in it are paraphrased and not quoted, and a state file may not copy a mouth at all.**

    **(c) THE ATTRIBUTION, AND THE REPAIR SHAPE, because a repair pass that cuts a sentence to fix an attribution has spent a sentence that was there for a reason.** In Chapter 0419 the marked turn at the end of a scene is the man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall, and a plain speech immediately before it is the woman of about twenty-nine's admission that the room had been wrong, and the turn read as a callback to hers. **One clause was added to the end of the narration paragraph that carries her speech, naming him as the man who says the next thing and not to her, and no speech was cut and no word of hers was touched, and the room's admission is still hers and nobody thanked either of them.** The repair shape that worked in this volume's first two batches — a plain-speech interjection from the other voice and a narration beat — was not available here, because the fault was not a turn that was too long but a turn whose speaker had to be named, and **a repair that has only one shape in it is a repair that will use the wrong one.**

    **(d) THE MONOTONY, AND HOW MUCH OF IT WAS ACTUALLY VARIED, published with the count on both readings because a repair pass that claims to have fixed a monotony and prints no count of it is asserting rather than measuring.** The house lead formula *about four pairs of hands* now stands in **seven of the ten** and at zero in **Chapters 0412, 0414 and 0417**, and the reason is a rule: it reports a room's attendance, and in those three chapters the room's attendance is not what the chapter is about, and the other seven are all set in the room over a market. **The formula is a method and not a token, it was not taken out of the volume, and a later batch may use it in all of its room chapters or vary it in this way and say which it did.** Two codas, in Chapters 0414 and 0418, no longer close on an inventory of objects, and the coda of Chapter 0412 was rewritten for (b) and does the same. **On the review's own test the shape falls from nine of ten to eight of ten; on the coda alone it falls from six to five; and the two chapters that still carry the clause in the paragraph before the coda were left alone, because what they carry there is a published fact — that nobody arranged for a language, and that nobody in this city can be shown one person on a road — and a repair pass does not remove a published fact to improve a shape count.** Four of the twenty-one time-measurement similes were replaced with a physical measure, in Chapters 0411, 0413, 0414 and 0417, and the family now stands at nine where it stood at thirteen.

    **(e) THE THREE FAULTS IN THE RECORD THAT THE REVIEW DID NOT RAISE, all three of them the failure this section is written to catch, and all three corrected in place with the first edition beside them.** One, **the batch record's §7 published that the man of about thirty-nine is not in any of the ten chapters and he is in Chapter 0412 three times, and the same error stood in `state/character-state.md`'s newest block in a list of who is not in this batch; a cast file that loses a man is a cast file that cannot be checked against a page, and a record that hands the next batch a wrong cast is not a witness.** Two, **the marker *mark* was published at four across the ten with all four about the crease on the page, and there are five, and the fifth is a length of blue thread worn into a woman's wrist, and *field* is at zero across the ten and none of the five is about anything in a field.** Three, **a room-count search was published as returning zero with no list of number words attached to it, and it returns one on a list that includes *dozen*, in a sentence about how many rooms a man had been asked in, which is an approximate number of rooms and not a count of any set of rooms in this city, so *about two hundred rooms* is at zero and no chapter of the ten prints a room count in the sense the prohibition is about.** A zero with an unstated list behind it is a figure, and this volume has now published three of them and corrected all three.

    **(f) THE FIGURES THIS SECTION'S ITEM 11 NOW STANDS AT, and the first edition of each of them, and a later writer comparing a figure against this section compares against the corrected list.** Words **11,698**; paragraphs **169**; bolded shares **12.94, 13.08, 21.54, 9.91, 9.63, 20.14, 10.88, 14.91, 8.43 and 14.44**, a range of **8.43 to 21.54** and a mean of **13.59** unweighted and **13.56** weighted, where item 11's first edition was 8.67 to 21.62 at 13.82 and 13.81 on 11,378 words; longest bolded turn **eighty-nine words, unmoved, in Chapter 0418**; unmarked first-person paragraphs **zero, unmoved**; exchange **21 and 16 at seventy-six point two per cent**, where the first edition was 20 and 15 at seventy-five point zero; chorus frame **24, 26, 29 and 31, unmoved**, in **169** paragraphs, one in **7.0, 6.5, 5.8 and 5.5**, maximum of one per section **unmoved**; longest run of consecutive speech paragraphs **four, unmoved**; long sentences **132** and **121** where the first edition was 127 and 116, longest **140 words in Chapter 0416, unmoved**; manuscript-wide duplication count **zero before and zero after**; word-run **39 or 37** inside the batch and **36 or 35** against the four hundred, two readings of each. **The full account, with the position of every repair in every file, is at `state/batch-summaries/volume-09-batch-0002.md` §13, and none of the nine inherited debts was repaired and none was rerouted and the list is the list.** *Open, and the calendar is where it was.*

## 54. Rules established in Volume 09, Chapters 0421–0430 — A ROOM WITH NO INSTRUMENT FOR ASKING, A PAGE CARRIED OUT ONTO A STEP, A WITHIE PUT BACK ON, TWO ORDINARY QUESTIONS, THE THIRD PLACE LEFT EMPTY, A BOARD FACE UP, THE NAME SPOKEN, AND A VOICE AT THE BOTTOM OF A STAIR

**The ten days are days 421 to 430, one chapter to one day, Chapters 0421 to 0430, and the run was recomputed from day 1 being a Tuesday and from nothing else before any prose was written: 421 Tuesday, 422 Wednesday, 423 Thursday, 424 Friday, 425 Saturday, 426 Sunday, 427 Monday, 428 Tuesday, 429 Wednesday, 430 Thursday. The Bare Month days are the hundred and sixth to the hundred and fifteenth, the Nth being day less 315. There is no double day in this batch and there is no double day in this volume. **Thirty-three weekday tokens stand across the ten files, every one of them was read in the sentence it stands in against the day that sentence names, and every one of them is correct. THE FIRST EDITION OF THIS SENTENCE SAID THIRTY, because the instrument behind it counted lines and not tokens, and three of the ten chapters carry two weekday tokens in one line and one of them carries three, and the corrected total and the corrected per-chapter list, 8, 2, 2, 1, 4, 6, 2, 1, 3 and 4, are at the batch record §2 and at item 15 below.**

**This is a batch section and it decides one thing, which is a decision the plan had already made and which is now on a page, and it decides nothing else. A batch may not make, amend or extend §32, §33, §33A, §33B or §51, and this section amends none of them and decides no calendar and no form. §51's fifty rows and twenty-one anchors are unchanged and every day figure below is derived from the two founding figures and from nothing else. NOTHING IN THIS SECTION IS A MOUTH. A state file may not copy a mouth at all, and every act below is paraphrased in the narrator's own words for that reason and not for the length of it, and the volume's own name is not quoted in it, is not paraphrased into recognisability, and is not described.**

**AND THE FIRST ITEM IS ABOUT THIS BATCH'S OWN BRIEFING, published before anything else because a witness that hides what it was not given is not a witness.**

0. **THE PROMPT FOR THIS BATCH INSTRUCTED IT TO LOOK FOR ITS OWN CARD FILE AT `outline/batches/volume-09-batch-0003.md` BEFORE READING IT, AND THAT FILE DOES NOT EXIST.** The directory holds six card files and there is no Volume 09 third-batch file in it. **These ten chapters were therefore written from the prompt's own five declared purposes and from `outline/volume-09.md` §7, §7.2, §7.3, §8, §9, §14.3, §14.5, §14.6, §15, §16, §17 and §20.6, and from the ten chapters immediately before them and from `state/batch-summaries/volume-09-batch-0002.md`.** The card-level instructions were reconstructed and not read, **three departures from the object inventory's chapter list are published at the batch record §4, and the card file was not created, because a batch may not create an outline file and a card file is one.** Routed, and the remedy is named rather than routed a fifth time in different words: **one outline phase that writes the three missing card files for Volume 09's second, third and fourth batches. It does not exist and this batch did not write it.** *Open, and it is the one item in this volume that a batch cannot discharge.*

1. **ON DAY 425 A WOMAN DECIDED OUT LOUD, IN A ROOM OVER A MARKET, IN DAYLIGHT, WITH A DATE IN CHALK ON THE DOOR, THAT THE THIRD OF THE FOUR PLACES ON THAT SHEET WAS GOING TO BE LEFT EMPTY, AND THE THIRD OF THE FOUR PLACES IS A PERSON.** She said what the third place is before anything else, and it took her nine words, and she said the cost out loud before the decision and the reason for the cost is hers. **The cost is that a line with nobody's name on it is easier to move than one with a name on it, and that whoever is standing where one of them runs is the one who finds out last, and she said in her own mouth that it is not her and not anybody at that table and that she is not going to name one of them while she is saying it. There was no vote and nobody asked for one. She is thanked by nobody, nobody told her she was right and nobody told her she was wrong, and about four people in that room worked out afterwards that the reason was not that it was the smaller of the two, and about four of them are not going to forgive her for it and about four are not going to blame her for it.** *Open, and no chapter of this volume gives a reader the reason, and this section does not have one and did not work one out.*

2. **AND ON DAY 425 IT IS ALSO ON A PAGE, IN ONE PLAIN SENTENCE OF NARRATION AND NOT IN ANY MOUTH, THAT THE PAGE WAS READ OUT IN THAT ROOM ON THE NINETIETH DAY OF THE BARE MONTH, A SUNDAY, THAT NOBODY WAS SENT FOR THAT MORNING OR FOR THE ONE IN QUESTION, THAT EVERY MORNING IN BETWEEN WAS A MORNING THE ROOM WAS IN AND NOBODY IN IT WENT DOWN NINE STEPS TO ASK ANYBODY ANYTHING, AND THAT THIS IS WHY IT CAME TO A DECISION IN ONE SITTING AND DID NOT COME TO ONE AT ALL FOR A FORTNIGHT.** *Open.*

3. **AND ON DAY 421 THE OTHER HALF OF THE SAME DECISION IS ON A PAGE, IN A MOUTH, AND IT IS NOT THE SAME SENTENCE AS THE ONE ABOVE.** A man of about thirty-nine said out loud in that room that four of the people in it had been there every working morning since the page came up the road and not one of them had gone down nine steps and put one question to one man, and gave the reason, which is that a sheet goes out of this city four times a year and comes back with things in it that were not written on it, and that there is no form in four hundred miles that puts a question to a man. A man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall said out loud that the man at the end of the bench is not being spared and is not being reached and that those are two different things, and nobody thanked either of them, and **the chapter says in plain narration that a true thing about why you have not done a thing is not a thing that does it.** **The man that finding is about was in that room and was not asked and was not looked at while it was being said.** *Open, and the four days are the cooling and the sentence belonging to that is on a page once in this volume, in Chapter 0401, in plain narration and in no mouth, and the word is at zero across these ten files and across the twenty before them.*

4. **THE FOUR UNDECLARED FRAMES THAT WERE LIVE WHEN THIS BATCH BEGAN, measured again on the files as they stand, and declared here as frames and NOT as motifs, because a motif is Volume 09's six and they are not any of the six and a batch may not add to a list the outline phase set.** | The frame | The two occurrences, as measured on the sequential-match instrument with the lead paragraphs included | What this batch did |

The full form of the day the chalk went into the pocket is printed at full length in five of the ten chapters, in a mouth in one and in narration in four, and the sentence around it is different in each of the five, so the largest run this batch makes of that frame is thirty-two words and it is the same date in a different sentence and not a sentence.** |
   | **the very-good-coat arrival frame** | thirty-five to thirty-six words between `chapters/volume-09/chapter-0415.md` and `chapter-0403.md` — a person, a coat, a document and nine steps | **It is not in this batch's ten. The man of about forty-one in a very good coat is in none of these ten chapters, the office is not named, and the phrase for the distance of the office stands at zero across the ten files.** |

The man of about thirty-eight at the salt wharf is in Chapter 0427 and comes up nine steps out of a room chapter and no chapter of these ten is set on the boards, and the strip of clear board is not named in any of the ten.** |

The other thirty-seven-word run in the batch, Volume 08's second-handle frame between Chapter 0421 and Chapter 0430, is a body against a body, at line 21 in one and line 27 in the other. It is published here because a fifth frame that existed for the length of one writing pass is a fifth frame, and a frame that is caught and cut is the only kind that is worth recording.** |

5. **AND ON DAY 422 A SHEET WAS CARRIED OUT OF A ROOM AND ONTO THE STEP AT THE BACK OF IT FOR ABOUT AN HOUR, IN DAYLIGHT, STILL FOLDED THE WAY IT CAME, BY THE WOMAN WHO KEEPS A PUBLIC REGISTER IN HER OWN TWO HANDS, AND A PERSON IN THE MARKET BELOW ASKED WHAT THE THIRD OF THE FOUR PLACES WAS FOR AND WAS TOLD OUT LOUD THAT IT IS A PERSON AND THAT NOBODY KNEW WHAT IT WAS FOR.** Nobody sent for anybody to ask that person anything and nobody stopped her looking, and a man who trades on a board was the only person standing where she was standing. The page went back on the same place on the table in the afternoon. **No hand in any of these ten chapters opened the sheet, wrote on it, turned it over or folded it, the four places under the heading were all empty before the hour and all empty after it, and the crease pressed down the length of the third of them is still the only thing on that paper anybody in this city has ever touched.** *Open.*

6. **AND ON DAY 423 ONE WITHIE CAME OFF A FENCE ALONG THE TOP OF A SHUT ROAD OUT OF THIS CITY AND WAS PUT BACK ON, AND THE COUNT DID NOT MOVE.** Sixteen willow posts and eleven withies bound along it with the same cord, and the count is printed once in these ten chapters and it is the count that holds, and there is no seventeenth post and no twelfth withy. **The shut road is out of this city, the light comes up out of the low ground and stops at that fence and does not come over it, the lane at the foot of that road is not in this city, and the man of about fifty-seven was on the low side of that fence and was not asked anything, was not told anything, did not move it, and did not touch the length of new rope, which is in a room in this city and is not named in that chapter at all.** The coil of rope in the handcart is a different rope, has been used, is not new, and **Chapter 0423 names both of them in adjacent sentences and joins them in nothing.** **The mark in a field is not named, not measured, not dug, not counted, not probed and not resolved in any of these ten chapters, no chapter goes and looks at it, and the word stands at zero across the ten files.** *Open, and whether that fence stands between the right two things is not settled in this volume and these ten chapters do not settle it.*

7. **AND ON DAY 424 A WOMAN ASKED A MAN TWO QUESTIONS THAT WERE NOT ABOUT ANY PAGE, AND HE ANSWERED BOTH, AND NOBODY THANKED HIM.** She asked what he sells and then what he does with the rest of his day, standing up, in the local language, in daylight, with a date on the door, and she wrote nothing down afterwards because there is nowhere on that table to write down what a man sells. **This is the only time in this volume that anybody has asked that man an ordinary question about his trade, and the woman who asks it is the same woman who asks him the volume's question four days later, and her reason on each occasion is her own and is not improved on.** *Open.*

8. **AND ON DAY 427 THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-EIGHT AT THE SALT WHARF PUT A TALLY-BOARD ON A TABLE IN A ROOM WITH A DATE ON THE DOOR WITH THE FACE OF IT UP FOR THE FIRST TIME IN THIS FLOOD, AND DID NOT READ FROM IT, AND SAID OUT LOUD WHY HE HAS BEEN TURNING IT OVER AND THAT HE HAS STOPPED.** **He printed neither of his two figures, the difference between them is stated with no number attached to either, the figure is on a page in Chapter 0414 and the other one is on no page and is printed nowhere in this volume, and no chapter of these ten asks him what a column is for.** Nobody in that room asked him for the second figure and nobody asked him where the first came from, he put a hand flat on the face of the board and said nothing about it, and he was thanked by nobody. **He and the man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall passed each other on nine steps that afternoon and neither of them said one word, and no chapter of these ten merges him with the man who came up a lane with a barrow of boards on day 347, and that man is in none of them.** *Open.*

9. **AND ON DAY 428 THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A STALL WAS ASKED WHAT A LINE IS, BY THE WOMAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO KEEPS A PUBLIC REGISTER, STANDING UP, IN THE LOCAL LANGUAGE, IN DAYLIGHT, WITH A DATE IN CHALK ON THE DOOR, IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINE PEOPLE, ONCE, AND HE ANSWERED IN FIVE WORDS IN THE LOCAL LANGUAGE, ONCE.** **Nobody thanked him, nobody improved on it, nobody asked him a second question, nobody said it back to him, nobody told him he was right and nobody told him he was wrong, and no chapter in this volume states that it is the name of anything.** **THE WORDS ARE ON THAT PAGE AND IN NO FILE IN THIS REPOSITORY — not in the bible, not in the series outline, not in the volume outline, not in a batch record, not in a state file and not in a prompt — and this section does not have them, does not paraphrase them and does not describe them, and the same protection that held the volume's last name through fifty chapters holds this one.** He has asked in advance, in a lane, not to be thanked, and is thanked in none of the seven chapters he speaks in. **A man who reads pages was in that room and said not one word about it, did not tell him what he had said, did not tell anybody it was the name of anything, and was thanked by nobody for being there.** *Open, and on day 438 that same man refuses the volume's one rule in about four seconds, and the cost of that is his own ninth line, and no chapter may have him say he knows what he said and no chapter may put the two acts in one room on one day and no chapter may make the ninth line and the four lines on a page the same line, and Chapter 0429's coda says in its own words that they are not the same thing.*

10. **AND THE THINGS THESE TEN CHAPTERS DID NOT DO, WHICH A LATER WRITER HAS TO KNOW ARE STILL UNTOUCHED.** There is no eighth notice in this flood and these ten chapters wrote none, and every room in them is a room about nine people were already in, and the marker for a notice stands at zero across the ten files. **The word *cooling* stands at ZERO across these ten files and the sentence belonging to it is on a page once in this volume, in Chapter 0401, in plain narration and in no mouth.** The word on the register-keeper's second slate is at zero in all ten and the common noun is not used of anybody in them, and **the second slate is named in two of the ten and in both it carries a date and nothing under it and in both it was not picked up and not turned over and not written on.** The register's third column has been empty on purpose and is empty on purpose after these ten days, **no chapter of the ten entered a line in that book, and the line entered on day 443 is a later chapter's.** The day-154 sentence is not said, restated, paraphrased or improved on in any of the ten, and **the four-second beat is at zero in all ten files, the exact string and the beat, and the beat stands in two chapters of this volume and both are earlier than these, and the volume's climax puts a man right and a man refusing on day 438 and that is neither of the two.** *Holder* is at zero, *claim* and its two inflections are at zero, and no chapter of the ten names the contents of any of the other two four-place forms. **The shut road is out of this city and no chapter of the ten puts it above this city or at the top of it, the fence is along the top of that road, and the lane of nine houses is at the foot of it and is not in this city.** The ninth line is not moved, not dated, not read aloud and not taken down, and whether it works stays unanswered. The days since the fourth of the four said no are not counted at any value in any of the ten and the count is zero. **NO ROOM COUNT IS PRINTED IN ANY OF THE TEN**, and a word-bounded search for a spelled-out number, optionally hyphenated, followed by *rooms* returns zero on a list of cardinals and zero on a list that includes *dozen*, and both readings are published. Nobody in this city can be shown one person on the four-hundred-mile road and the town four hundred miles inland is not entered, described from inside, or brought anybody back from, **and neither of the two elapsed counts for the three is printed in any of the ten, and the one chapter of the ten that names them at all names the day they went and prints no count of how far they have got.** The keeper of the Redroot gate is not named, not counted and not used; the woman of about fifty at a shut gate was not asked anything; the girl of seventeen is not fetched; the box is shut, the island is not owned, nobody picks the bag up, nobody counts the boards under the chapel and Break is not open. **The Bare Month did not end and no chapter of the ten printed a day after the hundred and fifteenth, said the month was nearly over, said what it is a month of, or attached *length*, *long* or *short* to it, and every one of the ten names the month in full at least once, and the hundred and tenth day of that month, which is day 425, is written as a day like any other with no meaning attached to it and the chapter says in its own words that nothing was said about that day by anybody.** *Open.*

12. **AND ONE FIGURE IN AN INHERITED RECORD DID NOT REPRODUCE, and the corrected figure is published beside the published one because a silent correction is the third version of the same fault.** The count of this city's silences, derived, on no page, in no mouth, and printed in no chapter of this volume: **one addition for every day the wall says nothing, one hundred and fifty-two at day 250, three panel days before this volume at 256, 340 and 393, and this volume's one panel on day 438, gives 299 at day 400 and 300 at day 401, which are the two figures §51 item 9 itself prints, and 310 at day 411, 319 at day 420, 320 at day 421 and 329 at day 430.** `state/batch-summaries/volume-09-batch-0002.md` §3 item 9 and §11.6 and the newest block of `state/current.md` before this batch's all publish **320 at day 411 and 329 at day 420**, and **both of those are wrong and each is exactly ten high: the figure at day 411 is 310 and the figure at day 420 is 319.** This section corrects nothing and repairs nothing and takes no other view of the two published figures. *Open, and the figure is for the state layer and is printed in no chapter of this volume.*

13. **AND THE THINGS THIS BATCH PUT ON A PAGE THAT ARE NOT THREADS.** One piece of bark off a stall frame, carried up nine steps and put on a table and left there, invented on the page in Chapter 0422 and on no page before it. One sheet carried out onto a step and brought back. One withie off a fence and put back on. A tally-board put face up. Two questions about a trade. A shuttered stall on a Saturday. A voice at the bottom of a stair. **None of them is a power and none of them is a resolution and all of them cost somebody something that nobody in this repository has put on a page.** *Open, and the third place is decided empty and the third column is empty and this batch settled nothing else.*

14. **AND NOTHING IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS IS A POWER.** The gains are a room that found out it cannot ask anybody anything, a page that was put in front of a market by accident and a woman who said out loud that she did not know, two ordinary questions answered, a decision made faster than it should because there was nothing to be done about the date, a man who stopped hiding a figure, five words said once, and a man who lost two mornings and did not go down. **None of them is a power. Some of them are the price of a power. All of them are heavier than a power.** Adrian Vale performed no working, no threshold was opened, the other world is not named once, nobody offered him a workway and he asked for none, **the words *passage* and *privilege* are at zero across the ten files**, he is aged nowhere, **he is named in Chapters 0422 and 0428 and in no chapter of the ten in any form, and in both he is in a room reading a page.** **And the wall said nothing on all ten of these days, and the count of the silences is at item 12 and is printed in no chapter.** *Open.*

## 55. Rules established in Volume 09, Chapters 0431–0440 — FOUR MARKS IN THE SECOND OF THE FOUR PLACES, A MORNING WITH NOTHING TO FILL IN, A PAGE THAT WENT OUT, A RULE THAT WAS REFUSED, A TABLE WITH A RECTANGLE ON IT, AND A PAGE THAT CAME BACK DIFFERENT
