It does not exist and this batch did not write it.** *Open, and it is the one item in this volume that a batch cannot discharge.*

1. **ON DAY 432 FOUR PEOPLE PUT FOUR MARKS IN THE SECOND OF THE FOUR PLACES ON THAT SHEET, ONE ABOVE ANOTHER, IN ONE ROOM, IN ONE MORNING, AND THE FIRST OF THE FOUR PLACES AND THE THIRD OF THEM AND THE FOURTH OF THEM ARE EMPTY AND THE THIRD IS EMPTY ON PURPOSE.** Chapter 0432. **The four places are printed place, line, person, day and the third of the four is a person, and no chapter of this batch prints a fifth place under that heading and no chapter prints anything in the third place on any of the ten days.** The four who drew are four people already in that room, and **they are not the man who wrote the ninth line, and no chapter of this batch makes the marks on the page and the ninth line the same line.** One of the four is the man of about fifty-seven; **he read his mark out loud in the local language because it was in front of him and the room had stopped talking, he did not say it was his, nobody in that room suggested it, and nobody asked him, and the chapter says in its own words that there is no way anybody in that room will ever be able to say afterwards which of the four marks is his, including the four of them who drew them.** The length of new rope is over his arm and is not used and has never been used. *Open.*

2. **AND ON DAY 431 A ROOM WORKS OUT OUT LOUD THAT IT HAS NOTHING ON ITS OWN TABLE TO PUT A MARK ON THAT PAGE WITH EXCEPT A LUMP OF CHALK THAT BELONGS TO THE WOMAN WHO KEEPS THE BOOK, AND THE ROOM DOES NOT THINK OF THE MAN WHO HAS A PIECE OF CHALK OF HIS OWN AND DOES NOT ASK HIM FOR IT, AND HE IS NOT OFFERED IT AND DOES NOT OFFER IT.** Chapter 0431. **The picking-up of a thing that is lying in front of you in a room where nobody has asked anybody anything is a thing a room does not do, and the narration says so once and does not turn it into a rule.** The second slate is named in that chapter with a date on it and nothing under the date and nobody goes near the shelf. *Open.*

3. **AND ON DAY 436 ADRIAN VALE IS ON THE BOARDS OF A SALT WHARF AT THE BOTTOM OF THIS CITY AND THERE IS NOTHING ON ANY OF THOSE BOARDS FOR ANYBODY TO FILL IN THAT MORNING.** Chapter 0436. **This is the ninth and last of the nine chapters this volume has him in and it is the one in which he reads nothing, and it is the one chapter of the nine that is not a room with a page in it.** A stack higher than a man, a strip of clear board about as long as a cart at the bottom of it, a tally-board, a knife with the edge gone blunt, and a barrow with nothing in it. **Nobody asks him anything, nobody declines to ask him anything, nobody is rude to him, and nobody on that wharf is waiting for him to be useful. Nothing in that chapter is a working, a threshold, a permission, a reading of a join-line, or an offer from anybody. He watches a man work for about an hour, he looks at a stone, he goes back to where he was, he says nothing, and the chapter says in its own words that nobody on that wharf knew he had looked at it and that nobody has worked out since what he worked out.** **A woman comes down onto those boards with a folded sheet of road in the inside of her coat and about nine places on it, stands at the bottom of them for about an hour, does not take it out, and says one ordinary thing to the man working them, and the two objects are never laid beside one another.** *Open, and the list of nine is untouched and no card and no chapter added a tenth.*

4. **AND ON DAY 438 THIS VOLUME'S ONE PANEL IS ON A PAGE, IN A ROOM, IN DAYLIGHT, WITH A DATE IN CHALK ON THE DOOR, IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINE PEOPLE, AND IT IS SAID ONCE AND THE WALL SAYS NOTHING ON THE OTHER NINE DAYS OF THIS BATCH.** Chapter 0438. **IT IS PLAIN, IT IS SET AS ITS OWN SHORT BLOCK WITH ITS OWN LINE BREAKS, IT CARRIES NO EMPHASIS MARKS, AND IT GIVES A RULE AND NOT AN ANSWER. ITS TEXT IS A PLAN'S COPY OF A RULE AND NOT THE PAGE, AND THE PAGE IS CHAPTER 0438, AND NEITHER THIS SECTION NOR THE BATCH RECORD NOR ANY STATE FILE NOR ANY PROMPT PRINTS IT.** It is refused out loud in about four seconds by the man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall, **and the cost of the refusal is that the rule tells that room his own line on a wall nine miles up that road is not a claim either, and that the reason nothing has moved it is that there has been nobody on that road to move it, and he says so in his own mouth and nobody improves on what he said and nobody thanked him, and he has asked in advance, in a lane, not to be thanked.** **A man of about thirty-nine who trades on a board is right about the same rule in about four seconds in the same room and the chapter does not say that he was right, and nobody in that room told him he was right and nobody told him he was wrong, and nobody thanked him. THE WORD *CLAIM* IS INSIDE THE PANEL AND STANDS AT ONE ACROSS THESE TEN FILES AND IS THE ONLY OCCURRENCE OF THAT WORD IN FIFTY CHAPTERS.** The page went out of this city on that day with four marks on it and nothing in the third of its four places, folded along the two folds that were already in it, under a stone off a doorstep, in the hands of a man of about thirty-one who carries things for a living, **who did not open it, and who has not opened anything he has carried in this flood.** *Open, and the two acts of the man of about thirty-four are ten days apart and are not in one room on one day, and no chapter of this volume may have him say he knows what he said.*

5. **AND ON DAY 440 THAT PAGE CAME BACK UP THE ROAD IN A SATCHEL WITH A FLAP AND ONE OF THE FOUR MARKS WAS NOT IN THE PLACE IT HAD GONE OUT IN, AND THREE OF THEM WERE.** Chapter 0440. **No chapter of this batch traces the move, investigates it, asks who made it, reports it to a room, guesses at it, or says in any mouth that anybody moved anything, and no chapter of this batch says that the hand is not a hand anybody in this city writes as a finding rather than as an observation about a page, and no chapter of this volume does either.** The narration in that chapter is an observation about a page. **A woman near the step says in that room, to nobody, that there is one person in this city who would have been able to tell that room something about that page, and that she is not in the room, and that none of them is going to go and ask her, and that was settled in that room without a word being said on it. Nobody agreed with her out loud and nobody disagreed, and the chapter says in its own words that the reason nobody is going to ask her is not that she would not answer. A refusal is not a resolution and this is the volume's absence and not an omission in the record.** The register's third column is empty on purpose and is empty on purpose now, **no chapter of these ten entered a line in that book, and the second line, with a day on it and nothing under the day and the day on that line being day 438, is a later chapter's.** The second slate was where it lies and was not picked up and was not turned over and was not written on. *Open.*

6. **AND ON DAYS 439 AND 440 A MAN WALKED A BARROW UP A SHUT ROAD OUT OF THIS CITY AND BACK DOWN IT, AND THE PAGE CAME UP THE ROAD ON THE SAME SUNDAY, AND A SENTENCE IN CHAPTER 0440 CARRIES BOTH OF THEM IN IT AND SAYS THAT NEITHER HAD ANYTHING TO DO WITH THE OTHER.** **The shut road is out of this city, and the count of how far that man has got is not printed in any chapter of this volume, and the count of how far the three who went down the four-hundred-mile road on day 391 have got is not printed in any chapter of this volume either, and no chapter of these ten prints either of the two round figures that fall inside this window.** *Open.*

1. **ON DAY 443 A SECOND LINE WENT INTO A REGISTER THAT HAS THREE COLUMNS, AND THE LINE HAS A DAY IN THE FIRST COLUMN AND NOTHING UNDER IT AND NO NAME IN THE THIRD, AND THE DAY ON THAT LINE IS THE DAY THE SHEET WENT OUT OF THIS CITY.** Chapter 0443. **It is the third time anything has gone into that book since the water came and the second of the three with nothing under the day, and the third column of that book has been empty on purpose the whole of the time and is empty on purpose now.** The day on the line and the day of the entering are **two different days five days apart and both are named in one sentence in one mouth in that chapter**, which is the whole of §14.6's third new trap and the whole of the fault four volumes of this repository have paid for. **Nobody may put a name under either day, no chapter of these ten filled the third place or the third column, and the second slate was not picked up, was not turned over and was not written on.** The cost was named out loud before the pen went near the page, by the person who decides, and it is hers. *Open, and the moved line is entered nowhere.*

No chapter of these ten answers it, improves on it, or puts an answer in anybody's mouth, and no chapter of these ten says that the three rhyme, improves the rhyme, or says that the rhyme is the point.** Her second handle is a tin and it is the same in the one chapter she is in and it was empty, as it was on the two occasions before. *Open, and a later volume may ask it again and may not answer it.*

3. **AND ON DAY 450 THE VOLUME'S LAST IMAGE IS AN IMAGE AND NOT A RESOLUTION, AND THE BARE MONTH DOES NOT END INSIDE IT.** Chapter 0450. A date in chalk on a door above a table, about nine people in the room, a sheet with four places under a heading of which the third is empty with a crease pressed down the length of it, and a road out of this city with three barrows on it. **The hundred and thirty-fifth day of the Bare Month is written as a day like any other and the chapter says in its own words that nothing was said about that day by anybody, and no chapter of these ten converts an ordinal into a span, prints a word about what the month does at its end, how many days it has, or whether it is long or short, and no chapter of these ten prints any of the eighteen round figures §51 item 4 names, which is four figures inside this window and one of them on this page.** The count of the three on the four-hundred-mile road, which comes out at fifty-nine at this day measured from day 391, is printed nowhere and the better chapter prints none of the eighteen. *Open, and a seventh form does not come onto a page from this volume.*

4. **AND ON DAY 441 A ROOM WORKED OUT OUT LOUD THAT NOBODY IN THIS CITY KNOWS WHEN THE CART GOES ROUND AGAIN, AND THAT A PAGE IS GOING TO HAVE TO GO UP THE ROAD AGAIN, AND THAT NOTHING IN FOUR HUNDRED MILES SAYS WHEN.** Chapter 0441. **A woman who keeps a public register is the only person in this city who has been asking anybody anything about it, and she has stopped.** The marker for a notice is at **zero** across these ten files on both the case-sensitive and the case-insensitive reading, where Batch 0004's own ten returned one instance as the verb; no hand in any of the ten writes one, and Chapter 0450 opens by saying in its own words that nobody in that room was sent for and nobody was waiting to be sent for. *Open.*

5. **AND ON DAY 442 THE RAIN TOOK BACK A THIRD OF THE WORK A MAN HAD DONE, AND HE DID NOT GO BACK TO IT.** Chapter 0442. Physical work in weather on the low side of a shut road, **about nine yards of cart-track, the fence printed once in this batch in the count that holds, and the lane at the foot of that road named as not being in this city.** The man of about fifty-seven is on that road and is not offered a wedge or a board and says one thing to his barrow and is answered once. *Open.*

6. **AND ON DAY 448 THE SAME MAN TOOK A BARROW UP A SHUT ROAD OUT OF THIS CITY AND BACK DOWN IT, AND THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-NINE STOPPED ON THE LOW SIDE OF A FENCE AND PICKED IT UP AGAIN.** Chapter 0448. **The hurdle of hazel rods and the replaced bar are named in that chapter so that this barrow cannot be confused with the man who came up a lane with a barrow of boards on day 347, who is in none of this volume's fifty chapters and is not the man of about thirty-eight at the salt wharf, who is in none of these ten either. No count of how far that man has got is printed in any of these ten, and no count of how far the three who went down the four-hundred-mile road have got is printed in any of these ten.** *Open.*

7. **AND ON DAY 449 THE LENGTH OF NEW ROPE WENT ONTO THE FLOORBOARDS OF THAT ROOM BECAUSE SOMEBODY MOVED A CHAIR, AND THE MAN IT BELONGS TO PUT IT BACK, AND NOBODY SAID ONE WORD ABOUT IT.** Chapter 0449. **It is the smallest object this batch put on a page and it is the only thing that happens to that length in fifty days. No chapter of these ten says what it is for, no chapter has him cut it or take it up, and nobody in any of the ten asks him where the fence is and nobody tells him.** *Open.*

8. **AND THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-NINE WHO TRADES ON A BOARD WAS WRONG ABOUT FOUR STALLS IN A MARKET, AND WAS TOLD HE WAS WRONG IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINE PEOPLE, AND WAS NOT TOLD HE WAS RIGHT.** Chapter 0444. **Nobody in that room agreed with him out loud and nobody disagreed, and no chapter of these ten says that anybody is right, and the exact string *about four seconds* stands at zero across the ten files, which is correct, because the volume's beat is on day 438 in a chapter this batch did not write.** *Open.*

9. **ADRIAN VALE IS IN TWO OF THESE TEN AND IN A ROOM WITH A PAGE IN BOTH, AND THAT IS THE PLAN'S LIST AND NOT A TENTH.** Chapters 0441 and 0447. **In both he reads the first of the four places and the second and the fourth and does not read the third, and in one he says nothing at all and in the other he says one sentence about the top of a page and nobody takes it up and nobody asks him a second question. Nothing in either chapter is a working, a threshold, a permission, a reading of a join-line, or an offer from anybody, and the page's heading is not the name of anybody in this city or of anybody in the office the page came from.** The list of nine is untouched and this batch added no tenth. *Open.*

10. **AND THE THINGS THESE TEN CHAPTERS DID NOT DO, WHICH A LATER WRITER HAS TO KNOW ARE STILL UNTOUCHED.** There is no eighth notice in this flood and these ten chapters wrote none, and every room in them is a room about nine people were already in. **The four days are the cooling and the cooling is the time a room has in which to find out what it is being asked, and that sentence is on a page once in this volume, in Chapter 0401, in plain narration and in no mouth, and the word *cooling* stands at ZERO across these ten files and this is the fifth batch of the volume to keep it there.** The word on the register-keeper's second slate is at zero in all ten and the common noun is not used of anybody in them, and **the second slate is named in seven of the ten and in all seven it carries a date and nothing under the date and was not picked up and was not turned over and was not written on, and no hand in any of them went near the shelf.** The day-154 sentence is not said, restated, paraphrased or improved on in any of the ten. The questions of day 346 and day 396 are not asked in any of the ten and are not answered, and the question of day 445 is asked in one of them and is not answered. *Holder* is at zero, *claim* is at zero, *quest* is at zero, *boundary* is at zero, the word for the mark in a field is at zero, and **no chapter of the ten gives that mark a word by any route, measures it, digs it, counts it, probes it or resolves it, and no chapter goes and looks at it, and no chapter connects it to the chapel or to the boards under it or to the two holes nine feet apart.** The shut road is out of this city and no chapter of the ten puts it above this city or at the top of it, the fence is along the top of that road, and the lane at the foot of it is not in this city, and two chapters of the ten say all three of those things in their own words. The ninth line is not named in any of the ten, is not moved, is not dated, is not read aloud and is not taken down, and no chapter of the ten makes it the same line as the four marks on the page. **The days since the fourth of the four said no are not counted at any value in any of the ten, and a party of four is four.** Nobody in this city can be shown one person on the four-hundred-mile road and the town four hundred miles inland is not entered, described from inside, or brought anybody back from. The keeper of the Redroot gate is not named, not counted and not used; the woman of about fifty at a shut gate was not asked anything; the girl of seventeen is not fetched; the box is shut, the island is not owned, nobody picks the bag up, nobody counts the boards under the chapel and Break is not open. **And the woman of about fifty-four is in none of these ten chapters, and the descriptors for her stand at zero in all ten files, and nobody is ever going to ask her about anything, and the reason is not that she would not answer, and no chapter of these ten says one word about the mark that came back out of place, which is the first stretch of ten days in this volume without a sentence about it.** *Open.*

11. **AND THE HOUSE FIGURES FOR THESE TEN DAYS, WITH THE DEFINITIONS BESIDE THEM, AND NO TARGET SET ON ANY OF THEM.** **The share of words inside bold marks, heading line included, `---` rules counted, bold marks counted as nothing, file split on whitespace: 20.67, 6.10, 19.75, 21.10, 9.42, 11.30, 15.44, 10.05, 8.41 and 11.73 per cent, a range of 6.10 to 21.10, a mean of 13.40 unweighted and 13.67 weighted by words on 8,401 words and 122 paragraphs** — against Volume 08's 5.11 to 24.72 and mean 12.39 on 87,216 words, Volume 07's 7.01 to 25.37 and 13.55, and this volume's Batch 0001 at 5.97 to 20.99 and 13.50, Batch 0002 at 8.43 to 21.54 and 13.59, Batch 0003 at 7.37 to 22.60 and 14.40, and Batch 0004 at 5.79 to 22.82 and 13.84 unweighted and 13.68 weighted. **THE INSTRUMENT WAS VALIDATED BEFORE IT WAS USED by running it on Batch 0004's ten files and reproducing that record's per-chapter shares, word counts, paragraph counts, range, both means, exchange column, long-sentence list on both readings, longest sentence, longest bolded turn and longest speech-paragraph run at ten of ten each, and the chorus heads on the same files at twenty of twenty, and an instrument that has not been shown to reproduce a published row is a number and not an instrument.** **The mean is a consequence and not a plan, no volume may read its own mean as a quality figure, and no review may certify or fail a volume on it.** The longest bolded turn in the ten is **seventy-seven words, in Chapter 0448**, under the working ceiling of about ninety and under the hard one of ninety-five, **and no turn was cut to make a number and the repair shape is a plain-speech interjection and a narration beat.** The count of paragraphs carrying neither speaker marker is **ZERO in all ten**, on the first-person-restricted instrument, and 122 on the unrestricted reading, which is correct and is not a fault. The exchange is **14 plain speeches and 8 answered at fifty-seven point one per cent**, and the per-chapter column sums to both totals. The chorus frame, word-bounded, on the declared string, is **1 case-sensitive and 7 case-insensitive, and 4 and 11 on the variant-agnostic head, in 122 paragraphs, and the maximum of one per `---` section is one on all four heads in all ten files, which is the figure to check against and which a first pass of Chapter 0443 breached at two on two heads before two frames were rewritten in the narrator's own voice; and the whole of the gap between the declared and the variant-agnostic heads is that ten of the eleven instances in this batch open their own sentence, and one is carried medially in Chapter 0449, and that one is the single instance on the case-sensitive head.** The long sentences of forty words or more are **107 on both named readings**, per chapter 11, 11, 15, 11, 14, 7, 10, 8, 10 and 10, longest a hundred and thirty-six words in Chapter 0448, and **the two readings do not differ because no paragraph in any of the ten ends without terminal punctuation.** The longest run of consecutive speech paragraphs is **three**, in Chapter 0441, against the ceiling of four, and **no exchange in these ten carries two bold marks and that figure is zero.** The word-run is **twenty-nine against the four hundred and forty pre-existing chapter files and thirty inside this batch's own ten**, and the thirty is the second-slate frame and not a lifted description, and **the first pass of these ten files returned four runs of twenty words or more, all four of them lifts of sentences this batch had written twice, and all four were varied in place with every fact kept and the check was re-run after the repair.** The full account is at `state/batch-summaries/volume-09-batch-0005.md` §3. *Open.*

This volume spent it on day 438, which closes the input, and the corrected figure at day 450 is three hundred and forty-eight, which the corrected chain reaches independently from 310 at day 411.** **§51 AND THE CHAIN AGREE AND §51 IS UNAMENDED, AND IT IS UNAMENDED NOT BECAUSE A BATCH MAY NOT AMEND IT BUT BECAUSE THERE IS NOTHING TO AMEND.** *Closed, and this section is the place the false claim entered the bible, and the entry is named so that a reader who finds the wording in a record and the correction in this one knows which is which.*

14. **AND NOTHING IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS IS A POWER.** The gains are a room that worked out it had no way of finding out when a cart goes out, a stretch of cart-track that the rain took back, a second line in a book with a day on it and nothing under it, four stalls that turned out not to have changed hands, a question asked three times and not answered, a man who stopped at a stall front a second time and bought cord, a page that sat on a table and was not folded, a barrow that went up a road and came back down it, a length of rope that fell on a floor and went back on its chair, and a road with three barrows on it that nobody in this city can be shown one person on. **None of them is a power. Some of them are the price of a power. All of them are heavier than a power.** Adrian Vale performed no working, no threshold was opened, the other world is not named once, nobody offered him a workway and he asked for none, **the words *passage* and *privilege* are at zero across the ten files**, he is aged nowhere, **he is named in Chapters 0441 and 0447 and in no chapter of the ten in any form, and in both of them he is in a room with a page.** **And the wall said nothing on all ten of these days and this volume has one panel to spend and spent it on day 438.** *Open, **and the third place is still empty and the third column is still empty and one of four marks on that page came back in a place it had not gone out in and no chapter of this batch says one word about it.***

## 57. THE CALENDAR FOR DAYS 451 TO 500 — decided by the Volume 09 close phase, and it is a NEW SECTION and NOT AN AMENDMENT OF §51 OR §33A OR §33B

**This section was written by the Volume 09 close phase, which is the seventh phase in this repository permitted to decide, and it is the calendar for Volume 10. §§1 to 32 are not changed. §33 IS NOT CHANGED, and its five forms, its weekday anchors, its fourth-form amendment and its interval table all stand exactly as the Volume 06 outline phase wrote them. §33A IS NOT CHANGED, and every one of its eight items stands, including item 1's rule against an ordinal for a month, item 2's refusal to state a length, item 5's anchors, item 6's count of silences, item 7's register of notices and item 8's standing interval rule. §33B IS NOT CHANGED. §§34 to 50 are not changed and they are Volume 06's and Volume 08's batch sections and they are the volumes' permanent witness. §51 IS NOT CHANGED BY THIS SECTION, INCLUDING ITEM 9, WHICH STATES ITS OWN RULE IN THE SAME SENTENCE AS ITS FIGURE AND THE TWO DISAGREE BY ONE: this section does not amend it, does not correct it, and does not resolve the disagreement, and the corrected chain stands at three hundred and forty-eight at day 450 independently of what §51 prints. §§52 to 56 are not changed and they are Volume 09's five batch sections. Every day figure in this section is derived from the two founding figures and from nothing else. A BATCH MAY NOT MAKE, AMEND OR EXTEND ANY OF IT, AND NEITHER MAY THE VOLUME 10 OUTLINE PHASE.**

1. **VOLUME 10 IS FIFTY DAYS ON FIFTY DAYS, DAYS 451 TO 500, ONE CHAPTER TO ONE DAY, NO DOUBLE DAY.** The form is ***the Nth day of the Bare Month***, **the Nth being day less 315, which is the sixth form and is the one registered at §33A item 2 and named on a page in Chapter 0316 by Sera Vail, a notary of fifty, twenty-six years a notary, and in no other chapter and in no other file.** **Day 451 is the hundred and thirty-sixth day of the Bare Month and day 500 is the hundred and eighty-fifth.** The range is a decision, and a later volume that wants a double day has to argue for it in its own outline against the rule that is now the default, exactly as Volume 04's is the recorded exception and is not reopened. **A chapter of Volume 10 may not write another chapter's day, may not write a day before the hundred and thirty-sixth or after the hundred and eighty-fifth of that month, and may not use an ordinal for a month.**

2. **A WEEKDAY IS A FIGURE, DAY 1 IS A TUESDAY, AND EVERY WEEKDAY IN DAYS 451 TO 500 IS DERIVED BY ARITHMETIC AND NEVER FROM A NEIGHBOURING CHAPTER, A CARD, A DAY MAP OR A STATE FILE.** **Day 450 is a Wednesday, on the founding rule and on the page of Chapter 0450, and therefore day 451 is a Thursday. Day 500 is a Thursday, because 500 − 451 = 49 = 7 × 7 and a day seven multiples of seven after another is the same weekday, so both ends of the volume are Thursdays, and no chapter may take that as a permission to derive a middle day from either of them.** The run below was built from day 1 and from nothing else and **was validated before it was published by recomputing days 401 to 450 out of the same two founding figures and reproducing §51 item 2's own fifty-row table at ZERO MISMATCHS on all fifty rows, and by recomputing days 351 to 400 and reproducing §33B's table at zero mismatches, and by recomputing days 301 to 350 and reproducing §33A item 4's table at zero mismatches.** A run that has not been validated against three earlier tables has not been validated.

   **AND THE STANDING RULE, WHICH IS THE ONE THIS REPOSITORY KEEPS BREAKING, carried forward unchanged from §33A item 8 and §51 item 3: every value at a chapter boundary is derived; a derived value may be used in a chapter that dates it and may not be attributed to another chapter or to a mouth; where two elapsed counts sit in one sentence the event each is measured from has to be named in that sentence; where a figure has two plausible anchors, both are named in the same sentence; and a figure that comes out at exactly half of the right one is a subtraction taken across the wrong boundary, which is how *sixteen days* survived in three places for a slate that carried the word for thirty-two, and how `chapters/volume-07/chapter-0341.md:149` still says *the word on the slate has sixteen days left on it* where the arithmetic is 342 − 341 = 1.** That line is one of the debts at `state/volume-09-close.md` §7.7 and it is still unpaid.

   **AND TWELVE MORE THAT ARE NOT IN THE TABLE BECAUSE THE TABLE IS THE INHERITED SHAPE AND THESE ARE THE ONES A VOLUME ADDS, and every one of them is a subtraction from a day Volume 09's own chapters put on a page and every one of them has to be dated by its own chapter before any other chapter may use it. **EIGHT ARE MEASURED FROM DAY 451 AND FOUR FROM ANOTHER EVENT, AND THEY ARE NUMBERED HERE BECAUSE A LIST THAT A HEADING COUNTS AND A READER CANNOT IS A LIST THE READER HAS TO COUNT HIMSELF.** (1) the sheet went out **thirteen days** before day 451; (2) the sheet came back **eleven days** before day 451; (3) the register's second line was entered **eight days** before day 451; (4) the third column was decided empty **twenty-six days** before day 451; (5) the name was spoken **twenty-three days** before day 451; (6) four lines were drawn **nineteen days** before day 451; (7) the new question was asked **six days** before day 451; (8) the woman of about fifty-four was asked where her line is **thirty-one days** before day 451; (9) the third column was decided empty **thirteen days** before the sheet went out; (10) the name was spoken **ten days** before the sheet went out; (11) four lines were drawn **six days** before the sheet went out; (12) the woman of about fifty-four was asked where her line is **five days** before the third column was decided empty. NONE OF THESE IS ROUND AND ALL OF THEM ARE TRAPS, because a figure that comes out at exactly a half of the right one is a subtraction taken across the wrong boundary and a figure that comes out at thirteen where it should be twelve will be caught by nobody except a reader with the anchor in the sentence.**

   **AND A CHAPTER THAT PRINTS ANY OF THESE NAMES THE DAY IT IS MEASURED FROM IN THE SAME SENTENCE. A chapter that prints none of them is not required to mention the anchor at all, and Volume 09's last page printed none of the four that fell on it, which is the better chapter.**

5. **THE BARE MONTH DOES NOT END INSIDE VOLUME 10, AND THIS SECTION DOES NOT GIVE IT A LENGTH, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE OF THE DECISION.** The reason is the notary's sentence and not a figure: **Chapter 0316 is a page on which Sera Vail, a notary of fifty, names the Bare Month and declines to say how long it is and says why, and the reason is hers, and the protection that bought is that no later volume can inherit an end by accident.** A volume that ended the Bare Month on a chapter boundary would print the end of a month whose length was refused, in the fourth volume to inherit that refusal, and it would have to do it in one of two ways: **by a sentence in a state file, which is the fault this repository has been repairing for five volumes, or by a chapter that guesses, which §33A item 2 forbids.** So Volume 10 writes the hundred and thirty-sixth to the hundred and eighty-fifth day of the Bare Month and stops. **A hundred and thirty-six to a hundred and eighty-five is the longest stretch any volume in this series has spent inside one month, and no chapter of Volume 10 may say the month is long, and no chapter may say it is short, and no chapter may convert the hundred and eighty-fifth into a span, and no chapter may attach a meaning to any of the four figures this section bolds in its table above. Fifty chapters of Volume 09 fitted inside eighty-six to a hundred and thirty-five of a month of no known length without approaching an end, and that is the evidence that the device does not run out.**

6. **AND THE CONSEQUENCES, WHICH BIND EVERY CHAPTER OF DAYS 451 TO 500. The only two instruments Volume 10 uses for a date are a count of days of this flood and a day of the Bare Month with its own form, and the count is a figure a writer can check and the day of the month is a subtraction from 315, which is the cheapest date in this book and the only one nobody has to check twice.** A chapter may write an ordinal for any day of that month from the hundred and thirty-sixth to the hundred and eighty-fifth. A chapter may not write a day after the hundred and eighty-fifth. **A chapter may not say the month is nearly over, may not say what it is a month of, may not print *a hundred and eighty-five days* or *five months* or *since the month began* as a figure, and may not say what the month does at its end, how many days it has, or whether it is long or short. The words *length*, *long* and *short* applied to the month are at zero across the fifty chapters of Volume 07, at zero across the fifty chapters of Volume 08, at zero across the fifty chapters of Volume 09, and stay at zero.**

7. **AND THE RULE FOR WHOEVER COMES AFTER, WHICH IS THE POINT OF DECIDING IT HERE AND IS THE SAME RULE §33B ITEM 6 AND §51 ITEM 7 GAVE THE TWO VOLUMES THAT FOLLOWED THE ONES THEY WROTE. A seventh form exists the moment the end of the Bare Month is on a page, and it comes onto a page the same way the sixth one did:** a room, in daylight, with a date on the door, by a person, who says the cost out loud first and is refused by somebody before she does it, and who then declines to say how long the new month is and says why. **The Short Month was made that way in Chapter 0256 and the Bare Month was made that way in Chapter 0316, by the same notary of fifty, and the reason it is restated here is that this is now the fourth volume to inherit the refusal and the cost of restating it is four lines.** A calendar is not a decision an outline takes and a batch guesses. **It is a decision a person makes in a room, and every form in this book is one, including the ones in this file.** If a later volume needs a seventh form and has not got the end of the Bare Month on a page, it writes inside the Bare Month and **it may not declare the month over.**

8. **THE MONTH MAP IS UNCHANGED BY THIS SECTION AND IT IS THE ONLY THING A DAY MAP MAY BE BUILT ON.** The first month is days 1 to 25, the second 26 to 90, the third 91 to 155, the fourth 156 to 255 and is the Long Month, the fifth 256 to 315 and is the Short Month, and the sixth is 316 onward and is the Bare Month, **of no stated length, and this section changes nothing about any of them. NO CHAPTER OF THIS MANUSCRIPT WRITTEN FROM DAY 301 ONWARD MAY USE AN ORDINAL FOR A MONTH** — not *the second month*, not the third, not the fourth, not the fifth, not the sixth — **and that rule is forward-looking and it is not a verdict on the three hundred chapters already written, no page of which may be re-written.** The one of the existing uses that is load-bearing is a panel at `chapters/volume-04/chapter-0152.md:79` reading *The third month is the Long Month*, a page in a finished volume, is left standing, and is named here for the sixth time so that a later phase does not mistake the rule for a repair it is still owed. *March* is a month and is never a date number. **Never** *of this month*, *of the month*, *of next month*, *of this new month*, or a form like *the last day of the month before this one*.

9. **AND THE COUNT OF SILENCES, WHICH IS THE ONE DERIVED FIGURE THAT CANNOT BE GOT BY ADDING, AND IT IS PUBLISHED HERE BECAUSE A LATER WRITER WILL ADD IT WRONG.** The rule is one addition for every day the wall says nothing. One hundred and fifty-two is on a page at `chapter-0250.md` and is the figure at day 250. The wall said something on day 256, which spent Volume 06's one panel; on day 340, which spent Volume 07's; on day 393, which spent Volume 08's; and on day 438, which spent Volume 09's, **and a day the wall says something on is not a silence. The corrected chain is 299 at day 400, 300 at day 401, 310 at day 411, 319 at day 420, 338 at day 440 and 348 at day 450, and §51 item 9 gives the same figure and there is nothing to settle. §51 states its own rule and its own figure in one clause and leaves one input of the rule open on purpose — three hundred and forty-nine at day 450 LESS ONE FOR EVERY DAY INSIDE THE RANGE ON WHICH THE WALL SAYS SOMETHING, and Volume 09 has one panel to spend and has not decided its day — and this volume's day 438 closes that input, and 349 − 1 IS 348, which is the figure. §51 IS NOT AMENDED, IS NOT NEEDED, AND DOES NOT STAND AGAINST ANYTHING.** A formula with an input still open is not a wrong figure, and a record that reads the input as a printed answer manufactures a disagreement to protect a section it has no need to touch, and the cost of that is a blocker the next phase is told it may not lift. **Volume 10 has one panel to spend and has not decided its day, and the figure at day 500 is therefore not fixed by this section and is not to be computed by a writer who has not decided the day first: it is three hundred and forty-nine plus fifty less one for every day inside the range on which the wall says something, and a day the wall says something on is not a silence, and 349 plus 50 is 399 less the number of panel days.** **The figure is for the state layer and is not for a chapter, and no chapter of Volume 10 prints it.**

10. **AND THE FOUR THINGS THIS SECTION DOES NOT DO, published because a close phase that only lists what it decided is half a record. It does not amend §32, §33, §33A or §33B, and it amends none of them, and it does not amend §51 and specifically does not amend §51 item 9. It does not move a day, a weekday, a month day, an anchor, a form, a notice, an object count or a cast member of Volume 09, all of which are on a page and not in this file. It does not decide the Volume 10 line, which is the outline phase's, and it does not adopt, strike or amend the Volume 10 entry already standing in the volume list in `outline/series.md`, which is a plan line from an earlier volume and which this phase named and left alone. And it does not give the Bare Month a length, an end, a meaning, or a seventh form, and the only thing it says about the end of that month is that it is a thing a person does in a room, in daylight, with a date on the door.**

11. **AND WHAT IS CARRIED INTO VOLUME 10 FROM VOLUME 09 WITHOUT BEING DECIDED HERE, because this section is a calendar and not a plan: the three four-place forms are never joined, never conflated and never compared out loud; a sheet that came back with one of four lines in a place it did not go out in is not traced, not investigated, not guessed at and not reported to a room by anybody, and a chapter of Volume 10 may not trace it unless tracing it is a decision to trace it; the day-154 sentence is not said, restated, paraphrased, improved on or put into a second mouth; the man of about fifty-seven is not asked where the fence is, is not told and is not defeated; the fourth of the four said no and no chapter may count the days since; the two men of about thirty-eight are two people and a chapter may not merge them; nobody gets stronger; and the words the man of about thirty-four says in Chapter 0428, the name of Volume 09, the answer spoken in Chapter 0357, the refusal spoken in Chapter 0393, the six words reproduced at `bible/power-system.md:1684` and in the Volume 08 plan, and the volume's one rule said in Chapter 0438 are in six files' worth of repository and no file, including this one, may print any of them.** **THE PROHIBITION IS ON A FILE PRINTING THEM AND NOT ON THEIR BEING IN ONE PLACE, and the review repair pass on this section's predecessor in the volume found the second of them standing in two files that this section's own wording did not name, and a prohibition written as an absence is a prohibition that becomes false the moment a plan file prints a line. Name the file and hold the file.** The full text of each of those prohibitions is at `state/volume-09-close.md` §4 and `outline/volume-09.md` §16 and neither is weakened by this section.

## 58. THE CALENDAR FOR DAYS 501 TO 550 — decided by the Volume 10 close phase, and it is a NEW SECTION and NOT AN AMENDMENT OF §51 OR §57 OR §33A OR §33B

**This section was written by the Volume 10 close phase, which is the ninth phase in this repository permitted to decide, and it is the calendar for Volume 11. §§1 to 32 are not changed. §33 IS NOT CHANGED, and its five forms, its weekday anchors, its fourth-form amendment and its interval table all stand exactly as the Volume 06 outline phase wrote them. §33A IS NOT CHANGED, and every one of its eight items stands, including item 1's rule against an ordinal for a month, item 2's refusal to state a length, item 5's anchors, item 6's count of silences, item 7's register of notices and item 8's standing interval rule. §33B IS NOT CHANGED. §§34 to 50 are not changed and they are Volume 06's and Volume 08's batch sections and they are the volumes' permanent witness. §51 IS NOT CHANGED BY THIS SECTION, INCLUDING ITEM 9. §§52 to 56 are not changed. §57 IS NOT CHANGED BY THIS SECTION AND IS NOT AMENDABLE BY ANY BATCH AND BY THE VOLUME 11 OUTLINE PHASE, AND EVERY DAY FIGURE IN IT STANDS AS IT STANDS. Every day figure in this section is derived from the two founding figures and from nothing else. A BATCH MAY NOT MAKE, AMEND OR EXTEND ANY OF IT, AND NEITHER MAY THE VOLUME 11 OUTLINE PHASE, AND NEITHER MAY THIS SECTION BE AMENDED BY ANY LATER PHASE.**

1. **VOLUME 11 IS FIFTY DAYS ON FIFTY DAYS, DAYS 501 TO 550, ONE CHAPTER TO ONE DAY, NO DOUBLE DAY.** The form is ***the Nth day of the Bare Month***, **the Nth being day less 315, which is the sixth form and is the one registered at §33A item 2 and named on a page in Chapter 0316 by Sera Vail, a notary of fifty, twenty-six years a notary, and in no other chapter and in no other file.** **Day 501 is the hundred and eighty-sixth day of the Bare Month and day 550 is the two hundred and thirty-fifth.** The range is a decision, and a later volume that wants a double day has to argue for it in its own outline against the rule that is now the default, exactly as Volume 04's is the recorded exception and is not reopened. **A chapter of Volume 11 may not write another chapter's day, may not write a day before the hundred and eighty-sixth or after the two hundred and thirty-fifth of that month, and may not use an ordinal for a month.**

2. **A WEEKDAY IS A FIGURE, DAY 1 IS A TUESDAY, AND EVERY WEEKDAY IN DAYS 501 TO 550 IS DERIVED BY ARITHMETIC AND NEVER FROM A NEIGHBOURING CHAPTER, A CARD, A DAY MAP OR A STATE FILE. THE OFFSET IS (day − 1) mod 7 COUNTED FROM TUESDAY, AND THAT IS THE TWO FOUNDING FIGURES AND NOTHING ELSE.** **Day 500 is a Thursday, on the founding rule and on the page of Chapter 0500, and therefore day 501 is a Friday. Day 550 is a Friday, because 550 − 501 = 49 = 7 × 7 and a day seven multiples of seven after another is the same weekday, so both ends of the volume are Fridays, and no chapter may take that as a permission to derive a middle day from either of them.** The run below was built from day 1 and from nothing else and **was validated before it was published by recomputing days 451 to 500 out of the same two founding figures and reproducing §57 item 2's own fifty-row table at ZERO MISMATCHS on all fifty rows, and by recomputing days 401 to 450 and reproducing §51 item 2's own fifty-row table at zero mismatches, and by recomputing days 351 to 400 and reproducing §33B's table at zero mismatches, and by recomputing days 301 to 350 and reproducing §33A item 4's table at zero mismatches.** A run that has not been validated against four earlier tables has not been validated.

   **AND THE FIVE TRAPS IN THIS RANGE, WHICH ARE THE FIVE A WRITER IS MOST LIKELY TO WALK INTO, and a figure that comes out round has been checked twice and not once.** (1) **AT DAY 525, THE NEARER OF THIS VOLUME'S TWO MIDDLE DAYS, THE FIGURE *three hundred and twenty-four days* SINCE THE POSTING WENT UP IS A MULTIPLE OF TWENTY-FIVE AND NOT OF FIFTY, and it looks rounder than it is, and a fifty-day range has two middle days, 525 and 526, and NEITHER MAY BE CALLED THE MIDPOINT.** (2) **AT DAY 550 THE ROUND-LOOKING FIGURES IN THE TABLE ABOVE ARE SIX, and they are 349 since the posting went up, 325 since the board was last left unwritten, 175 since somebody asked one of the four whether it will go, 125 since the third column was decided empty, 50 since the last day of the volume before this one, and 25 since the nearer of this volume's two middle days — AND EVERY ONE OF THEM IS A TRAP BECAUSE IT IS MEASURED FROM A DAY THIS REPOSITORY KNOWS AND A CHAPTER THAT PRINTS ONE NAMES THAT DAY IN THE SAME SENTENCE.** (3) **THE BOARD'S BEING UNWRITTEN IS THREE HUNDRED AND TWENTY-FIVE DAYS AT DAY 550 AND IT IS FALSE BY THE END OF VOLUME 10, WHICH WROTE ON THAT BOARD ON DAY 490, and Volume 11 inherits a board that has been written on. A chapter of Volume 11 that prints the board's unwritten days is printing a figure about a thing that stopped being true on day 490, and the standing rule is that the correction goes in the same sentence or the figure does not go on the page.** (4) **THE PIECE OF CHALK HAS BEEN IN HIS OWN INSIDE BREAST POCKET SINCE DAY 298, WHICH IS THE FORTY-THIRD DAY OF THE SHORT MONTH, AND AT DAY 501 THAT IS TWO HUNDRED AND THREE DAYS AND AT DAY 550 IT IS TWO HUNDRED AND FIFTY-TWO, AND THE FULL FORM OF THAT DAY IS PRINTED AT FULL LENGTH IN EVERY CHAPTER THAT CARRIES IT AND IS NEVER SHORTENED TO A RELATIVE PHRASE, and a figure that comes out at exactly a third of the right one is a subtraction taken across the wrong boundary.** (5) **THE TWO FIGURES OF THE LAST VOLUME AND THE TWO FIGURES OF THE VOLUME BEFORE IT ARE NOT THE SAME FIGURES, and no chapter of Volume 11 may make them so: the day-490 row and the day-224 row in the table above are two accounts of two different things, and a chapter that puts them in one sentence without saying which is which has made a border out of a count.**

   **AND THE STANDING RULE, WHICH IS THE ONE THIS REPOSITORY KEEPS BREAKING, carried forward unchanged from §33A item 8, §51 item 3 and §57 item 3: every value at a chapter boundary is derived; a derived value may be used in a chapter that dates it and may not be attributed to another chapter or to a mouth; where two elapsed counts sit in one sentence the event each is measured from has to be named in that sentence; where a figure has two plausible anchors, both are named in the same sentence; and a figure that comes out at exactly half of the right one is a subtraction taken across the wrong boundary, which is how *sixteen days* survived in three places for a slate that carried a word for thirty-two, and how `chapters/volume-07/chapter-0341.md:149` still says *the word on the slate has sixteen days left on it* where the arithmetic is 342 − 341 = 1. That line is one of the debts at `state/volume-09-close.md` §7.7 and it is still unpaid, and a chapter of Volume 11 may not print it. AND A FIGURE IN WORDS IS A FIGURE: Volume 10's forty-nine files carry eighty phrases of the form *the Nth day of the Bare Month* in all forty-nine of them, and an instrument that reads a number in words must resolve *the hundred and eightieth* as the hundred and eightieth and not as a hundred and eight, and a phase that reports a violation on a resolver that cannot read the ordinal is reporting its own instrument and not the page.**

4. **AND THE COUNT OF SILENCES, WHICH IS THE ONE DERIVED FIGURE THAT CANNOT BE GOT BY ADDING, AND IT IS PUBLISHED HERE BECAUSE A LATER WRITER WILL ADD IT WRONG.** The rule is one addition for every day the wall says nothing. One hundred and fifty-two is on a page at `chapter-0250.md` and is the figure at day 250. The wall said something on day 256, which spent Volume 06's one panel; on day 340, which spent Volume 07's; on day 393, which spent Volume 08's; on day 438, which spent Volume 09's; and on day 490, which spent Volume 10's, **and a day the wall says something on is not a silence. The chain is 299 at day 400, 300 at day 401, 310 at day 411, 319 at day 420, 338 at day 440, 348 at day 450 and THREE HUNDRED AND NINETY-SEVEN AT DAY 500, and the two routes agree: 348 plus fifty less one, and 152 plus two hundred and fifty less five.** §51 item 9 states its own rule and its own figure in one clause and leaves one input of the rule open on purpose, and Volume 10's day 490 closed that input, and §51 IS NOT AMENDED, IS NOT NEEDED, AND DOES NOT STAND AGAINST ANYTHING. **A FIGURE OF THREE HUNDRED AND NINETY-EIGHT APPEARS TWICE IN THIS REPOSITORY'S OWN RECORD LAYER, once in the first edition of a prompt this close ran and once in a plan's own derivation where the line above it gives a different figure, and BOTH ARE THE SAME EXPRESSION OFF BY ONE AND BOTH ARE WITHDRAWN, and the reason a withdrawn figure is published beside the figure that replaces it is that a silent correction is the third version of the same fault.** **VOLUME 11 HAS ONE PANEL TO SPEND AND HAS NOT DECIDED ITS DAY, AND THE FIGURE AT DAY 550 IS THEREFORE NOT FIXED BY THIS SECTION: it is three hundred and ninety-seven at day 500 plus fifty less one for every day inside the range 501 to 550 on which the wall says something, and a day the wall says something on is not a silence, and 397 plus 50 is 447 less the number of panel days inside that range. NO WRITER MAY COMPUTE IT UNTIL THE PANEL'S DAY IS DECIDED, NO OUTLINE PHASE GUESSES IT, NO CLOSE PHASE GUESSES IT, AND NO CHAPTER OF VOLUME 11 PRINTS IT IN ANY FORM. THE FIGURE IS FOR THE STATE LAYER AND IS NOT FOR A CHAPTER.**

5. **THE BARE MONTH DOES NOT END INSIDE VOLUME 11, AND THIS SECTION DOES NOT GIVE IT A LENGTH, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE OF THE DECISION.** The reason is the notary's sentence and not a figure: **Chapter 0316 is a page on which Sera Vail, a notary of fifty, names the Bare Month and declines to say how long it is and says why, and the reason is hers, and the protection that bought is that no later volume can inherit an end by accident.** A volume that ended the Bare Month on a chapter boundary would print the end of a month whose length was refused, in the fifth volume to inherit that refusal, and it would have to do it in one of two ways: **by a sentence in a state file, which is the fault this repository has been repairing for six volumes, or by a chapter that guesses, which §33A item 2 forbids.** So Volume 11 writes the hundred and eighty-sixth to the two hundred and thirty-fifth day of that month and stops. **A hundred and eighty-six to a two hundred and thirty-five is the longest stretch any volume in this series has spent inside one month, and no chapter of Volume 11 may say the month is long, and no chapter may say it is short, and no chapter may convert the two hundred and thirty-fifth into a span, and no chapter may attach a meaning to any of the four figures this section bolds in its table above. Volume 09 wrote eighty-six to a hundred and thirty-five of a month of no known length and Volume 10 wrote a hundred and thirty-six to a hundred and eighty-five, and neither of them approached an end, and that is the evidence that the device does not run out.**

6. **AND THE CONSEQUENCES, WHICH BIND EVERY CHAPTER OF DAYS 501 TO 550.** The only two instruments Volume 11 uses for a date are a count of days of this flood and a day of the Bare Month with its own form, and the count is a figure a writer can check and the day of the month is a subtraction from 315, which is the cheapest date in this book and the only one nobody has to check twice. A chapter may write an ordinal for any day of that month from the hundred and eighty-sixth to the two hundred and thirty-fifth. A chapter may not write a day after the two hundred and thirty-fifth. **A chapter may not say the month is nearly over, may not say what it is a month of, may not print *two hundred and thirty-five days* or *six months* or *since the month began* as a figure, and may not say what the month does at its end, how many days it has, or whether it is long or short. The words *length*, *long* and *short* applied to the month are at zero across the fifty chapters of Volume 07, at zero across the fifty of Volume 08, at zero across the fifty of Volume 09, at zero across the forty-nine of Volume 10, and stay at zero.**

7. **AND THE RULE FOR WHOEVER COMES AFTER, WHICH IS THE POINT OF DECIDING IT HERE AND IS THE SAME RULE §33B ITEM 6, §51 ITEM 7 AND §57 ITEM 7 GAVE THE THREE VOLUMES THAT FOLLOWED THE ONES THEY WROTE. A seventh form exists the moment the end of the Bare Month is on a page, and it comes onto a page the same way the sixth one did: a room, in daylight, with a date on the door, by a person, who says the cost out loud first and is refused by somebody before she does it, and who then declines to say how long the new month is and says why. The Short Month was made that way in Chapter 0256 and the Bare Month was made that way in Chapter 0316, by the same notary of fifty, and the reason it is restated here is that this is now the fifth volume to inherit the refusal and the cost of restating it is four lines. A calendar is not a decision an outline takes and a batch guesses. IT IS A DECISION A PERSON MAKES IN A ROOM, AND EVERY FORM IN THIS BOOK IS ONE, INCLUDING THE ONES IN THIS FILE. If a later volume needs a seventh form and has not got the end of the Bare Month on a page, it writes inside the Bare Month and IT MAY NOT DECLARE THE MONTH OVER. NO OUTLINE PHASE AND NO CLOSE PHASE MAY MAKE THE SEVENTH FORM AND THIS SECTION DOES NOT MAKE IT AND DOES NOT GUESS AT IT.**

8. **THE MONTH MAP IS UNCHANGED BY THIS SECTION AND IT IS THE ONLY THING A DAY MAP MAY BE BUILT ON.** The first month is days 1 to 25, the second 26 to 90, the third 91 to 155, the fourth 156 to 255 and is the Long Month, the fifth 256 to 315 and is the Short Month, and the sixth is 316 onward and is the Bare Month, **of no stated length, and this section changes nothing about any of them. NO CHAPTER OF THIS MANUSCRIPT WRITTEN FROM DAY 301 ONWARD MAY USE AN ORDINAL FOR A MONTH** — not *the second month*, not the third, not the fourth, not the fifth, not the sixth — **and that rule is forward-looking and it is not a verdict on the four hundred and ninety-nine chapters already written, no page of which may be re-written.** The one of the existing uses that is load-bearing is a panel at `chapters/volume-04/chapter-0152.md:79` reading *The third month is the Long Month*, a page in a finished volume, is left standing, and is named here for the seventh time so that a later phase does not mistake the rule for a repair it is still owed. *March* is a month and is never a date number. **Never** *of this month*, *of the month*, *of next month*, *of this new month*, or a form like *the last day of the month before this one*.

9. **AND THE FOUR THINGS THIS SECTION DOES NOT DO, published because a close phase that only lists what it decided is half a record. It does not amend §32, §33, §33A or §33B, and it amends none of them, and it does not amend §51, §52 to §56, or §57, and specifically it does not amend §57's fifty-row table, its twenty round figures, its twelve non-round traps, its interval anchors or its silence count. It does not move a day, a weekday, a month day, an anchor, a form, a notice, an object count or a cast member of Volume 10, all of which are on a page and not in this file. It does not decide Volume 11's line, its cast, its protagonist's rule or its pressure rotation, which are the outline phase's, and it does not edit `outline/volume-10.md` to repair the two rows its own close record measured and published. And it does not give the Bare Month a length, an end, a meaning, or a seventh form, and the only thing it says about the end of that month is that it is a thing a person does in a room, in daylight, with a date on the door.**

10. **AND WHAT IS CARRIED INTO VOLUME 11 FROM VOLUME 10 WITHOUT BEING DECIDED HERE, because this section is a calendar and not a plan: the three four-place forms and the one-place form are never joined, never conflated and never compared out loud; a sheet that came back on day 440 with one of four lines in a place it did not go out in is not traced, not investigated, not guessed at and not reported to a room by anybody, and a chapter of Volume 11 may not trace it unless tracing it is a decision to trace it; the day-154 sentence is not said, restated, paraphrased, improved on or put into a second mouth; the man of about fifty-seven is not asked where the fence is, is not told and is not defeated; the fourth of the four said no and no chapter may count the days since; the two men of about thirty-eight are two people and a chapter may not merge them; the man of about thirty-four's ninth line is not moved, dated, read aloud, taken down, thanked for or resolved; no room count is printed for anybody on a list of cardinals or on a list that includes *dozen*; nobody gets stronger; and the words the man of about thirty-four says in Chapter 0428, the name of the volume before the last, the answer spoken in Chapter 0357, the refusal spoken in Chapter 0393, the six words reproduced at `bible/power-system.md:1684` and in the Volume 08 plan, the rule said in Chapter 0438, and the words the man of about twenty-seven speaks in Chapter 0486 are in six files' worth of repository and **NO FILE, INCLUDING THIS ONE, MAY PRINT ANY OF THEM.** **THE PROHIBITION IS ON A FILE PRINTING THEM AND NOT ON THEIR BEING IN ONE PLACE, and a prohibition written as an absence is a prohibition that becomes false the moment a plan file prints a line. Name the file and hold the file.** The full text of each of those prohibitions is at `state/volume-09-close.md` §4, `outline/volume-09.md` §16, `outline/volume-10.md` §16 and `state/volume-10-close.md` §5, and none is weakened by this section. **AND THE MEASUREMENT OF THE HOLD IS AT `state/volume-10-close.md` §7.8 WITH THE FILE PATHS AND THE COUNTS AND NO SPAN. TWO OF ITS ROWS ARE PUBLISHED THERE AS DISAGREEMENTS RATHER THAN RESOLVED, and they are the refusal spoken in Chapter 0393, which two earlier records publish as standing in three files and which that run returns in two, and the panel of day 438, whose whole forty words stand on their own page and of which only a twelve-word prefix stands in a plan; the day-154 row reproduces its published figure of seventeen chapter files across Volumes 02 to 06 exactly, and its instrument lesson is that a regular expression taking the first italic span in that paragraph takes the prohibition clause and not the sentence, and a hold measured on the wrong span is not a loose hold but a measurement of something else. A writer who runs the search itself and gets a different number has been given the instrument to run it with and not only the answer, and no absence is certified anywhere in this section.**
